# Design detail: permission manifests + runtime enforcement for Agent Skills

Companion to `RESEARCH.md`. Status: proposal. Mechanics marked **verified** were run on this Mac (macOS 27, `/usr/bin/sandbox-exec`, Claude Code hooks docs as of 2026-10-11). Linux is CI-tested on ubuntu-24.04 (2026-10-12) but has not been run on a developer machine. The M4 runner and sandbox tests have not run on Linux yet (§7). Name decided 2026-10-11: `watchdeck` (free on npm, PyPI, crates.io, Homebrew and GitHub when checked).

---

## 1. What the user sees

### `watchdeck audit`

Inventory of every installed skill across harness directories, no API calls, under a second:

```
$ watchdeck audit
SKILL           SOURCE          SCRIPTS  CAPS               MANIFEST  PROFILE                                                     HASH
alpha           personal        -        -                  none      n/a                                                         f1268827
canary-basic    personal        py(1)    net cred exec env  none      default (needs net)                                         d4d097bf
canary-exec     personal        py(1)    net cred exec env  ok        exec:3 (needs net)                                          3aa9845b
canary-request  personal        py(1)    net cred exec env  ok        default (needs net) asks[network: all; env: CANARY_SECRET]  d2e13d81
delta           claude.ai sync  -        -                  none      n/a                                                         d51b660b
…
9 skills, 3 ship scripts, 2 have manifests. 3 reference network calls: canary-basic, canary-exec, canary-request.
```

### A manifest

A sidecar file at the skill root, `PERMISSIONS.yaml`. The spec allows arbitrary extra files in a skill directory, and this avoids two traps: spec `metadata` is string-to-string only, and claude.ai uploads hard-error on unknown top-level frontmatter fields.

```yaml
# PERMISSIONS.yaml  (skill: pdf)
version: 1
scripts: ["scripts/**"]          # what this manifest governs
filesystem:
  read:  ["${SKILL_DIR}", "${CWD}"]
  write: ["${CWD}", "${TMP}"]
  # deny: the built-in list (~/.ssh ~/.aws ~/.gnupg ~/.claude ~/.config/gcloud ~/.netrc ~/.docker/config.json **/.env); run also denies watchdeck's state dir
  #       always applies; write `deny:` only to extend it, and then it must still include every built-in entry
network: none                     # none | localhost | all   (domain lists: v0.2, needs a proxy)
exec: any                         # or an allow-list: ["/usr/bin/python3", "/usr/bin/qpdf"]
env: ["PATH", "HOME", "LANG", "TMPDIR"]   # everything else is scrubbed
tools: ["Read", "Write", "Bash"]  # prose half: declared only, cannot be enforced
notes: "Fills and merges PDFs locally. Never needs the network."
```

Categories map onto the BIV paper's taxonomy (network, filesystem, process execution, environment, credential) so audits and research speak the same language.

### A session (worked example)

Claude Code, default settings, the `pdf` skill has no manifest:

```
Claude → Bash: python3 ~/.claude/skills/pdf/scripts/fill_form.py in.pdf out.pdf

[hook] path is under a skill root → skill "pdf" → no PERMISSIONS.yaml → default profile
[hook] rewrite:  watchdeck run --skill pdf -c 'python3 ~/.claude/skills/pdf/scripts/fill_form.py in.pdf out.pdf'
[hook] decision: ask   reason: "pdf has no manifest; running under default profile (no network, write cwd+tmp)"

User: y                       (or auto-allow configured for this skill)

[watchdeck] compiled profile 3f9a… (macOS seatbelt) → exec
… script tries to POST to a host …
Traceback: URLError: <urlopen error [Errno 1] Operation not permitted>
[watchdeck] exit 1 under profile pdf/default: network=none, write=cwd,tmp.
            If this is a permission error, the policy caused it. Allow with:
            watchdeck allow pdf --network all     (writes ~/.config/watchdeck/overrides.yaml)
```

The hook also attaches `additionalContext` to the tool call: *"This command runs under a watchdeck profile. If it fails with a permission error, do not copy the script elsewhere or retry outside the sandbox; report the denial to the user."* That is the cheap half of the copy-to-tmp mitigation.

### `watchdeck diff` (v0.2)

Runs after a skill update and compares old and new capability sets, declared and inferred:

```
pdf  3f9a… → 7b21…
  + network: all            (scripts/upload.py: requests.post to api.example-share.com)
  + read: ~/.aws            (scripts/upload.py: boto3 session)
  ~ exec: added /usr/bin/curl
```

Hash lockfiles say *changed*. This says *how*.

### `watchdeck ui`, the activity log and the agent map (M4, 2026-10-12)

- **Dashboard.** `watchdeck ui` serves a local dashboard from the same binary on `127.0.0.1` and opens it in the browser, behind a per-launch token. Its pages:
  - **Skills:** a traffic light per skill, request against grant, per-item Grant buttons.
  - **Grants:** every override block.
  - **Enforce:** installs or uninstalls the hooks after a confirm.
  - **Flow:** the agent map.

  Started from a terminal, the server exits after 30 idle minutes; since M5.3 it can also run with no idle exit (`ui --ensure`, `ui --service`; see "Always-on dashboard").
- **Activity log.** The hook and `run` append what they decided and what happened to `${XDG_STATE_HOME:-~/.local/state}/watchdeck/activity.jsonl`. `watchdeck activity` reads it back.
- **Agent map.** The Flow page draws one Claude Code session from the activity log joined with the session's transcript: agents, models, tools, skills and the sandbox, with edges coloured by policy and denied runs cut, with the fix beside them.
- **In the session.** The plugin's hooks module draws a policy band above the prompt. `/watchdeck:open` and `/watchdeck:status` are slash commands that only the person can run.

§7 has the mechanics, the threat model and what was verified.

---

### M1 verified (2026-10-11, this Mac, macOS 27, Claude Code 2.1.296)

`watchdeck audit` against the real home: **105 skills** by default (6 personal, 23 claude.ai sync, 76 from enabled plugins), **422** with `--all` (the disabled `ecc@ecc` alone holds 302), **22 ship scripts**, 0 have manifests, zero duplicate real roots, the five `~/.agents/skills`-backed skills appear once each under their `~/.claude/skills` path. Three skills reference network calls in scripts: `ios-simulator-skill`, `obsidian-second-brain`, `watch`. `google-workspace` does not: its scripts only build request JSON and the network happens through MCP tools. Anthropic's `pdf`/`xlsx`/`docx` carry no `cred` flag. Two SKILL.md problems surfaced on day one: `obsidian-second-brain` description over the 1024-character spec limit, and `coderabbit` autofix with a list-valued `metadata` entry. Wall time 0.10 s warm, 0.26 s cold (`--all` 0.19 s). Acceptance checklist: the `--json` count equals a manual `find` over the skill roots plus the enabled-plugin count; no duplicate real roots; per-skill flags as expected (`obsidian-second-brain` net cred exec env enc fs with `+2 outside`, `google-workspace` exec enc and not net, `pdf` nothing, `xlsx`/`docx` exec env and never cred); synced skills without scripts show `-` and `n/a`; under 300 ms by default and 1 s with `--all`; golden `--json` for `testdata/home` in CI.

## 2. Manifest schema

| Field | Type | Default (no manifest) | Enforced by |
|---|---|---|---|
| `version` | int | 1 | parser |
| `scripts` | globs | `scripts/**` | hook (which files count as "this skill's code") |
| `filesystem.read` | paths | everything except `deny` | seatbelt `file-read*` / bwrap ro-binds |
| `filesystem.write` | paths | `${CWD}`, `${TMP}` | seatbelt `file-write*` / bwrap binds |
| `filesystem.deny` | paths | built-in sensitive list | seatbelt deny, later rules win |
| `network` | `none\|localhost\|all` | `none` | seatbelt `network*` / bwrap `--unshare-net` |
| `exec` | `any` or list | `any` | seatbelt `process-exec` |
| `env` | list | `PATH HOME LANG TMPDIR` | `env -i` in the wrapper |
| `tools` | list | unset | not enforced; audit/infer compare only |

**Request, grant, clip (decided 2026-10-11).** The skill's own `PERMISSIONS.yaml` is a *request*. The *grant* is the default profile, widened only by the project override (`.watchdeck.yaml`) and then the user override (`~/.config/watchdeck/overrides.yaml`). `run` executes under `clip(request, grant)`, the field-by-field minimum, and never fails because a skill asked for more: the ungranted items (for example `network: all`, `write: ~/Library/Caches/pip`) are named in the hook's `"ask"` reason and in the run trailer together with the exact `watchdeck allow <skill> …` command that would grant them. Exit 125 means a policy error before the script runs. The cases are a malformed manifest, a manifest that tries to remove a built-in deny, an unknown skill, a nested `watchdeck run`, and a compiled profile the OS rejects (`internal/runner/run.go`). Asking for more than the grant is never one of them. That is the entitlement model: the author asks, the owner grants.

**The project override is untrusted.** `.watchdeck.yaml` arrives with a cloned repository, so it cannot be allowed to grant what the user has not: it may add read/write paths only under `${CWD}` or `${TMP}` and raise network to at most `localhost`. `network: all`, cache directories, extra environment variables, `auto_allow`, and paths outside the project are ignored and reported (`audit` and the run trailer say "project override ignored: …"). Only the user's own `~/.config/watchdeck/overrides.yaml` grants beyond the project boundary. The boundary is checked twice: lexically when the override is applied, and again on real paths when `run` resolves the profile, so a symlink inside the repository (`build -> ~/Library/LaunchAgents`) cannot carry a project grant outside the project. (Flagged by automated review 2026-10-11; a future `watchdeck trust <project>` could relax this per project by content hash.)

---

## 3. Enforcement mechanics

### Skill root discovery

`~/.claude/skills/**`, `.claude/skills/**` (cwd and parents up to the repo root), enabled plugins' `skills/` directories (from `~/.claude/plugins`), `~/.agents/skills`, and the `${CLAUDE_SKILL_DIR}` value if present in the hook input. Cached per session.

### The hook, step by step

The same `watchdeck hook` command is registered three times, via the plugin's `hooks.json` or `watchdeck enforce install` (which edits settings):

- `PreToolUse` with matcher `Bash`: the path below.
- `PreToolUse` with matcher `Skill`: records which skills the model invoked and never answers.
- `PermissionDenied` with matcher `*` (widened from `Bash|Skill` on 2026-10-19): records auto-mode denials for every tool.

The last two only write to the activity log (§7). Every Bash call pays the Bash path, so its budget is a few milliseconds:

1. Read the hook JSON from stdin: `tool_input.command`, `cwd`, `session_id`, and since M4 also `tool_use_id`, `transcript_path`, `permission_mode` and, inside a subagent, `agent_id`/`agent_type` (fields verified 2026-10-12, §7).
2. Fast reject: if the command contains no token that resolves under a known skill root and no interpreter-reads-stdin pattern, exit 0 (no opinion, normal flow). This is the 99% path.
3. Parse the command with a real shell-word parser (handles `cd x && …`, `;`, `|`, quoting, `env VAR=… cmd`). Resolve each path-like argv token against skill roots. Match → skill identity.
4. Load the effective manifest (override chain above). Compile to a profile, cached by manifest hash.
5. Return `hookSpecificOutput` with `updatedInput.command` rewritten to `watchdeck run --skill <name> -c '<original>'` (the whole original command, quoted, so chains and pipes are sandboxed as a unit), `permissionDecision` `"ask"` by default (`"allow"` if the user opted that skill or all skills into auto-allow), a one-line `permissionDecisionReason`, and the `additionalContext` note. Claude Code evaluates permission rules against the rewritten command. The answer is written to stdout first; only then does the hook append a `decision` record to the activity log, so a slow disk never delays Claude Code (`TestStdoutBeforeAppend`). A command the hook has no opinion on opens no file (`TestNoOpinionWritesNothing`).
6. If parsing confidence is low (unbalanced quotes, `eval`, here-docs that embed a skill path), return `"ask"` with the reason "could not determine the sandbox boundary" rather than silently allowing.

Since M4 (2026-10-12) the `Skill` matcher records which skills the model invoked, keyed by `session_id`, but the Bash decision does not use that record yet. The session-state heuristic is still planned for v0.2: once a skill is active, an interpreter reading stdin, or a fresh file run from a temp dir, would be treated as `"ask"`. A skill the person types does not pass through `PreToolUse`, so a `UserPromptExpansion` hook would still be needed for that case. Today the hook only acts on commands that reference a skill path; a command that feeds a skill script to an interpreter through stdin or a here-doc is `"ask"` with low confidence, and a command that references no skill path gets no opinion.

### Why a wrapper binary instead of inlining `sandbox-exec` (verified)

Nesting is refused under any restrictive outer profile. Verified 2026-10-11 (`internal/sandbox` TestNestedApplyFails and the probe in `spikes/seatbelt/README.md`): an inner `sandbox-exec` applies only when the outer profile is a bare `(allow default)`; any deny rule in the outer profile (network, file, mach-lookup, sysctl, process-exec) makes the inner apply fail with exit code 71 and `sandbox-exec: sandbox_apply: Operation not permitted`. Claude Code's Bash sandbox always carries denies, so inside it a second `sandbox-exec` fails. The precise mechanism, mapped in spike 2: a sandboxed process may re-apply only a profile identical to the one in force (whitespace and duplicate rules ignored); anything stricter or looser is refused. A real per-skill profile is never identical to the harness's, so the practical rule stands. So the rewrite targets `watchdeck run …`, and `watchdeck enforce install` adds `"watchdeck run *"` to `sandbox.excludedCommands`. Claude Code then runs that one command outside its session sandbox and `watchdeck run` applies the per-skill profile itself. The wrapper never applies anything looser than the default profile, and when a manifest asks for `network: all` inside a sandboxed session the hook downgrades to `"ask"` and says why. If `watchdeck run` detects it is already inside a sandbox (apply fails), it exits non-zero with a clear message instead of running unsandboxed.

### Profile compilation, macOS (verified)

The shape that works in practice is "allow default, then deny," not "deny default, then allow." A deny-default Seatbelt profile breaks Python and most interpreters (dyld, mach lookups). This profile ran a Python script that wrote to the working directory, and was refused on home writes, `~/.ssh` reads, and the network:

```scheme
(version 1)
(allow default)
(deny network*)
(deny file-read* (subpath "/Users/you/.ssh") (subpath "/Users/you/.aws") (subpath "/Users/you/.claude"))
(deny file-write* (subpath "/Users/you"))
(allow file-write* (subpath "/private/tmp") (subpath "/Users/you/project"))
```

Later rules win, so a cwd allow after a home deny works (verified). Child processes inherit the profile. `network: localhost` compiles to `(deny network*) (allow network* (local ip "localhost:*")) (allow network-outbound (remote ip "localhost:*"))`. `exec` allow-lists compile to `(deny process-exec*) (allow process-exec (literal …))` and are the one place deny-default is used, so expect to list the interpreter and its helpers.

### Profile compilation, Linux (implemented 2026-10-12; CI green on ubuntu-24.04 the same day, not yet run on a developer machine)

`profile.BwrapArgs` compiles the same `Resolved` policy to a bubblewrap argument vector; `internal/sandbox/exec_linux.go` runs it. Mount order is the whole security argument, because bwrap applies mounts in argument order:

```
bwrap --ro-bind / / --dev /dev --proc /proc \
      --bind "$CWD" "$CWD" --bind "$TMP" "$TMP" [--bind <extra> <extra>] \
      --tmpfs ~/.ssh --tmpfs ~/.aws --tmpfs ~/.gnupg --tmpfs ~/.claude --tmpfs ~/.config/gcloud … \
      --tmpfs /run/user/<uid> [--tmpfs <ssh-agent socket dir>] \
      --ro-bind /dev/null ~/.netrc [--ro-bind /dev/null /run/docker.sock ...]   # deny files and sockets \
      --ro-bind <skill dir> <skill dir> \
      --unshare-all [--share-net]           # net shared only for network=all
      --clearenv --die-with-parent --new-session [--setenv K V ...] -- python3 /path/scripts/x.py args
```

Writable binds come first and deny `tmpfs` mounts after them, so a deny always shadows a bind; deny paths that exist as files or sockets (`~/.netrc`, `~/.docker/config.json`, the Docker socket) are hidden by binding `/dev/null` over them, because a tmpfs needs a directory mount point (the first CI run on 2026-10-12 failed with `--tmpfs /run/docker.sock: Not a directory` on the GitHub runner); the skill's own directory is re-bound read-only after the `~/.claude` deny; a deny path that is an ancestor of a writable path hides it (warned). Limits that Seatbelt handles and bwrap cannot, each printed as a warning by `run`: exec allow-lists (not enforced), `network: localhost` (treated as `none`: an unprivileged network namespace has no usable loopback), and `**` deny patterns such as `**/.env` (only literal paths can be hidden). Denials look different from Seatbelt's EPERM: a write outside the binds fails with EROFS, a hidden path reads as missing (ENOENT), and a connect in the unshared namespace fails with ENETUNREACH; the canary probe treats all of these as denied. The variables the runner keeps are re-added with `--setenv` after `--clearenv` (the third CI run, 2026-10-12, showed the child with an empty environment). CI on `ubuntu-24.04` installs bubblewrap, relaxes `kernel.apparmor_restrict_unprivileged_userns`, and runs the basic canary under it. The first four CI runs on 2026-10-12 each found a Linux-only defect (tmpfs over a socket, duplicate mounts via `/var/run`, an empty child environment, probe errnos); the fifth was green (run 38280365417). It has not yet run on a developer's Linux machine. Windows: `audit`, `validate` and `init` only; `run` exits 3.

### Env scrubbing (verified, with a footnote)

The wrapper execs through `env -i` with only the manifest's `env` list. On this Mac, `/usr/bin/python3` is Xcode's shim and re-injects `CPATH`, `SDKROOT`, `LIBRARY_PATH` and similar; those are added by the shim, not leaked from the parent. Secrets in the parent environment (API keys exported in the shell) do not reach the script.

### Network is binary in v0.1

Seatbelt filters by IP and port, not hostname. Domain allow-lists need a local proxy, which is how Claude Code's sandbox does it (`httpProxyPort`). v0.1 ships `none | localhost | all`. v0.2 can add a proxy mode or hand domain lists to Claude Code's per-command allowed-domains mechanism.

### Approval policy and the `allowed-tools` regression

Rewriting changes the command text, so a skill that pre-approved its script with `allowed-tools: Bash(${CLAUDE_SKILL_DIR}/scripts/render.sh *)` will no longer match that rule after the rewrite and will prompt once per run. Two mitigations, both explicit choices: (1) default to `"ask"` showing the sandboxed command, which is one prompt but a legible one; (2) `watchdeck allow <skill>` or a global `auto-allow: true` returns `"allow"`, which is Claude Code's own philosophy for sandboxed commands ("runs without asking because the OS enforces the boundary"). Document the regression; do not hide it.

### The prose half

Instructions are not sandboxable. The manifest's `tools` field is a declaration that `audit` and `infer` compare against what the SKILL.md text asks the agent to do (for example, prose that says "use WebFetch" when `tools` omits it). That is a flag, not a block.

---

### M2 verified (2026-10-11, this Mac)

Implemented and tested under the real sandbox (`internal/runner` canary tests, `internal/hook` tests, `internal/sandbox` nesting test):

- **Default profile, no manifest:** project write ALLOWED; home write, `~/.ssh/id_fake` read, outbound connect (192.0.2.1:80) and a connect to 127.0.0.1 all DENIED with EPERM; subprocess ALLOWED; `CANARY_SECRET` absent from the child environment; `PATH` kept; `WATCHDECK_PROFILE` set.
- **Request, grant, clip:** a skill requesting `network: all` runs clipped to `none`, the trailer names `requested but not granted: <skill>: network: all` and the `watchdeck allow` command; after a user override the same probe reaches the network. A skill requesting `localhost` is clipped until granted; granted, 127.0.0.1 is reachable and 192.0.2.1 is not.
- **Child inheritance:** a bash script that execs python inherits the profile.
- **Exec allow-list:** works, but on macOS Apple's `python3` is a chain of three binaries (`/usr/bin/python3` shim → `Xcode.app/.../usr/bin/python3` launcher → `Python.app/Contents/MacOS/Python`), all of which must be listed. `exec: any` stays the default; allow-lists are for people who know their toolchain. Interactive dogfood on 2026-10-12 found the other chain: on a machine where `python3` on PATH is a pyenv shim (a bash script with `#!/usr/bin/env bash`), `canary-exec` dies with "bad interpreter", exit 126, and `allow` has no exec flag to widen with; the trailer now names the manifest's exec list and the shim case, and `allow --exec` is on the v0.2 line.
- **Fail closed:** malformed manifest → exit 125 before anything runs; nested `WATCHDECK_PROFILE` → 125; apply failure → 126 with the `excludedCommands` fix named; execvp failure of the target is not mistaken for an apply failure.
- **Hook:** fast reject 4.5 ms median end to end (process spawn included; measured 2026-10-11, before M4 linked the dashboard into the binary; the M4 figure is 6.7 ms, §7), full match plus rewrite 5.5 ms, on an index of 105 skills with 114 needles. `git status`, `python3 -c`, `cat SKILL.md`, `grep -r … references/` get no opinion. A real invocation of `obsidian-second-brain/scripts/research/perplexity.py` is rewritten to `watchdeck run --skill obsidian-second-brain -c '…'` with decision `ask`.
- **Rewrite form:** the whole original command is wrapped as `-c '<quoted original>'` so `cd … &&`, pipes and quoting are sandboxed as a unit; `/bin/bash -c` is the only process added. On an exec allow-list `/bin/bash` is admitted automatically for this form (bash can then run only builtins and the listed binaries).
- **Recursion guard:** only a program that is exactly one `watchdeck run …` call passes; `watchdeck run … && other` is `ask`.
- **Project override untrusted:** `.watchdeck.yaml` may add paths only under `${CWD}`/`${TMP}` (re-checked on real paths, symlinks followed) and raise network to `localhost`; `auto_allow`, `caches`, `env`, `network: all` from it are ignored and reported.
- **Exit codes:** child's code passes through (signals → 128+n); 125 policy error before the script runs (malformed manifest, removed built-in deny, unknown skill, nested run, profile rejected by the OS); 126 sandbox not applied; 3 usage/unsupported platform.
- **End to end (2026-10-12):** `scripts/e2e.sh` drives `claude -p` with a temporary `--settings` file (exec-form hook, `permissions.allow: ["Bash(watchdeck run *)"]`), a temporary project whose `.claude/skills` holds `canary-basic`, and temporary XDG dirs with `auto_allow: true`. The hook log shows `allow` with the rewrite to `watchdeck run --skill canary-basic -c '…'` and the tool result is `net: DENIED(1)`. The stream-json transcript shows the model's original command, not the rewrite. At the time the evidence was the hook's own debug log, an environment variable M4 removed; the activity log replaces it, and `e2e.sh` now asserts a `decision` record and a joined `run` record (§7).
- **Interactive dogfood (2026-10-12):** `scripts/dogfood.sh` (temporary settings from `enforce install --file`, three canaries in a temp project, no overrides, so every run asks). Observed in the session, from the hook log: four decisions, all `ask` with the rewrite, for `cat <script>` (documented above), for a chained `S=…; python3 $S net; python3 $S write-cwd; python3 $S read-secret` (one rewrite, one prompt; net DENIED(1), write-cwd ALLOWED), for `probe.py write-home`, and for `cd <skill dir> && cat scripts/probe.py | head -50 && python3 scripts/probe.py net` under `canary-request` (the reason named the ungranted request; net DENIED(1)). Paths arrived as literals; no `${CLAUDE_SKILL_DIR}` reached the hook. Two defects: `read-secret` printed DENIED(2) because `~/.ssh/id_fake` does not exist on a real machine and the probe treated ENOENT as a denial; measured in a throwaway HOME under `/var/tmp`, Seatbelt returns ENOENT for a missing file inside a denied directory, EPERM for a present one and EPERM on `stat` of the directory, so the probe now stats the directory and prints MISSING only when nothing is proven (the darwin test keeps DENIED(1) with the file present). Re-run on the same machine it prints DENIED(1): `~/.ssh` exists there and the sandbox refuses the stat, so the original denial was real and only the label was wrong. The second is the pyenv shim case under "Exec allow-list" below. Verified from the shell afterwards, not in the session: with `allow canary-request --network all` written, the hook reason drops the network item and the probe's connection leaves the sandbox (printed ALLOWED, formerly LEAKED); `cp <script> /tmp/probe.py && python3 /tmp/probe.py net` is `ask` and rewritten under `canary-basic` (the copy runs sandboxed), and a bare `python3 /tmp/probe.py net` gets no opinion, the documented copy-then-run limit. Prompts 3 and 4 of the session were typed into a second `claude` started without `--settings`, which has no hook; those results prove nothing and the script now says so.
- **Auto-allow is narrow:** `allow` is returned only when the command is solely the skill invocation (optional leading `cd`, then one call whose argv[0] is an interpreter or the skill's own script). `python3 skill/x.py; rm -rf …`, pipes, `cp … && python3 /tmp/x.py` and `cat skill/x.py` all stay `ask` even for an auto-allowed skill (automated review finding, 2026-10-12).
- **Two deliberate softnesses:** the skill directory's read allow follows the `**/.env` deny, so a `.env` inside the skill's own directory is readable (the skill shipped it); and running from the home directory compiles (only the protected paths stay denied), which `run` warns about.
- **Temp dir:** the profile allows the real `DARWIN_USER_TEMP_DIR` (`/private/var/folders/…/T`) and `/private/tmp`; the Xcode shim writes `xcrun_db-*` there regardless of `TMPDIR`.

## 4. Hard parts and known evasions

- **Command recognition** is the make-or-break. Cases the parser corpus must cover: `python3 path`, `./scripts/x.sh`, `bash scripts/x.sh`, `uv run scripts/x.py`, `node scripts/x.js`, `cd skilldir && python3 scripts/x.py`, `python3 "$HOME/.claude/skills/pdf/scripts/x.py"`, pipes and `;` chains, `xargs`, `find -exec`, here-docs, `${CLAUDE_SKILL_DIR}` already expanded by Claude Code before the model sees it.
- **Copy-then-run.** Prose that says "copy `scripts/x.py` to `/tmp` and run it there" or `cat scripts/x.py | python3` yields a command that references no skill root. Partial mitigations: the `additionalContext` instruction (v0.1), `"ask"` on commands that feed a skill script to an interpreter via stdin or a here-doc (v0.1), the session-state heuristic for temp-dir execution after a skill is active (v0.2), `audit` flagging relocation instructions in the prose (v0.1, scan rule `instr-relocate-script`), and `infer` (v0.3). State the boundary in the README: this sandboxes script execution; it does not stop a model from being talked into relocating code. OS app sandboxes share that limit.
- **Sandboxed sessions without the exclusion.** If a user installs the hook but not the `excludedCommands` entry, `watchdeck run` fails inside Claude Code's sandbox. The wrapper must fail closed with a message naming the fix, never fall through to unsandboxed execution.
- **Claude Code's own config and a moved `$HOME`.** `~/.claude` is a built-in deny, but `~/.claude.json` (MCP server tokens) and its backups are sibling files, `CLAUDE_CONFIG_DIR` can move the directory elsewhere, and `HOME=/x watchdeck run …` moved every home-relative deny. Since 2026-10-14 `run` denies the siblings by prefix, the relocated directory, and the built-in denies under the account home from the user database, to every sandboxed run outside the manifest (§7, "Claude Code's config is denied to every run"; `TestRunDeniesClaudeConfig`, `TestRunClaudeConfigNotReallowed`).
- **Seatbelt is deprecated-but-present.** It has been "deprecated" for years and is still what Claude Code uses. Keep profiles minimal and test on each macOS release.
- **Interpreters that need more.** Some scripts legitimately write to `~/.cache` or `~/Library/Caches`. Add those to the default write list rather than widening home; collect the list from the local skill corpus.
- **Latency.** Every Bash call pays the fast-reject path. Measured 2026-10-12 over three runs of `scripts/measure-hook.sh` (§7), its median per call was 4.7 ms before M4 and 6.7 ms (p95 8.0 ms) once the dashboard was linked into the same binary, then 5.9 ms (p95 7.3 ms) on 2026-10-13 once every linked package was made init-free. Node adds ~40 ms per call and Bun ~10 ms; both would be felt on every command.

---

## 5. Testing

- **Parser corpus:** a table of several hundred command strings with expected (skill, script, confidence), including adversarial quoting. Property tests for quoting round-trips.
- **Canary skills:** a fixture skill whose scripts attempt each capability (home write, `~/.ssh` read, outbound HTTPS, localhost, subprocess, env read). Assert each is denied or allowed exactly per manifest, on macOS and Linux CI runners.
- **End-to-end:** drive `claude -p` with the hook installed against the fixture skill and assert the rewrite and the `"ask"`/`"allow"` decisions from the transcript JSONL.
- **Local corpus:** run `audit` and the default profile against the 41 skills on this machine; fix every false positive before Show HN.
- **Dashboard (M4):** the server is tested over a real loopback listener on `127.0.0.1:0`, never a browser. The guard matrix (401, 403, 405, 413, 415), the token handling, the enforce nonce and idle exit each have a named test (§7). The static pages are checked for CSP compliance and local-only assets.
- **Transcripts (M4):** fixtures come only from a throwaway session captured by `scripts/fixture-session.sh`, scrubbed by `scripts/scrub-transcript.py` and reviewed by hand. They are never taken from a developer's own `~/.claude/projects`.
- **In-session mod (M4):** `claude plugin validate plugin --strict` and `claude plugin test plugin`; process and file access are answered by the tests' own hooks, so the mod never touches the real home.

---

## 6. Stack, distribution and roadmap

- **Binary:** Go, one static binary, still three dependencies (`mvdan.cc/sh/v3`, `github.com/goccy/go-yaml`, `github.com/bmatcuk/doublestar/v4`). The dashboard uses only the standard library, with its pages and fonts embedded: no JavaScript build step, no CDN. The hook runs on every Bash call, so startup time is a feature, and every package the binary links is init-free (`cmd/watchdeck/initfree_test.go`). Owner decision 2026-10-12: keep one binary. The measurement history is in sections 7 to 12.
- **Distribution:** GitHub releases built by goreleaser from the tag: darwin and linux, arm64 and amd64; archives ship `LICENSE`, `NOTICE`, `README.md` and this file. `-trimpath`, an empty build id and commit-time file times make the binaries reproducible (the ci.yml `repro-a` and `repro-b` jobs build the snapshot on two runners and `repro-compare` diffs the four sha256s; archive-level reproducibility is designed, not tested). One SPDX SBOM per archive by syft; `checksums.txt` signed keyless by cosign as `checksums.txt.sigstore.json`; a build-provenance attestation for every file in `checksums.txt`. Signing and attesting run only from the public repository: `release.yml` signs and attests only when `github.event.repository.visibility == 'public'`, and any other value, including an absent one, yields an unsigned draft (fail-closed; `TestReleaseSignAndAttestGatedOnPublic`). The pipeline is designed, not tested: no tag exists in this repository, and the first signed draft needs the public repository (section 12). A Homebrew tap and an npm wrapper come after the first release. The Claude Code plugin under `plugin/` registers the hooks and the in-session mod, with two user-only slash commands (`/watchdeck:open`, `/watchdeck:status`); `plugin.json` stays 0.1.0 until the owner tags. License: Apache-2.0 (chosen 2026-10-12).
- **Config:** `~/.config/watchdeck/overrides.yaml` (user, trusted), `.watchdeck.yaml` (project, untrusted), `PERMISSIONS.yaml` (skill, a request).
- **Roadmap:** v0.1 is manifest, `audit`, hook, `run`, `allow`, `enforce` (macOS verified, Linux CI-tested), the dashboard, the activity log, the agent map and the mod (sections 7 to 11). Still out of scope:
  - live approval from the UI (the ask prompt is Claude Code's own);
  - writing a manifest into a plugin's skill directory from the UI;
  - redacting transcript content.

  v0.2: `diff` on update, proxy-based domain allow-lists, `localhost` on Linux, manifests contributed for the most-installed public skills, session-state heuristics for copy-then-run, `allow --exec` for widening a skill's exec list on machines with version-manager shims. v0.3: `infer` (Python AST, JS/TS, shell; optional LLM pass) and a declared-versus-actual report.

---

## 7. M4: dashboard, activity log, agent map and the in-session mod (2026-10-12)

Built on 2026-10-12 (some sessions ran into 2026-10-13 UTC). Parallel workers built each package from a written contract, and a separate verifier checked it before it merged. Not released: `plugin.json` and `marketplace.json` stay at 0.1.0 until the owner re-tags. This section says what each part does, what stops it from being misused, and which test shows it. "Not verified yet" at the end lists what has not been run.

### Paths

`internal/paths` resolves every location once, as a pure function of home and environment. `watchdeck paths [--json]` prints the result so scripts and the in-session mod never re-derive the XDG rules:

- `configDir` is `${XDG_CONFIG_HOME:-~/.config}/watchdeck` and holds `overrides.yaml`.
- `cacheBase` is `${XDG_CACHE_HOME:-~/.cache}`.
- `stateDir` is `${XDG_STATE_HOME:-~/.local/state}/watchdeck`.
- `activity` is `stateDir/activity.jsonl`. `WATCHDECK_ACTIVITY=<path>` overrides it; `WATCHDECK_ACTIVITY=off` empties it.
- `claudeDir` is `${CLAUDE_CONFIG_DIR:-~/.claude}`.
- `projectRoot` is the git root, or the current directory when there is none.

A relative `XDG_CONFIG_HOME`, `XDG_CACHE_HOME` or `XDG_STATE_HOME` is ignored and the default is used, as the XDG Base Directory spec requires (2026-10-13; before that, a relative value put state under the current directory). Test: `TestRelativeXDGIgnored`.

`enforce` now honours `CLAUDE_CONFIG_DIR` for the user settings file. Skill discovery still reads `~/.claude/skills` and `~/.claude/plugins` whatever `CLAUDE_CONFIG_DIR` says; that is a follow-up. Tests: `TestResolveDefaults`, `TestResolveXDG`, `TestActivityOverride`, `TestProjectRootStopsAtHome`, `TestPathsJSON`.

### Three hook registrations, and what the hook receives

`enforce install` and the plugin's `hooks.json` register one command, `watchdeck hook`, three times:

- `PreToolUse` on `Bash`: the sandbox path described in §3.
- `PreToolUse` on `Skill`: writes a `skill_invoked` record and never answers, so it cannot block a skill.
- `PermissionDenied` on every tool (matcher `*`, widened from `Bash|Skill` on 2026-10-19): writes a `denied` record. `enforce install` migrates an earlier `Bash|Skill` group of ours in place, never adding a second one (`TestDeniedMatcherMigrates`). A `PermissionRequest` event is reserved: the hook prints and writes nothing for it (`TestPermissionRequestSilent`).

`uninstall` restores the file byte for byte, and an existing backup is never overwritten. A v0.1 install (the Bash group and the exclusion) is upgraded: install adds the missing groups, every existing group keeps its place and bytes, and uninstall returns the file to its state before any install (`TestUpgradeFromV01`, fixture `internal/settings/testdata/v01-installed.json`). Tests: `TestInstallAppendsAndPreserves`, `TestUpgradeFromV01`, `TestUninstallRestoresBytes`, `TestInspectReportsEveryHook`, `TestPluginHooksJSONShape`, `TestBackupsInTheSameSecondDoNotCollide`.

Since M5.2 (2026-10-13) there are six: the three above plus `SessionStart`, `SessionEnd` and `UserPromptSubmit`, registered without a matcher; see "Session lifecycle (M5.2)" at the end of this section.

`enforce --project` now writes `<git root>/.claude/settings.json`, or the current directory's `.claude/settings.json` when no `.git` is found. That is the same file the dashboard's Enforce page names. Before M4, `--project` was always relative to the current directory.

`PermissionDenied` fires only when **auto mode** denies a tool call (Claude Code hooks docs, read 2026-10-12). A person who answers "no" to an ask prompt in default mode does not fire it, so `denied` records are auto-mode denials only. Since 2026-10-19 the hook records a `denied` record for every tool (`TestPermissionDeniedAllTools`). A Skill call keeps its input, and a Bash command that resolves to a skill or is a single `watchdeck run` keeps its command and skills. Any other Bash command keeps its program name only (`argv0`, never the command text; `TestDeniedBashKeepsArgv0Only`). A file tool keeps a normalised `target` (home as `~`), WebFetch the host, an MCP call its tool name. The `reason` is stored verbatim and never parsed. The `Skill` matcher also has a gap: it sees only Skill calls the model makes, because a `/skill` the person types does not pass through `PreToolUse`.

These facts were verified 2026-10-12 on Claude Code 2.1.296, from one scrubbed `claude -p` capture in `internal/transcript/testdata/e2e-canary/`:

- **Hook stdin** carries `cwd`, `effort`, `hook_event_name`, `permission_mode`, `prompt_id`, `session_id`, `tool_input`, `tool_name`, `tool_use_id` and `transcript_path`. Inside a subagent it also carries `agent_id` and `agent_type`. `transcript_path` names the main session's file even for a subagent's call. Evidence: `testdata/e2e-canary/hook-stdin.jsonl`, which holds both stdin objects as the hook received them.
- **The hook's `tool_use_id` equals the transcript's `tool_use.id`**, both for the main agent's Bash call and for the subagent's. The subagent's file is `dirname(transcript_path)/<session_id>/subagents/agent-<agent_id>.jsonl`. Evidence: `TestHookStdinIdsMatchTranscript`.
- **The transcript stores the model's original command**, not the rewrite. The rewrite and the decision appear once, in an `attachment` row of type `hook_success` with `hookName "PreToolUse:Bash"`, a `toolUseID`, and the hook's JSON in `stdout`. Evidence: `TestLoadFixtureA`, `TestHookSuccessAttachmentParsed`, `TestOnlyHookSuccessAttachmentsRetained`.
- **Transcript layout:**
  - The main session is `<projects>/<escaped cwd>/<session id>.jsonl`, where `<projects>` is `~/.claude/projects` or `$CLAUDE_CONFIG_DIR/projects`.
  - Each subagent is `<session id>/subagents/agent-<id>.jsonl`, beside an optional `agent-<id>.meta.json` (`agentType`, `description`, and the `toolUseId` of the Agent call that spawned it).
  - Subagent rows carry `isSidechain: true` and `agentId`.
  - `message.model` is on assistant rows; `resolvedModel` is on the Agent call's result.
  - Tool-result rows carry `permissionDecision {decision, source, reasonType}`.

  Evidence: the fixture A files, `TestLoadFixtureA`, and `TestFixtureBAgentsAndSpawnLinks` (fixture B is synthetic).
- **Escaping rule:** the project directory name is the cwd with every character that is not an ASCII letter or digit replaced by `-`. The replacement is one `-` per UTF-16 code unit, so an emoji gives two. A name over 200 characters is cut to 200, plus `-` and a 6-character hash that cannot be recomputed, so `Discover` lists `projects/` once and matches by prefix. Only `transcript.EscapeProjectDir` builds the name. Evidence: `TestEscapeProjectDir`, `TestDiscoverLongCwd`.
- **`CLAUDE_CONFIG_DIR`** moves `projects/`, `sessions/` and `.claude.json`, but credentials stay where they were: the relocated run said "Not logged in". So `scripts/fixture-session.sh` runs without `CLAUDE_CONFIG_DIR` by default and copies exactly one session's files.
- **No `Skill` tool_use appeared in the capture.** A skill the person types shows up as a `user` row with `<command-name>` tags, followed by an `isMeta` user row.

### The activity log (schema v1)

The hook writes one record per decision it makes, and `run` writes one per outcome. Both append JSONL to the `activity` path above:

- The file has mode 0600, in a 0700 directory.
- Above 8 MiB the file is renamed to `activity.jsonl.1`, replacing any older `.1`.
- The log is on by default. `WATCHDECK_ACTIVITY=off` turns it off; `WATCHDECK_ACTIVITY=<path>` moves it.
- It replaces the hook's earlier debug-log variable, which no longer exists.

`watchdeck activity [--json] [--summary] [--session ID] [--after N] [--tail N] [--limit N] [--since RFC3339] [--kinds …]` reads the log and joins records the same way the dashboard does. `--json` prints the body of `GET /api/activity`. Without `--json`, control characters are escaped, so a hostile command cannot write terminal escapes (`TestActivityTextEscapesControlCharacters`).

Every record has `v: 1`, `kind` and `ts` (RFC 3339, UTC). The other fields depend on the kind:

| Kind | Written by | Fields |
|---|---|---|
| `decision` | hook on Bash, only when it answers | `session_id`, `tool_use_id`, `transcript_path`, `agent_id`/`agent_type` (subagent), `permission_mode`, `cwd`, `skills` (sorted), `command` (the original `tool_input.command`), `decision` (`ask`/`allow`), `reason` (the permission reason, verbatim), `rewritten`, `confidence` (`high`/`low`), `ungranted[]` (`"<skill>: network: all"`), `ignored[]` |
| `run` | `run`, for every outcome including usage and policy errors | the ids above when correlated, `cwd`, `skills`, `command` (the `-c` payload), `exit`, `outcome` (`ok`, `failed`, `signaled`, `policy_error`, `no_sandbox`, `usage`), `duration_ms`, `profile_hash`, `backend` (`seatbelt`/`bwrap`), `effective` (the policy it ran under, structured), `warnings`, `hints[]`, `error`, `joined_by` |
| `skill_invoked` | hook, `PreToolUse` on `Skill` | the ids, `cwd`, `tool_name`, `skill`, `tool_input` (the whole Skill input, capped at 2 KiB) |
| `denied` | hook, `PermissionDenied` (auto mode) | the ids, `cwd`, `tool_name` (any tool), `skills`, `command` (Bash on a skill only; otherwise `argv0`), `target` (file path, host or MCP tool name), `reason` |

"Denied" is never stored as an outcome; readers derive it from hints and records.

A **hint** is one output line that looked like a sandbox denial. Its fields:

- `kind`: `network`, `write`, `read`, `exec` or `unknown`;
- `errno`;
- `stream`: `stdout` or `stderr`;
- `label`: the canary probe's label (`net`, `write-home`, …) when the line was its `<label>: DENIED(n)` marker;
- `text`: one sentence for people;
- `line`: the matched line, at most 200 bytes;
- `heuristic`: always `true`.

`run` keeps a 64 KiB tail of each stream, scans both, and keeps at most 8 hints, canary markers first. stdout is scanned strictly: only a canary marker or a line carrying an OS error message (`Operation not permitted`, `Read-only file system`, …) counts, never a comment line or a bare `EPERM`/`PermissionError` token, because a command may print source code (the 2026-10-13 dogfood ran `cat probe.py && python3 probe.py net` and the loose rule turned the probe's comments into seven false hints; `TestStdoutSourceListingIsNotAHint`, `TestCanaryMarkerSurvivesCap`). So a quiet canary that prints `net: DENIED(1)` and exits 0 produces `outcome: ok` with one stdout hint (`TestRunRecordsOutcome`). A loud failure with a traceback produces `failed` with a stderr hint (`TestRunRecordsHintOnLoudFailure`). Tests: `TestHintsTable`, `TestHintTexts`, `TestHintsCapAndTrim`, `TestTailWriterKeepsTail`, `TestRunRecordsPolicyError`.

**Privacy statement.** The log stores:

- the shell command of each decision and run;
- the session, tool-use, agent and transcript identifiers;
- the permission reason;
- the Skill tool's input when the model invokes a skill: name and arguments, which can contain prompt text, capped at 2 KiB;
- run outcomes and the policy each run ran under;
- from the script's output, only the lines that matched a denial pattern: at most 200 bytes each, at most 8 per run.

It stores nothing else from stdout or stderr, no transcript content, and never the dashboard token. The file is mode 0600 under the user's state directory, and nothing is sent anywhere. `WATCHDECK_ACTIVITY=off` disables the log.

On the no-opinion path the hook does not open the file, so a command it has no opinion on is not recorded (`TestNoOpinionWritesNothing`, `TestLogOffNeverOpens`). Since 2026-10-19 an auto-mode denial of any command is recorded; for a Bash command that touches no skill the record keeps the program name only, and every copy of the command inside the denial reason is replaced by that name and " …" (`TestDeniedBashKeepsArgv0Only`). When the hook does answer, it writes the answer to stdout **before** appending, so a slow or read-only disk never delays Claude Code (`TestStdoutBeforeAppend`). Tests: `TestAppendCreatesFile0600`, `TestAppendOffIsNoop`, `TestDecisionRecorded`, `TestSkillInvokedRecorded`.

**Joining a run to its decision.** The rewrite carries no id; it stays `watchdeck run --skill <n> [--skill <n>] -c '<original>'`. Instead, `run` correlates at write time. Before appending, it reads the last 256 KiB of the log. It also reads the tail of `activity.jsonl.1` when the current file is shorter than that, because a decision may have just rotated.

From that tail, `run` takes the **newest unclaimed** `decision` that meets every condition:

- it has a rewrite;
- its sorted `skills` are equal to the run's;
- its `command` (the original text) equals the run's `-c` payload;
- its `cwd` is equal: compared cleaned, then through `EvalSymlinks`, since `/var/tmp` and `/private/var/tmp` are one directory on macOS;
- its timestamp is at most 10 minutes before the run's.

The newest wins, not the oldest, so that when a declined ask is followed by the same command approved, the run attaches to the approval. `run` copies `session_id`, `tool_use_id`, `transcript_path`, `agent_id`, `agent_type` and `permission_mode` into its record and sets `joined_by: "write"`.

On the reading side, `Join` trusts those ids and applies the same rule to runs that have none, marking them `joined_by: "heuristic"`. When two runs raced to claim one decision, it clears the second run's ids and re-joins that run.

The rule depends on the decision's command and the run's `-c` payload being byte-equal. `TestRewritePayloadRoundTrips` checks that through `/bin/bash` over a corpus with quotes, `$VAR`, backslashes, newlines, chains and non-ASCII. Known limit: two sessions that share a cwd and run the same command within 10 minutes can be mis-paired. `joined_by` tells the reader how each join was made.

Tests for the join: `TestJoinHeuristic`, `TestJoinNoDoubleUse`, `TestJoinTrustsWriteTimeIds`, `TestCorrelateWriteTime`, `TestCorrelateReadsRotatedTail`, `TestCorrelateTailWindow`. Tests for reading: `TestReadAfterOffsetAndTornLine`, `TestReadTail`, `TestActivityTailThenPoll`, `TestReadReset`, `TestReadResetAfterRotationKeepsNewest`, `TestReadFilters`, `TestRotateAtMaxBytes`, and `TestConcurrentAppend` (1000 appends from 100 goroutines, all parseable).

### The dashboard: `watchdeck ui`

The same binary serves the dashboard, using only `net/http` and `embed` from the standard library: no new Go dependency, no JavaScript build step, no CDN. Fonts (Space Grotesk and JetBrains Mono, latin subsets, with their OFL texts alongside) were embedded (`TestFontsAreWOFF2AndSmall`) until 2026-10-28, when the dashboard moved to the system fonts and shipped no font file (section 16).

The command is `watchdeck ui [--port N] [--no-open] [--idle MINUTES] [--no-transcripts] [--detach [--open]] [--ensure [--open]] [--service] [--stop] [--rotate-token]`. It binds `127.0.0.1` only; since M5.3 on the port it last used when that is free, else a random one. Started from a terminal it exits 0 after 30 minutes without an authenticated API request. `--idle 0` disables that, except under `--detach`, which always keeps an idle exit. `--ensure` and `--service` run with no idle exit; one dashboard runs per state dir (see "Always-on dashboard (M5.3)").

Pages:

- **Sessions** (the home page since M5.5): every session of every project, "Live now" cards, filters and full-text search; see "Sessions page (M5.5)".
- **Skills:** cards and a table, a traffic light per skill, the Request → Grant → Runs-under strip, per-item Grant buttons, and the equivalent `watchdeck allow` command echoed back.
- **Grants:** every override block.
- **Enforce:** install or uninstall the hooks (three in M4, six since M5.2) after a confirm, with a `.bak-<ts>` copy written first, and a card for a settings file that does not parse.
- **Flow:** the agent map.

The page polls `GET /api/activity?after=<next>` while it is visible: every 2 s, or every 15 s while the event stream is connected. Since M5.5 it also holds one `GET /api/events` stream (text/event-stream) open, read with `fetch` and a streaming body reader, never `EventSource`: `EventSource` cannot send an `Authorization` header, so the token would have to go in the query string. Details in "Sessions page (M5.5)".

**Token model.**

- The token is 32 random bytes (43 characters, base64url). Until M5.3 each launch made a new one; since M5.3 it is kept in `<stateDir>/ui.token` (0600) and reused across restarts (see "Always-on dashboard (M5.3)").
- The launch URL carries the token in the fragment, `http://127.0.0.1:<port>/#t=<token>`, which browsers never send to the server.
- The page moves the token to the tab's `sessionStorage` and rewrites the address to `#/sessions` (`#/skills` before M5.5).
- Every `/api/` request sends the token as `Authorization: Bearer`, compared in constant time.
- The full URL goes to stdout only when no browser was opened (`--no-open`, `--detach` without `--open`, or the opener failed), and never to stderr. After a successful open, stdout says `watchdeck ui: opened http://127.0.0.1:<port>/ in your browser`, without the token, so an inline slash-command expansion never puts the token in a transcript.
- `--detach` re-executes the binary in its own session with stdio on `/dev/null`. The child hands its launch line to the parent over an inherited pipe (fd 3). (Before M5.3 this kept the token off the disk; it is now in `ui.token`, 0600, in the state dir that sandboxed scripts cannot read.)

This is a local tool, not multi-user auth: anyone who can read the launch link, or run code as the user, can use the dashboard.

**Threat model.**

| Asset | Threat | Control | Evidence |
|---|---|---|---|
| Dashboard API (grants, settings writes) | another host on the network | listens on `tcp4 127.0.0.1:<port>` only; no host flag exists | `TestListenLoopbackOnly` |
| Dashboard API | a web page using DNS rebinding | `Host` must be exactly `127.0.0.1:<port>` or `localhost:<port>`, else 403 | `TestHostCheck`, `TestOptionsStarIsGuarded` |
| Dashboard API | a cross-site page posting to localhost | every non-GET needs `Origin` equal to `http://` + Host, `Sec-Fetch-Site` `same-origin` or `none` when present, `Content-Type: application/json` (no simple form post) and a body of at most 1 MiB (403, 403, 415, 413) | `TestPostNeedsOrigin`, `TestSecFetchSiteCrossSiteRejected`, `TestContentTypeAndBodyCap` |
| Dashboard API | another local process, including a sandboxed script granted `network: localhost`, which can reach the port | the per-launch Bearer token on every `/api/` path, constant-time compare, 401 | `TestAPIRequiresBearer`, `TestExtraRouteIsGuarded`; no test drives a sandboxed script against the port (designed, not tested) |
| Token | leaking through output, logs or the transcript | fragment only; stdout only when no browser opened; never stderr, never logged; kept on disk only in `ui.token` (0600, M5.3); `/watchdeck:open` and the band's `2` key run `--ensure --open`, which never prints the URL (an opener failure exits 1) | `TestNoTokenInLogsOrURLPrint`, `TestUIDetach`, `TestUIPrintsNoTokenOnStderr`, `TestUIEnsureOpen`, `TestRotateTokenCommand`, `TestEnsureNeverPrintsToken`; mod test "the band key 2 ensures the dashboard and never toasts a token" |
| The page | injected script or style, framing | CSP `default-src 'none'; script-src 'self'; style-src 'self'; img-src 'self' data:; connect-src 'self'; base-uri 'none'; form-action 'none'; frame-ancestors 'none'` (`font-src 'self'` until 2026-10-28, when the last font file left the binary), plus `nosniff`, `X-Frame-Options: DENY`, `no-referrer`, `no-store` and COOP/CORP same-origin on every response; no inline style or script; only local assets | `TestStaticServedWithCSP`, `TestIndexHasNoInlineStyleOrScript`, `TestScriptsAndStylesStayLocal` |
| Server state and lifetime | unauthenticated requests with side effects, or keeping the server alive | outside `/api/` only GET and HEAD exist (405); the skills cache is dropped and the idle clock touched only after the bearer check, so idle exit counts only authenticated API calls | `TestSkillsReportShape` (unauthenticated and static POSTs leave the cache alone), `TestOptionsStarIsGuarded`, `TestIdleExit`, `TestHandlerBeforeServeRefuses` |
| Claude Code settings | a replayed or forged install or uninstall | `GET /api/enforce` issues a 16-byte nonce: one use, 5-minute TTL, spent even when the POST fails, at most 64 outstanding per purpose (since the final fix wave of Plan 04, 2026-10-19, the oldest of the same purpose goes first, so a busy Home tab's `needs` nonces never evict another tab's `rules` or `enforce` nonce: `TestNonceCapPerPurpose`); a `.bak-<ts>` copy before any write, never overwriting an existing backup | `TestEnforceNonceOneUse`, `TestEnforceNonceSpentOnFailure`, `TestEnforceNonceCap`, `TestNonceCapPerPurpose`, `TestEnforceRequiresGuard`, `TestBackupsInTheSameSecondDoNotCollide` |
| `overrides.yaml` | the model granting itself through a slash command | both plugin commands carry `disable-model-invocation: true`; each pre-approves one `watchdeck <sub> *` pattern only | `plugin/skills/{open,status}/SKILL.md`; the model's refusal in a live session is designed, not tested |
| `overrides.yaml` | the in-session mod granting without the person | grant only from a key press, after `$.ui.ask` returns exactly `Grant`; argv built from the activity record's structured `ungranted[]` and checked against an allow-list; only the absolute binary resolved at session start | mod tests "Cancel runs nothing; Grant runs exactly the allow argv", "the band key 1 goes through the same confirm", "a fix outside the allow-list is refused before any confirm", "a copy under the project or $TMPDIR is refused (decision 13)" |
| The person's judgement | forged trailers, or text imitating the hook's "requested but not granted" clause, in script output or in a cloned repository's `.watchdeck.yaml` | the mod and the map build fixes only from structured `ungranted[]`, never from `reason` or output text; a trailer on a call the hook did not name is ignored; override parse errors name the field, never the value; paths echoed in "project override ignored" messages, in `Effective.Summary()` and in the protected-path warning go through `manifest.displayPath` | mod tests "a forged trailer on a call the hook did not name is ignored" and "a forged ungranted line in the script's output never reaches the fix"; `TestOverridesErrorEchoesNoValue`, `TestRunOverridesErrorEchoesNoValue`, `TestParseOverridesErrorsCarryNoValues`, `TestDisplayPath`, `TestProjectIgnoredEchoesSanitizedPath`, `TestProjectIgnoredPathCannotImitateReason`, `TestRunProjectIgnoredPathCannotImitateTrailer` |
| `watchdeck allow` command line | a skill name with a leading dash read as a flag, or `.`/`..` | `ValidSkillName`: `*`, or `^[A-Za-z0-9._][A-Za-z0-9._-]{0,127}$` and not `.` or `..`; checked by `allow`, `manifest.Apply`, the API and the overrides parser; the mod drops such names everywhere it parses one | `TestApplyRejectsInvalid`; mod tests "a skill named like a flag (…) is never granted" and "names that read as flags are dropped everywhere they are parsed" |
| Built-in deny list | a grant spelled as an absolute or symlinked path to `~/.ssh`, `~/.aws`, … | `manifest.Apply` resolves paths against the real home and refuses; the dashboard sets `Edit.Home` on the server, never from the client; `run` drops a write under a deny path and does not report it as granted | `TestApplyRefusesAbsoluteDenyPath`, `TestGrantRefusesAbsoluteDenyPath`, `TestRunDropsWriteUnderDenyPath`, `TestRunEffectiveOmitsDroppedWrite` |
| Transcripts | reading outside `projects/`, or more than the compact view reaching the page | read-only and on demand; under the projects dir only (`EvalSymlinks` and a prefix check); ids validated before the filesystem is touched; the compact view keeps no assistant text, thinking, file contents or tool results beyond a 240-character Bash snippet; the paths the reader opened are not serialized to the page, only the cwd and transcript path as display values (`TestNoRealPaths`); `--no-transcripts` opens none | `TestPathSafety`, `TestDiscoverSymlinkedDirsOutside`, `TestSubagentSymlinkOutsideRefused`, `TestDiscoverDoesNotOpenFiles`, `TestCompactRetention`; `TestTranscriptOutsideProjectsRefused`, `TestSessionsOpensNoTranscript`, `TestNoAbsolutePathsInJSON`, `TestNoRealPaths`, `TestRoutesAreGuarded` |
| The activity log | recording more than the stated fields | fixed schema; hints keep matched lines only; Skill input capped; mode 0600 | `TestAppendCreatesFile0600`, `TestHintsCapAndTrim`, `TestHintsTable` |
| The Bash fast path | dashboard code slowing every Bash call | packages linked into the hook do no init work; measured below | `TestPackageIsInitFree`, `TestLinkedPackagesHaveNoInitWork`, `TestLinkedPackagesAreInitFreeInSource`, `scripts/measure-hook.sh` |
| `run` | a backgrounded grandchild holding the output pipe and blocking `run` | `cmd.WaitDelay` (2 s) closes the pipes; the child's exit code and output come back intact | `TestLingeringGrandchildBoundedByWaitDelay`, `TestRunBackgroundedGrandchildDoesNotBlock`; the Linux versions were reviewed but not run |
| A Bash call | the mod failing, or the binary missing | the mod only observes and always returns the engine's result; a missing binary makes every hook pass through | mod tests "a call the hook has no opinion on is a dim row and is passed through" and "process.run rejects: binary missing, status says so, calls pass through" |

### The agent map

The Flow page draws one Claude Code session as a map of what policy did, in two views:

- **Topology:** You; agents, each with a policy ring; models; tools; MCP servers, one node per server; skills as shields, dashed when requested and solid when granted; and the OS sandbox.
- **Timeline:** a swimlane per agent, with skills and the sandbox as lanes.

Edge width counts calls, and edges are coloured by what policy did. A denied edge is drawn cut, and the card beside it shows the fix.

**Topology is an interactive canvas** (2026-10-13, after the owner's dogfood screenshot showed labels on edges and pills under nodes). The server still decides the nodes, their column and their order; the page lays the columns out from the measured label sizes, with every label under its shape (You's above it) on a canvas-coloured plate, packs each column so no two footprints touch, routes every edge from the current node positions, and places each pill at the first point along its edge that clears node bodies, labels and other pills. A pointer drag moves a node (mouse, touch and pen); its edges follow on the next animation frame; dragged positions persist per session in `localStorage` until "reset layout". Dragging the background pans; following canvas tools, a wheel or two-finger scroll pans too, and ctrl/⌘ + wheel (what a trackpad pinch sends) or a touch pinch zooms around the pointer; arrow keys move the focused node (Shift for larger steps; Tab walks the nodes). Hovering or focusing a node dims everything outside its neighbourhood; an edge is selectable and its card lists the calls behind it; solid edges carry a moving dash while the session is live (static under `prefers-reduced-motion`). While the content overflows the canvas, a "map" button in the controls column opens a minimap panel beside the controls (closed by default; Escape closes it), and Replay steps through the session's calls in order, revealing nodes and edges as they happened. Fit prefers a scale of at least 13/14 (14 px subtitles render at 13 px) and at most 1.3; to keep every column on screen it goes down to 12/14 (12 px text), and only past that does the Models side pan off, with the Enforcement column anchored on screen and the minimap offered. The timeline keeps the server's coordinates. Checked in Chromium at 1280×800 and 1600×1000, light and dark, with 1, 3 and 6 tools, an MCP server and fixture A (main agent plus a subagent, hide no-opinion on and off): every column, label and pill is on screen and clear of the zoom controls on first view, no two overlap, and the smallest text is 12.0 px (fixture A at 1280) to 14.8 px. Not checked in Safari or on a touch device (pinch was exercised with synthetic pointer events only).

**Data sources.**

1. The activity log: decisions, runs, skill invocations and denials.
2. Claude Code's transcripts, read by `internal/transcript`.
3. The skills report: request and grant per skill, used for "declared vs did".

Joins:

- A run joins its decision by the write-time rule above.
- A decision joins its transcript call by `tool_use_id`.
- A subagent's calls join through `agent_id` and the subagent's file.
- Runs left unjoined are matched by command, skills and cwd within 10 minutes. A run that falls inside another session's window for the same cwd and command is excluded.
- Transcript calls without ids are matched by exact command within 60 s.

Under `--no-transcripts`, or when the transcript is missing, the map is built from the activity log alone and says so.

**Evidence, ranked.** Each tool call gets exactly one policy, in this precedence: **deny** > **ask** > **allow** > **none**.

A call is denied when any of these holds:

- a `denied` record exists. Label `BLOCKED <reason>`: the script never ran.
- a joined run has any hint, whatever its exit. Label `DENIED <kind>`, evidence "hint": the kind of the first hint that carries a probe label or a known kind (a label names the kind when the scanner could not), and just `DENIED` when no hint names one.
- the run's outcome is `policy_error`, `no_sandbox` or `usage`. Labels `POLICY ERROR exit 125`, `NOT SANDBOXED exit 126`.
- the transcript's Bash result text shows `DENIED(`, `Operation not permitted`, `EPERM` or `Errno 1`. This is the third source, evidence `result_text`.

The `hook_success` attachment and the tool-result row's `permissionDecision` are further sources for the decision itself. A `failed` run with no hint and no marker is a plain failure, `exit N`, not a denial. The card names the evidence source in words.

An ask has four sub-states: `ask (unsandboxed)` for a low-confidence ask with no rewrite; `ask ✗` for a declined ask; `ask …` while pending; and `ask ✓` once a run has joined. The in-session mod no longer shows these states (pane removed 2026-10-18).

**Declared vs did is a floor.** For each skill in the session, the card compares what its manifest requested with what the evidence shows it tried. The verdict is one of `as_declared`, `undeclared`, `clean` or `unknown`. Every row says it is a floor, not a ceiling: the sandbox reports what it refused and what the script said, not everything the script did.

**Privacy.** Transcripts are read on demand and read-only, and never persisted; the only cache is in memory. The page gets a compact view: no assistant text, no thinking, no file contents, no full tool results. The only paths it carries are two display values the API passes in: the session's cwd and its transcript path, used for the "transcript not found at …" banner. The file paths the reader actually opened stay on the server (`TestNoRealPaths`). There is no redaction layer, by design: the page shows the person their own machine's data, behind loopback and the token. `watchdeck ui --no-transcripts` turns transcript reading off, and the map then shows watchdeck's own events only. The map's one write is the Grant button, which goes through the guarded grant route.

**Routes.**

`internal/mapapi` registers the routes. `mapapi.Routes(Deps{Paths, Cwd, Environ, NoTranscripts, Version})` builds them, and `cmd/watchdeck/ui.go` mounts them on the dashboard server through `ui.Options.Routes`, behind the same guard as every other `/api/` path.

- `GET /api/sessions` lists sessions from file metadata and one pass over the activity log. It opens no transcript. Runs without a `session_id` (a `watchdeck run` typed by hand) are grouped into a per-cwd "direct runs" pseudo-session.
- `GET /api/sessions/{id}/map?view=topology|timeline&hide_none=1` returns the graph. The session id is validated and resolved through `Discover`, never taken from a client path. A transcript path outside `projects/` is `Stat`ed for the ETag but never opened.
- **The ETag** is a sha256 over a `Stat` (size and mtime) of each of these inputs:
  - the transcript, and every file under its `subagents/`;
  - the activity log and its `.1`;
  - the user `overrides.yaml` and the project `.watchdeck.yaml`;
  - each skill's `PERMISSIONS.yaml`.

  The hash also covers the view, `hide_none`, the binary's version and a liveness bit. So a grant, or an edit to a manifest, refreshes the card. A poll whose `If-None-Match` still matches gets a 304: the server re-`Stat`s the inputs it remembered for that tag (at most 64 entries) and opens nothing.
- The graph's request and grant data (`Declared`) is built only for skills found on disk. Any other skill shows "not loaded" and no fix.
- Known limits:
  - A skill installed or removed while nothing else changes does not change the tag.
  - An unclaimed run in two sessions that share a cwd shows in both.

`internal/mapmodel` builds the graph as a pure, deterministic function. Layout is computed on the server, so the client only places coordinates.

Tests for `internal/mapmodel`: `TestGolden` (golden graph JSON for both views), `TestDeterminism`, `TestLayoutGrowsInsteadOfOverlapping`, `TestMissingTimestampsSortLast`, `TestMCPGrouping`, `TestJoinByIDFixtureA`, `TestJoinRunFallbackInOrder`, `TestJoinDecisionByCommand`, `TestUnmatchedRunOnSandboxOnly`, `TestOtherSessionsRecordsIgnored`, `TestSubagentAttributionWithoutTranscript`, `TestPolicyPrecedence`, `TestOutcomeStream`, `TestHookAttachmentAloneInferredRun`, `TestApprovalsAndAutoMode`, `TestHideNone`, `TestDeclaredVsDid`, `TestGrantReflected`, `TestNoFixWithoutDeclared`, `TestNoTranscripts`, `TestNoRealPaths`, `TestFixtureB`. Tests for `internal/mapapi`: `TestSessionsOpensNoTranscript`, `TestMap200Then304`, `TestETagChangesOnActivityAppend`, `TestETagChangesOnSubagentGrowth`, `TestETagChangesOnOverridesEdit`, `TestBadSessionID`, `TestUnknownSession`, `TestNoTranscripts`, `TestTranscriptOutsideProjectsRefused`, `TestDirectPseudoSession`, `TestNoAbsolutePathsInJSON`, `TestRoutesAreGuarded`. Front end: `TestPagesBarrelImportsMap`.

**What is verified for the map, and what is not.** The V numbers are the items of the planning record.

| # | Claim | Status 2026-10-12 |
|---|---|---|
| V1 | hook stdin carries `tool_use_id` and `transcript_path` | held (`hook-stdin.jsonl`) |
| V2 | hook `tool_use_id` equals transcript `tool_use.id`, main and subagent | held (`TestHookStdinIdsMatchTranscript`) |

| V4 | project dir escaping | corrected: every non-alphanumeric character becomes `-`, per UTF-16 unit, hashed over 200 (`TestEscapeProjectDir`, `TestDiscoverLongCwd`) |
| V6, V7 | subagent files, `.meta.json`, Agent input and result keys | held on fixture A; the larger subagent fixture B is synthetic |

Fixture A is a real capture: one throwaway `claude -p` session in a temp project with `canary-basic`. That skill has no manifest, so the decision is `allow` with a rewrite. The capture was scrubbed by `scripts/scrub-transcript.py` and reviewed by hand (`internal/transcript/testdata/e2e-canary/README.md`). `scripts/fixture-session.sh --skill canary-request` captures the ask variant.

### The in-session mod

The plugin's `hooks.json` carries the three command hooks and `"modules": ["./register.tsx"]`. That module is a Claude Code hooks module: it draws a one-line policy band above the prompt. It has no build step and no npm dependencies. Claude Code writes the type declarations into `plugin/.claude-plugin/types/` on load, and that directory is gitignored.

**Trimmed 2026-10-18.** The map pane, `/watchdeck:map`, `/watchdeck:audit` and `/watchdeck:allow` were removed from the mod; the band stays (`1` grant via a confirm, `2` open the dashboard, `3` fix), `/watchdeck:ui` became `/watchdeck:open`, and `/watchdeck:status` no longer prints activity counts.

Hooks the module uses:

- `session.start`: resolves the binary, probes `watchdeck version --json`, and reads `paths --json`.
- `classic.PreToolUse` on Bash: observes the plugin hook's own decision, which `next(e)` returns (verified 2026-10-12).
- `tool.call`: reads the result text, the trailer and the markers.
- `agent.spawn`, `turn.start` and `turn.complete`.
- `skill.prompt`: catches skills the person typed.
- `ui.render` for `AbovePrompt`.

The module reads the activity log through `watchdeck activity --json --session <id> --kinds decision,run,skill_invoked,denied --tail 200`, then `--after <next>`. It goes through the binary because the module's file reader stops at 4 MiB while the log rotates at 8 MiB. With an older binary it falls back to reading the file directly while it is under 4 MiB. The activity log is the source of truth and corrects the module's in-process guess.

**Grant path.** This is the one place the mod executes something with the person's privileges, and it is bounded at every step:

- **The binary.** At session start the mod resolves `watchdeck` once to an absolute path, searching PATH, then `~/.local/share/watchdeck/bin`, `/opt/homebrew/bin` and `/usr/local/bin`. It refuses any path under the cwd or `$TMPDIR`, because a sandboxed script can write there.
- **The trigger.** A grant runs only from a key press, `1` on the band, and only after `$.ui.ask` returns exactly `Grant`.
- **The argv.** It is built only from the activity record's `ungranted[]`: `allow <skill> [--network localhost|all] [--write <path>] [--caches]`. It only widens, so it never uses `--auto`, a revoke flag or `*`, and never writes to `$HOME`, a built-in deny path or a `.env` file.
- **What it cannot grant.** `read`, `env` and `exec` items have no flag; for those the card says which file to edit.
- **Timing.** `1: grant` stays dim until the run's record arrives.

Nothing on a path the model can reach (`tool.call`, `agent.spawn`, `skill.prompt`, timers) can grant. `$.ui.ask` refuses under `-p`, so a headless session never grants.

**Deviations from the approved mockup.** The module API forced these, and the owner was informed on 2026-10-12:

- The band's keys are digits, `1` to grant, `2` to open the dashboard and `3` to fix, because a letter key only works while the band holds the keys. Side effect: while a denial is pending, typing `1`, `2` or `3` as the first character in an empty prompt presses the band.
- The per-row tool annotation ships turned off.

**Slash commands.** `/watchdeck:open` and `/watchdeck:status` both carry `disable-model-invocation: true`. The reason: a pre-approved inline shell line plus model-chosen `$ARGUMENTS` is a surface a security tool's plugin must not add. The advisory `watchdeck` skill stays model-invocable, and it runs nothing.

Tests run with `claude plugin test plugin`, every UI test over both the terminal and desktop surfaces: `observe.test.ts`, `activity.test.ts`, `band.test.tsx`, `grant.test.tsx`, `missing-binary.test.ts`, `subagent.test.ts` and `parsers.test.ts`. `claude plugin validate plugin --strict` passes with the dual-form `hooks.json`.

### CLI changes

- **`audit` exit code.** `audit` now exits 0 by default, even with an invalid manifest; `--strict` restores exit 1. The reason: Claude Code aborts a slash command's inline shell on any non-zero exit, so one malformed `PERMISSIONS.yaml` would have emptied the `/watchdeck:audit` command (removed 2026-10-18; the exit code stays for scripts). Exits 2 (discovery error) and 3 (usage) are unchanged.
- **`audit --json`.** `effective` stays the summary string, so it is backward compatible there. New keys: `description`, `request`, `grant`, `effectiveDetail`. One breaking key change: `scripts[]` entries are now `rel`, `lang`, `inGlob`, and `hits[]` entries are `category`, `file`, `line`, `pattern`, `snippet`; they used to be the Go field names (`Rel`, `Category`, …). Test: `TestJSONTagsStable`.
- **`allow` revoke flags.** New: `--remove-write PATH` (repeatable), `--no-caches`, `--no-auto`, and `--reset`, which deletes the skill's whole block; `--network none` takes network back. Skill names must pass `ValidSkillName`, and unknown flags exit 3. Edits go through `manifest.Apply` and an atomic `SaveOverrides`: temp file, mode 0600, then rename, and an invalid result is never written. Tests: `TestApplyWidenAndRevoke`, `TestApplyDefaultsStar`, `TestRenderRoundTrip`, `TestSaveOverridesAtomic`, `TestAllowCommandMapping`.
- **Invalid skill keys.** An overrides file whose `skills:` map has a key that fails `ValidSkillName` is now invalid as a whole, like any other parse error (`ParseOverrides`).
- **New commands.** `activity`, `paths [--json]`, `version --json` (prints `{"version","platform","enforcement"}`) and `ui`. Tests: `TestActivityJSONShape`, `TestActivityPagingMatchesAPI`, `TestActivityAbsentLog`, `TestActivityOff`, `TestActivityUsageErrors`, `TestPathsJSON`, `TestVersionJSON`, `TestUIDetach`, `TestUIUsageErrors`.

### Fast path and binary size

Measured with `scripts/measure-hook.sh` on 2026-10-12: darwin arm64, go1.27.2, stripped builds of the revision before M4 and of the M4 tree (after), 200 spawns per payload, three runs, on a machine that was not idle.

| | before | after |
|---|---|---|
| no-opinion hook, median | 4.7 ms | 6.7 ms |
| no-opinion hook, p95 | 5.9 ms | 8.0 ms |
| stripped binary | 4.69 MB | 8.64 MB |
| summed package init (`GODEBUG=inittrace=1`) | ≈1.2 ms | ≈1.2 ms |

No watchdeck package added init work (`TestPackageIsInitFree` guards `internal/ui`). The extra 2 ms is the cost of loading a larger binary after linking `internal/ui` (`net/http`, `crypto/tls`, `mime`, `compress`), not of running anything. The plan's acceptance limit was +1 ms on the median, and this is +2 ms. Owner decision 2026-10-12: keep one binary and accept the cost. If the cost starts to matter, a separate hook binary or a build-tag split is the way back.

**Init-free linked packages (M5.0, 2026-10-13).** M4 added no init work, but watchdeck already had some: inittrace of the hook at the M4 close showed it in seven of its packages, all older than M4 (`scan` about 1.1 ms and 1.3 MB from ~80 package-level regexps; `match`, `manifest`, `skillmd` regexps; `hook`, `inventory`, `skills` map literals and one call). They now build on first use (`sync.Once`, `internal/lazyre`) or use switches and per-OS constants, and inittrace shows none. Measured with `scripts/measure-hook.sh` with BEFORE at the M4 close and AFTER at the M5.0 code (measured before its rebase onto main; the rebase touched no Go file), darwin arm64, go1.27.2, 200 spawns per payload, three runs, machine not idle; each cell is the middle of the three runs (the three in brackets):

| | M4 close | M5.0 code |
|---|---|---|
| no-opinion hook, median | 6.7 ms (7.48, 6.72, 6.64) | 5.9 ms (5.93, 6.08, 5.74) |
| no-opinion hook, p95 | 8.3 ms (8.75, 8.32, 8.01) | 7.3 ms (7.34, 7.34, 7.19) |
| rewrite hook, median | 7.4 ms | 6.4 ms |
| summed package init | 1.15–1.48 ms, 13,608 allocs | 0.27–0.33 ms, ≈484 allocs |
| packages with init work | 95 | 88 (no watchdeck package) |
| stripped binary | 9,115,730 bytes (8.69 MiB) | 9,115,682 bytes (8.69 MiB) |

The no-opinion median fell by 0.6 to 1.6 ms per run. Guards: `TestLinkedPackagesHaveNoInitWork` runs the built binary under `GODEBUG=inittrace=1` and fails on any package of this module with init work; `TestLinkedPackagesAreInitFreeInSource` parses every non-test file (all GOOS) of every module package that `go list -deps ./cmd/watchdeck` names and reports a `func init`, a call or a map literal in a package-level var (`errors.New` of a string literal is allowed; inittrace shows it is static). Both are in `cmd/watchdeck/initfree_test.go`.

- **Map API:** `TestSessionsOpensNoTranscript`, `TestMap200Then304`, `TestETagChangesOnActivityAppend`, `TestETagChangesOnSubagentGrowth`, `TestETagChangesOnOverridesEdit`, `TestBadSessionID`, `TestUnknownSession`, `TestNoTranscripts`, `TestTranscriptOutsideProjectsRefused`, `TestDirectPseudoSession`, `TestNoAbsolutePathsInJSON`, `TestRoutesAreGuarded`.
- **Mod:** the seven `plugin/tests` files listed above.
- **Fast path:** the measurement table above.

### Onboarding: setup, doctor, demo (M5.1, 2026-10-13)

Three commands for the first run and for troubleshooting.

- **`watchdeck setup [--yes] [--project] [--file PATH] [--plugin] [--exclusion-only]`**
  1. Runs the sandbox self-test (below) and stops before writing anything if it fails. If the profile cannot be applied because setup is already inside a sandbox, the self-test is reported as skipped and setup goes on.
  2. Prints a few lines of audit totals.
  3. Settings. If the plugin is active, setup adds only the sandbox exclusion `watchdeck run *`. "Active" means `skills.PluginActive` finds `watchdeck@*` in `$CLAUDE_CONFIG_DIR/plugins/installed_plugins.json` and it is enabled (or unlisted) in the user, user-local, project and project-local settings, the same rule discovery uses. `--plugin` covers `claude --plugin-dir`, which leaves no trace in those files. Otherwise setup does what `enforce install` does.
  - `settings.Plan` shows the change before anything is written, and every write is confirmed. Without a terminal and without `--yes`, setup prints the plan and exits 1. Terminal detection uses the termios ioctl, so `/dev/null` does not count as a terminal (`TestIsTerminalRefusesDevNullAndPipes`).
  - `--exclusion-only` skips steps 1 and 2. The in-session mod's band fix uses it (`3: fix`): it runs `<absolute binary> setup --exclusion-only --yes --plugin` only after `$.ui.ask` returns exactly "Fix" (`plugin/tests/setup-band.test.tsx`).
