Facts the tests pin: the managed file comes first and drop-ins follow in name order (`TestLocateManagedDropIns`, `TestLocatePerOS`); a scalar such as `sandbox.enabled` takes the managed value over local, project and user (`TestReadMergesScopes`, `TestReadScalarPrecedence`); `$defaults` entries are kept (`TestReadKeepsDefaultsEntry`); one unreadable file does not hide the others (`TestReadPerFileError`). `Manage` is idempotent, removes only strings it added, refuses a managed file, retries once when the file changes while it is written, and leaves unrelated bytes identical in Claude Code's two-space form (`TestManageIdempotentAndUnclaimed`, `TestManageRemovesOnlyOwned`, `TestManageRefusesManaged`, `TestManageRetriesOnDrift`, `TestManageLeavesUnrelatedBytesIdentical`). `Reconcile` reports a value the person removed by hand (`TestReconcileUserRemoval`). The ledger is 0600 (`TestLedger0600`). `settings.SaveSync` fsyncs file, backup and directory, and the encoder no longer escapes `&`, `<` and `>` (`TestSaveSyncWritesAndBacksUp`, `TestEncodeKeepsAmpersand`). Package init cost: `TestLinkedPackagesHaveNoInitWork`.

### Native deny and ask rules before the rewrite

The mechanism, the matcher's documented and chosen readings, and the cost are in "Native rules before the rewrite" at the end of section 7. Summary of what is verified: a deny or ask rule the person wrote is matched against the original command before any rewrite (`TestNativeRulesDenyBeforeRewrite`, `TestNativeRulesAskBeforeRewrite`, `TestNativeRulesUnparseableAsks`, `TestNativeRulesBareBashDeniesAll`, `TestNativeRulesRecordNamesThePattern`, `TestNativeRulesModelTypedAskNamesTheRule`); the matcher has 139 table rows including every documented example (`TestMatchBashDocExamples`); no rules load on the no-opinion path (`TestNativeRulesNotLoadedOnNoOpinion`); and a wrapper behind an expanded word still exposes the wrapped command at hook level (`TestNativeRulesWrappersBehindExpansions`, 34 probes). An ask keeps the sandbox rewrite, except in `bypassPermissions` and `dontAsk`, where it goes out without it (`TestNativeRulesAskDropsRewriteInBypass`, `TestNativeRulesModelTypedRunInBypass`). In bypassPermissions a hook ask carrying updatedInput is not auto-approved: Claude Code reports a permission denial and the rewritten command does not run; tested 2026-10-19 with Claude Code 2.1.303, one `-p` run (details in section 7); `dontAsk` and an interactive bypass session were not run.

Fast path, `scripts/measure-hook.sh` against the Plan 01 close (200 spawns, four runs, machine not idle):

| No-opinion path | Run 1 | Run 2 | Run 3 | Run 4 |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| Median delta (ms) | +0.02 | +0.93 | -0.01 | +0.03 |
| p95 delta (ms) | -0.05 | +2.11 | -0.09 | +0.07 |

Run 2 is read as noise (the after binary's rewrite payload was faster in that run). The stripped binary grew by 33,680 bytes. The measurement home has no Claude Code settings file, so it shows the stat-only cost, not a cache read.

### Known limits

- **Fixed 2026-10-19 (Plan 03 Task 2).** A wrapper option whose attached value is only an expansion (`timeout -s"$X" KILL 5 rm …`) was read one way and escaped a deny rule; it is now read both ways (see "Native deny and ask rules before the rewrite").
- **Fixed in the final fix wave (2026-10-19).** Chained wrappers are linear: the walk records forms only for a start it has not walked, a wrapper's option scan visits each word once, forms are deduplicated through a set, and the single-space forms are slices of one joined string. No reading is ever dropped, since a cap would fail open (security review, 2026-10-19): `nice -"$A" 1 … -"$J" 10 rm -rf x` (ten readings) is denied, and one `nice` with 1,200 expanded option words is timed too. 1,200 chained wrappers of each probed kind take about 2 to 6 ms (`TestMatchBashChainCost`, bound 1 s; the final reviewer's probe measured 23 to 46 ms before, growing quadratically). The legacy `nice -10 -n 5 rm …` family, bundled short flags, long-option prefixes and line continuations are seen through (section 7).
- **Not read: the forms Claude Code's documentation does not strip.** A quoted or escaped command word (`'rm' -rf x`, `\rm -rf x`), `${IFS}`, brace expansion, and `exec`, `env`, `sudo`, `eval` or `xargs` with options are not matched, in parity with the documented non-matches (final review ruling, 2026-10-19). Such a command the hook would rewrite is asked and rewritten, not denied.
- **`time "$P" rm …`.** The matcher denies it, but at hook level the call is "ask, no rewrite", because skill detection returns low for a first word that is a bare expansion; the original reaches Claude Code, whose own rules strip `time`.
- **One skip per wrapper.** After a wrapper, at most one expanded word is skipped, plus one value word for an expanded option and timeout's duration. `nice "$N" "$M" rm …` stops at `"$M"` and is not matched as `rm`. The duration is never validated, so the matcher can deny a command that runs nothing; never the other way round.
- **Not a boundary.** Claude Code's own rules do not stop `/usr/bin/curl` or `sh -c 'curl …'`, and the check inherits that. A command the hook does not rewrite is left to Claude Code.
- **`installed_at`.** `watchdeck setup` writes `installed_at` (RFC 3339, UTC) to `config.yaml` on every successful completion when it is unset, on the settings, plugin and exclusion-only paths alike, and never overwrites it (`TestInstalledAtRoundTrip`, `TestInstalledAtNeverOverwritten`, `TestInstalledAtUnquotedHandWritten`, `TestSetupRecordsInstalledAtOnce`, `TestSetupPluginPathRecordsInstalledAt`). A declined or not-applied run records nothing, and `--yes` writes no autostart key (`TestSetupPluginOnlyAddsExclusion`). `doctor` reads it, and the inbox uses it as its cutoff since Plan 03 (section 9, "The inbox"). `setup --file <path>` and `--project` also record this global date, so the `doctor` line is not proof that the user's own settings file was hooked. `doctor` prints "installed since <date>" or warns "not recorded". `enforce install` alone does not write it.
- **Hooks on file tools (per tool since 2026-10-19).** `enforce install`, `setup` and the dashboard's Enforce page register one PreToolUse group naming exactly the file tools that need the hook (`policy.FilesNeed`), for example `Write|Edit|MultiEdit|NotebookEdit`, and none when no tool needs it. A file tool needs the hook for a path or agent gate naming it, the person's own rule naming it, or an enabled built-in pack rule for it at ask or deny on any input field, unless that rule's native twin is installed (`rulebook.CoveredTwins`: every recommended string with that rule as Twin is present, a deny counting for an ask) and the rule is at its default action. A rule raised above its default never counts as covered, because its twin's strings were chosen for the default and can be narrower than the rule (`read-other-credentials` pairs with `Read(~/.netrc)` only). With default packs and all twelve recommended strings installed, Read is unhooked and the four write tools stay hooked: the `cred-in-write` asks read written content and have no native twin. The covered list is read once per command (or Enforce request) from Claude Code's settings, never in the hook: managed and user settings for a user-scope install, plus the project's files for a project-scope install, so native rules in one project do not drop a hook for every other project. A settings file that cannot be read covers nothing. When the needed group changes, `InstallWith` moves our entry from the old group to the new one, leaving anyone else's entries and their group in place (a plain `Install` does not prune); `ExtraStatus` counts a wider group of ours as covering a narrower need. `doctor` prints one `file tools` line per tool set: hooked with the first reason, needed but not hooked (warn, noting when the recommended rules are not all installed), hooked though nothing needs it, not hooked because the recommended rules cover reads or edits, and not hooked with no enabled rule. Gap: log-only pack rules on paths without a twin stop producing records for a tool that is unhooked. With Read unhooked these are `read-other-credentials` on gcloud, kube, docker and gh hosts files and `read-dotenv` on a `.env` outside the project root. With `cred-in-write` off and the write tools unhooked they are `persistence/shell-rc-write` on `.zshenv`, `.zlogin`, `.bash_login` and fish config, `persistence/launch-agent-write` on `/Library/LaunchDaemons` and `/Library/LaunchAgents`, and `persistence/systemd-unit-write` on `/etc/systemd/system` (the pack regexes cover these system paths; the native twins cover only the user's `~/Library/LaunchAgents` and `~/.config/systemd/user`). Prompts are not always kept: the secrets-read Read ask rules also match other accounts' homes, which their native twins do not cover (section 9, "Known limits", item 1). Tests: `TestFilesNeedTable`, `TestCoveredTwins`, `TestNeededExtraFilesByNativeCover`, `TestInstallWithDropsUnneededFiles`, `TestInstallWithReplacesFilesGroup`, `TestInstallWithReplacesSharedFilesGroup`, `TestFilesHookedWhenNativeDeclined`, `TestDoctorFileToolsLine`. Gap: the plugin's static `hooks.json` always registers the file tools (documented in GUIDE, "What this does not do").

### Not verified yet (2026-10-19)

- **A person's "No" in the transcript.** Interactive capture pending. Until then a blocked call with no decision fields is `blocked` (reason unknown) and a `user` verdict for a rejection is designed, not tested against a real capture.
- Parallel `tool_result` blocks in one row (above).
- A hook's `ask` with `updatedInput` in `dontAsk`, and in an interactive `bypassPermissions` session (only `bypassPermissions` under `-p` was run).
- Linux and Windows: `claudesettings` managed paths were checked by table only; Windows has no managed path. Nothing in this section was run on Linux.

## 9. The Rules control plane (Plan 03, 2026-10-19)

Plan 03 made watchdeck a writer of Claude Code's own settings. It lists every rule on the machine in one place, adds a rule in one step (as a native Claude Code string where one exists, else as a hook rule), offers twelve recommended native rules at setup, keeps an inbox of what needs the person, and hooks file tools only where a rule needs the hook. This section says what was verified, with the test that shows it, and what was not. Run on darwin arm64 only.

### Ownership and the lock

- **Per-intent claims.** A ledger entry names a settings file, a key and a string, and holds the intent ids that claim it (`rule_ids`). An add by a second intent adds its id; a remove drops only the caller's id; the string leaves the file only when its last claim goes, and a string the person wrote is never claimed (`TestManageSharedStringRefCounts`, `TestManageRemoveUnknownRuleIsNoop`, `TestRecommendedNeverClaimsUserString`). An older ledger with a lone `rule_id` is read as one claim and written back in the new form, still `v: 1` (`TestLedgerMigratesRuleID`). Removing the last string leaves the empty array and the `permissions` object in place (`TestManageLeavesEmptyArray`).
- **One lock for every writer.** `claudesettings.Transact` takes `claude-settings.lock` in the state dir (flock, 2 s deadline), loads the ledger, runs the change and saves the ledger only when the change succeeded. Two processes are serialised and a held lock times out with `ErrLockTimeout` (`TestTransactLoadsEditsSaves`, `TestTransactSerialisesProcesses`, `TestTransactTimesOut`). The lock is a no-op on Windows (known limit 6).
- **One write and one backup per file.** `ManageMany` and `ManageKeys` apply every change to one settings file in one write, across keys, so `rules recommended --install`, setup and uninstall write each file once with one `.bak-<ts>` (`TestManageManyOneWrite`, `TestManageKeysOneWriteAcrossKeys`, `TestApplyManyStepsOneBackup`).
- **Tests never read the real managed settings.** `ManagedDirOverride` is a package variable, not an environment variable, so a sandboxed script cannot point watchdeck at a managed file of its choosing; the cmd and ui test mains set it to an empty temp dir (`TestManagedDirOverride`).

### The rulebook: groups, ids, sentences

`rulebook.List` composes Claude Code's permission, auto-mode and sandbox settings at every scope, the built-in packs and the person's policy files into five groups, always present and in this order: Sandbox, Blocked, Auto mode, watchdeck rules, and "Ask first, or allowed without asking". Each row has a plain-English sentence, an owner (us, the person, managed, a pack), what enforces it and whether it is on. List is read-only: it reconciles a copy of the ledger to report drift and never saves (`TestListComposes`, `TestListDriftRow`, `TestListSubjectRows`, `TestSentenceTemplates`). One unreadable file becomes a warning naming it, not a failure (`TestListUnreadableFileWarns`).

Row ids are stable across runs. A settings string is `native:<scope>:<key>:<fnv32a hex8 of the value>` (and `automode:`, `domain:`, `excluded:` for the other settings lists), a pack rule `pack:<pack>/<rule>`, a hook rule `user:<id>`, and a subject `subject:<kind>:<name>`, with later rows of the same subject suffixed by a hash of their list and entry. `watchdeck rules list` prints the groups, the id and the sentence on each line, and `--json` the whole book (`TestRulesListShowsNativeAndPacks`).

### Recommended rules

Twelve native strings, all at user scope, each paired with the built-in pack rule that covers the same ground in the hook (its twin):

| Key | Strings | Twin |
|---|---|---|
| `permissions.deny` | `Read(~/.ssh/id_*)`, `Read(~/.aws/credentials)`, `Read(~/.netrc)` | `secrets-read/read-ssh-private-key`, `read-aws-credentials`, `read-other-credentials` |
| `permissions.ask` | `Read(.env)`, `Read(.env.*)` | `secrets-read/read-dotenv` |
| `permissions.ask` | `Edit(~/.zshrc)`, `Edit(~/.bashrc)`, `Edit(~/.profile)`, `Edit(~/.zprofile)`, `Edit(~/.bash_profile)` | `persistence/shell-rc-write` |
| `permissions.ask` | `Edit(~/Library/LaunchAgents/**)`, `Edit(~/.config/systemd/user/**)` | `persistence/launch-agent-write`, `systemd-unit-write` |

`Read(~/.ssh/id_*)` also matches the public keys `id_*.pub`, so reading them is blocked too; the setup question says so. The two dotenv strings are bare file names, which Claude Code's documentation (fetched 2026-10-19) says match at any depth under the working directory; `Read(./.env)`, their spelling before the final fix wave, is anchored at the working directory and covered the top level only. `rules recommended --install` and setup build these steps with `rulebook.RecommendedPlan`, which writes each string exactly as listed, since `Propose` anchors a relative path (`TestRecommendedPlanExactStrings`). Each twin names a real pack rule; no recommended string writes, every other one is anchored for user scope (`~/`), and each has its own ledger intent id, `recommended:` plus the value with every byte that is not an ASCII letter or digit as `-` (`TestRecommendedNamesRealPackRules`, `TestRecommendedNeverWrite`, `TestRecommendedUserScopeAnchors`, `TestRecommendedIntentIDs`, `TestRecommendedMissing`). `watchdeck setup` offers the missing ones in a Rules step before the Hooks step, default yes, also with `--exclusion-only`; a second run adds nothing; declining still completes setup (`TestSetupInstallsRecommendedByDefault`, `TestSetupRecommendedIdempotent`, `TestSetupRecommendedDeclined`, `TestSetupExclusionOnlyTouchesNothingElse`). `watchdeck rules recommended --install` adds them outside setup (`TestRulesRecommendedInstall`). `watchdeck uninstall` removes the strings its ledger owns in the same step as the hooks, keeps the person's own, lists each string under `--dry-run`, and runs before `--purge` deletes the ledger (`TestUninstallRemovesOwnedNativeRules`, `TestUninstallLeavesUserStrings`, `TestUninstallDryRunListsOwned`, `TestUninstallPurgeAfterRemoval`). It cleans every settings file the ledger names, so a string added to a project's `.claude/settings.json` goes too, without `--project`, in a rules-only step (`TestUninstallRemovesOwnedInProjectFile`). When the ledger does not load, or a file it names is missing, unreadable, not a user or project settings file, or fails to write, `--purge` keeps the state dir and prints "NOT deleting the state dir: <N> rules watchdeck added could not be removed from <paths>; the ledger stays so you can retry"; the config and cache dirs still go (`TestUninstallPurgeRefusedWhenLedgerUnreadable`, `TestUninstallPurgeRefusedWhenProjectFileGone`). Without `--purge` the plan still names those files and says the rules and the ledger stay (`TestUninstallListsStuckRulesWithoutPurge`). A ledger entry no intent claims (a legacy entry with neither `rule_id` nor `rule_ids`) owns nothing: `Ledger.Owners` is nil for it, `rules list` shows its string as the person's, and `rules remove` and uninstall leave it (`TestOwnersNilForOwnerlessEntry`, `TestListOwnerlessLedgerEntryIsUser`). `watchdeck doctor` has a "native rules" line: how many of the twelve are installed and which strings watchdeck added that were since removed by hand (`TestDoctorNativeRulesDrift`).

### Propose and apply

`rulebook.Propose` turns one request into the writes that add it and reads and writes nothing; `Apply` writes them with one `Transact` per Claude Code settings file, and a separate edit of the overrides file (known limit 4), and reports each file and its backup. The mechanism is chosen by what the rule names (`TestProposePicksMechanism`):

| Request | Written as | Where |
|---|---|---|
| `--read`, `--edit`, `--path PATH` | `Read(p)`, `Edit(p)`, or both | Claude Code settings, user scope by default, `--project` for the project file |
| `--domain HOST` | `WebFetch(domain:HOST)` | same |
| `--mcp TOOL` | the bare `mcp__…` name | same |
| `--command PREFIX` | `Bash(PREFIX *)`; the hook text twin is suggested, or added with `--twin` | same; the twin in the user policy file |
| `--regex RE`, `--text TEXT` | a hook rule `user-<fnv32a hex8 of the value>`, Bash by default | the user policy file only |
| an MCP launch | a subject | the user policy file only |
| a skill | refused; a skill's sandbox is widened with `rules grant` | |

Path anchoring follows how Claude Code reads a settings file of that scope: an absolute path becomes `//abs`, a relative path `./rel` at user scope and `/rel` at project scope, a `~/` path stays as it is after cleaning. A value ending in `/` names a directory and gets `/**`: `secrets/` is `./secrets/**` (`/secrets/**` at project scope), `~/x/` is `~/x/**`, `/abs/` is `//abs/**`, and a lone `~/` is `~/**` (rows in `TestProposeAnchorsUserScopePaths`, `TestProposeProjectScopePaths`). A `--command` prefix is trimmed of surrounding blanks and refused when it holds `*`, since the rule already ends in ` *`; a `--domain` must be a bare host (no scheme, path, port or user; `policy.NormalizeHost` must give back the value itself) and is written lower-cased (rows in `TestProposeRefusesControlChars`, `TestProposeTrimsCommandAndHost`). A `..` left after cleaning, `.` alone, a `~` not followed by `/`, and control characters are refused, and so are `(` and `)` in any native value, since a parenthesis would end the settings string early (`TestProposeAnchorsUserScopePaths`, `TestProposeAnchorsUserScopeRefusals`, `TestProposeProjectScopePaths`, `TestProposeRefusesControlChars`, `TestProposeSuggestsBashTwin`). The local and managed files are never written (`TestProposeRefusesLocalAndManaged`, `TestApplyRefusesForeignFile`). Apply writes a backup before each write, is idempotent, and checks a deserialised plan before writing: a hook rule's values, a native string's value for `(` and `)` as `Propose` checks it (`TestApplyRefusesParens`), and a hook rule's id, recomputed from its match as `user-<fnv32a hex8>` rather than trusted (`TestApplyRecomputesHookRuleID`) (`TestApplyWritesNativeWithBackup`, `TestApplyRegexWritesOverrides`, `TestApplyTwice`, `TestApplyProjectScope`, `TestApplyChecksHookRuleValues`, `TestApplyMCPLaunchKeepsToolsAndRemoveSubject`). `Remove` takes away only a native string watchdeck owns (all of its claims when it is shared), clears a pack rule's override (since 2026-10-19; the pack rule itself stays, and `SetEnabled` switches it off), or deletes a hook rule or subject; a string the person wrote is `ErrNotOwned` (`TestRemoveNativeOwned`, `TestRemoveNativeSharedRemovesAllClaims`, `TestRemoveNativeUnownedErrors`, `TestRemovePackRuleClearsOverride`, `TestSetEnabledPackRule`, `TestRemoveUserRule`, `TestRemoveSkillSubject`). `watchdeck rules remove` on a pack row still switches the rule off, through `SetEnabled` (`TestRulesRemovePackTurnsOff`). `policy.Edit` gained `PutRule` and `DeleteRule` for the hook rules (`TestApplyPutRule`, `TestApplyDeleteRule`).

The CLI is `watchdeck rules list|add|remove|recommended|grant` (`grant` is `allow` under another name). Without a terminal and without `--yes`, `add` prints the plan and exits 1; a string watchdeck does not own is refused with exit 2 before any question. A user-scope `add` run inside a git project writes only the user file, and `--project` never writes `settings.local.json` (`TestRulesAddUserScopeInsideProject`, `TestRulesAddProjectScope`). The result says "nothing changed" only when `Apply` succeeded (`TestRulesAddFailureNotNothingChanged`). In a multi-key write, an edit that changes nothing creates no parent object (`TestManageKeysNoopLeavesBytes`) (`TestRulesAddBlockCommandWritesSettings`, `TestRulesAddNoTTYPrintsPlanExit1`, `TestRulesAddProjectScope`, `TestRulesRemoveOwned`, `TestRulesRemoveUnownedExit2`, `TestRulesGrantAliasesAllow`, `TestRulesUsageExit3`).

### The inbox

`rulebook.Open` builds the "Needs you" list in one pass over the activity log: auto-mode denials, the hook's gate denies and asks, sandbox refusals, and skills asking past their grant, plus refusals a caller read from transcripts. It starts at `installed_at` from `config.yaml`, else seven days back, also when `config.yaml` cannot be read or does not parse (`TestInboxFromDeniedAndDecisions`, `TestInboxOnlyAfterInstall`, `TestInboxWithoutInstallDateUsesWindow`, `TestInboxCorruptConfigUsesWindow`). Each item carries a suggested fix from `Suggest`: for a Bash item (a denial, an ask or a rejection), an ask on the program, never a block, because the program name alone says little about what the call was for; `watchdeck inbox` prints it and, on the next line, the path rule to use instead when a file was the target; for a file, web or MCP item, a deny on the path, host or tool (`TestSuggestTable`). `Resolve` appends `add_rule`, `acknowledge` or `dismiss` to `inbox.jsonl` in the state dir, 0600, opened `O_NOFOLLOW` so a symlink planted there is refused (`TestInboxResolveRefusesSymlink`); a resolved id leaves the list, a dismiss hides every item with the same signature, and an item whose fix is already an enabled native deny or ask string is closed (`TestInboxResolve0600`, `TestInboxItemClosesWhenRuleExists`). `watchdeck inbox` lists the open items and `inbox resolve <id> add|ack|dismiss` closes one; `add` prints the exact `rules add` line before the plan and the confirm, also with `--yes` (`TestInboxListsAndResolves`). With the activity log off, an empty inbox says "the activity log is off (WATCHDECK_ACTIVITY=off): only transcript rejections can be listed" (`TestInboxActivityOffSaysSo`). Since Plan 04 the dashboard's Home page shows the inbox as "Needs you" (section 10, "Home").

### File-tool hooks are conditional

Since Plan 03 Task 9 the settings install hooks only the file tools some rule needs, and a pack rule at its default action whose native twins are all installed no longer needs the hook. The mechanism, the scope reading, the pruning of our old group, the `doctor` line and the tests are in section 8, "Known limits", "Hooks on file tools". With default packs, `setup --yes` on a fresh home ends with the file-tools group `Write|Edit|MultiEdit|NotebookEdit`, Read unhooked (`TestSetupDefaultLeavesReadUnhooked`). The prompts this can lose are known limit 1 below.

### Smaller fixes

- A denied Bash command made only of `NAME=value` words, or of one word, kept its denial reason unredacted or got a doubled ellipsis; the reason now shows `…` for the command (`TestRedactReasonAssignmentOnly`, `TestRedactReasonOneWord`, `TestRedactReasonTrailingSpace`, `TestRedactReasonUnchangedForSkillCommand`).
- A wrapper option whose attached value is only an expansion (`timeout -s"$X" KILL 5 rm …`) is read both ways (section 8; rows in `TestMatchBashDocExamples` and `TestNativeRulesWrappersBehindExpansions`).
- `scripts/fixture-session.sh` also follows `parentToolUseID`.

### Fast path

`scripts/measure-hook.sh` with BEFORE at the Plan 02 close, darwin arm64, 200 spawns per payload, three runs, 2026-10-19; the machine was not idle. No-opinion payload, in ms:

| Run | Before median | After median | Before p95 | After p95 | Median delta | p95 delta |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | 5.04 | 5.06 | 5.36 | 5.44 | +0.02 | +0.08 |
| 2 | 5.09 | 5.14 | 5.38 | 5.93 | +0.05 | +0.55 |
| 3 | 4.93 | 5.07 | 5.24 | 5.80 | +0.14 | +0.56 |

All three runs are inside the acceptance (median within +1 ms, p95 within +2 ms), and no package of this module has init work in either binary. The stripped binary grew from 10,866,562 to 11,167,714 bytes (+301,152), the cost of linking `internal/rulebook` and the new commands. The measurement home has no Claude Code settings file.

### Known limits

1. **Read rules wider than their twins.** The secrets-read Read ask rules match other accounts' homes (every account's home on macOS, `/home/*/…` on Linux, and `/root`) while their native twins cover only `~/` (`internal/rules/packs/secrets-read.yaml`). Once Read is unhooked, a read of another account's key file is no longer asked about; such a file is usually unreadable anyway. Log-only pack rules on paths without a twin (gcloud, kube, docker and gh hosts files; `.zshenv` and fish config, `/Library/LaunchDaemons`, `/Library/LaunchAgents` and `/etc/systemd/system` when `cred-in-write` is off) stop producing records. No other prompt is known to be lost.
2. **Partial quotes survive redaction.** A classifier reason that quotes only a fragment of a denied command keeps that fragment, because the redaction replaces the whole command text only (`internal/hook/events.go`).
3. **Value reading over-matches.** `nice -n"$N" echo rm -rf x` and `stdbuf -o"$M" echo rm -rf x` match `Bash(rm *)` as `rm`. This is a deny too many, never too few; found in the Task 2 review, pinned by two rows in `TestMatchBashDocExamples` ("over-match: …").
4. **What `rules add` refuses and how it writes.** Native values holding `(` or `)`, a `~` not followed by `/`, any `..` segment, and control characters are refused (`TestProposeRefusesControlChars`, `TestProposeAnchorsUserScopeRefusals`). A trailing slash names a directory: `dir/` becomes `./dir/**` (since the final fix wave, 2026-10-19). A `--command` prefix holding `*` and a `--domain` that is not a bare host are refused. Hook rules (`--regex`, `--text`) get one id per value, so a second rule with the same text replaces the first. `Apply` is not atomic across the settings file and the overrides file; each file write is atomic and reported.
5. **Setup and uninstall.** A recommended rule the person removed by hand is honoured by every install path since the final fix wave of Plan 04 (2026-10-19): `setup`, `rules recommended --install` and the dashboard's Add all each run `rulebook.Reconcile` before computing the offer, so the removal is declined and the rule is not put back (`TestRecommendedInstallReconcilesFirst` in `cmd/watchdeck`, `TestRulesRecommendedInstallReconcilesFirst` in `internal/ui`). A reconcile that fails is printed, or returned as `warning`, and the install goes on, so in that case the rule can still come back. `--all` and an explicit Add of that row still add it, and take it off the declined list. The other way round: if the person deletes a rule watchdeck added and later types the same rule themselves, with no watchdeck write in between, watchdeck still counts it as its own until its next write, so `rules remove` or `uninstall` would take it. Run `watchdeck rules list` after a hand edit and remove the row first if you want to keep your copy. An install whose settings hold the older `Read(./.env)` spellings does not count the bare `Read(.env)` strings as installed, so setup and `rules recommended --install` add the bare ones beside them. `setup --file PATH` moves only the hooks; the recommended rules always go to the user settings file. Uninstall's confirm shows a count of rules to remove; `--dry-run` shows the strings.
6. **Platforms and JSON.** The ledger lock is a no-op on Windows (`internal/claudesettings/lock_other.go`). Managed settings paths were checked by table only on Linux. `rules list --json` serialises each file's error as `{}`, because the field is a Go `error`; the message is in `warnings`, and Plan 04 adds a view type.
7. **Plugin hooks are static.** The plugin's `hooks.json` still registers every file tool, whatever the rules need.

### The native deny, end to end (2026-10-19)

`scripts/prove-native-deny.sh` adds `Bash(echo native-deny-marker *)` with `watchdeck rules add` into a temp settings file, runs one `claude -p --permission-mode auto` against it, and checks that the session's one refused `tool_result` row carries `permissionDecision` `{reject, config, subcommandResults}` and `toolDenialKind` `permission-rule`. Run by the controller on 2026-10-19 against Claude Code 2.1.303 in auto mode: PASS — the rule `watchdeck rules add` wrote was honoured by Claude Code and the transcript row carried `reject/config/subcommandResults` with `toolDenialKind permission-rule` (scripts/prove-native-deny.sh). Since that run the script exits 130 on an interrupt after cleaning up, runs `claude -p` with the XDG variables unset, and prints the last 20 lines of Claude Code's stderr when the result has no session id; those changes were syntax-checked (`bash -n`), not rerun.

### Not verified yet (2026-10-19)

- **Bare dotenv names at any depth.** That `Read(.env)` matches a `.env` at any depth under the working directory rests on Claude Code's documentation (fetched 2026-10-19), not on a transcript run.
- **A person's "No" in the transcript.** Unchanged from section 8; interactive capture pending.
- **A hook ask in `dontAsk` and in an interactive `bypassPermissions` session.** Unchanged from section 8.
- Nothing in this section was run on Linux or Windows.

## 10. The surface (Plan 04, 2026-10-19)

Plan 04 put the Plan 02 and Plan 03 backends on screen: a dashboard of three tabs (Home, Sessions, Rules) and a Settings gear, the write routes behind them, the carry-forward Plan 03 left for the rulebook, a CLI cut to six documented commands, and a hook guard for watchdeck's own commands. This section says what was verified, with the test that shows it, and what was not. Run on darwin arm64 only. The dashboard's JavaScript has no committed runtime test: the Go tests below read the source text, `scripts/walk.sh` visits every route in Chromium, and each page task checked its behaviour by hand in Playwright against a fixture home on 2026-10-19. A browser behaviour named below without a test is one of those hand checks.

### Information architecture

- **Routes.** `#/` is Home; `#/sessions` the session list with the live lane map; `#/sessions/<id>` and `#/sessions/<id>/timeline` one session's map; `#/rules`, `#/rules/skill/<name>` (also `*`, written `%2A`) and `#/rules/mcp/<name>` the rules and their drill-ins; `#/settings` the gear. An unknown route lands on Home. The nav order, the brand link, the default route and the three registrations are pinned by `TestHomePageIsHome`; Sessions before Rules and the page barrel by `TestSessionsPageOwnsTheMap` and `TestRulesPageWritesThroughWriteJS`.
- **Redirects.** `LEGACY_ROUTES` in `app.js` maps the old first segment to the new hash before any page is looked up, with `history.replaceState`, once: `overview` → `#/`, `skills[/<name>]` → `#/rules[/skill/<name>]`, `grants` → `#/rules`, `enforce` → `#/settings`, and `flow[/topology|/timeline]/<id>` → `#/sessions/<id>[/timeline]`, keeping `n=` and turning `hide_none=1` into `a=` with every authority word but `none` (`TestLegacyRouteTable`). `scripts/walk.sh` loads each old hash in Chromium and fails when it does not move; its last run is under "Fast path" below.
- **Pages.** The Overview page's script and stylesheet are gone; `pages.js` is a barrel that imports the page modules and registers nothing; shared helpers live in `shared.js`, which imports only `app.js` and `copy.js`, so no page imports the barrel (`TestRulesPageWritesThroughWriteJS`). `/api/status` gained `live`, the count of live sessions, for the pill (`TestStatusLive`).

### Copy and the lint

- **One copy module.** Every on-screen string of the dashboard lives in `static/copy.js`: `COPY` per page plus the helpers `authorityLabel`, `authorityGlyph`, `protectionWord`, `protectionLabel`, `groupTitle`, `ownerLabel`, `enforcementLabel` and `scopeLabel`. Wire words appear there only as unquoted object keys, so the lint never sees them as text.
- **The lint.** `internal/copylint` scans string literals and template text of `.js`, text nodes and `title`, `aria-label` and `placeholder` values of `.html`, and every line outside fences of `.md` and `.txt`, for the banned words: no opinion, ungranted, subject, gate, matcher, profile hash, hook and hooks, PreToolUse, classifier, nonce, whole word and case-insensitive, with a phrase broken across a line still caught (`TestBannedWords`, `TestScanJS` with the cases in `testdata/tricky.js`, `TestScanJSHitFields`, `TestScanJSMultilineTemplate`, `TestScanHTML`, `TestScanMarkdown`, `TestScanUnknownExtension`). `TestCopyLint` runs it over every `static/*.js` and `static/index.html`; since Task 15 there is no exemption list. `TestUsageCopyLint` (`cmd/watchdeck`) runs it over the documented usage text and every plugin `SKILL.md`.
- **Authority labels.** Nine server words, eight labels: `deny_rule` Deny rule, `classifier` and `auto` Auto check, `allow_rule` Allow rule, `user` You, `hook` watchdeck, `sandbox` Sandbox, `blocked` Blocked, `none` No check. Each label has a glyph (shield, eye, check, person, box, circle), so who decided is never shown by colour alone. An unknown word reads as Blocked. Protection reads Protected, Partly protected or Not protected from `/api/status`.
- **Server text.** Map node text no longer carries a raw authority word or "no opinion"; an unchecked call reads "no check" (`TestNodeTextCarriesNoRawAuthorityWord`, the six map goldens in `TestGolden`).

### Routes

Every write route spends a one-use nonce of its own purpose, minted by its GET (`rules`, `needs`, `config`; a nonce of another purpose, the enforce one included, is a 403). Checks that need no state (body shape, unknown fields, the nonce being present, `?project=`) answer 400 before the spend, so the nonce stays usable. After the spend every answer, success or not, carries a fresh nonce. Errors map as: the settings lock held by another writer → 409 with a fixed sentence; a string watchdeck does not own (`ErrNotOwned`) → 409; `ErrScope` and `ErrValue` → 400; anything else → 500.

| Route | Shape and purpose | Tests |
|---|---|---|
| `GET /api/config`, `POST /api/config` | `{path, autostart, installed_at, nonce, nonce_ttl_seconds}`; the POST takes `{nonce, autostart}` and writes `config.yaml` through `userconfig` | `TestConfigGetShape`, `TestConfigPostWritesAutostart`, `TestConfigNonceOneUse`, `TestConfigPostValidation` |
| `GET /api/rules[?project=]` | the five groups, `recommended`, `drift`, `files` (a file's read error as a string), `warnings`, `project_root`, `home`, `updated`, a `rules` nonce | `TestRulesShapeAndNonce`, `TestRulesFilesErrorAsString` |
| `POST /api/rules/propose` | `{request: AddRequest}` → `{plan: {steps, suggested}, refused: ""}`; a request the rulebook refuses is a 200 with `plan: null` and the specific reason; writes nothing, takes no nonce | `TestProposeRoundTrip`, `TestProposeRefusedIs200` |
| `POST /api/rules` | exactly one of `add` (cleared `intent_id`, Propose, Apply), `remove` (row id; a subject entry row is refused, and removing a subject reports every row that went), `set` (pack rows only) | `TestRulesAddWritesNativeWithBackup`, `TestRulesRemoveNotOwned409`, `TestRulesSetPackRule` (`internal/ui`), `TestRulesWriteLockBusy409`, `TestRulesNonceOneUse`, `TestRulesExactlyOne`, `TestRulesRemoveSubjectEntryRefused`, `TestRulesRemoveSubjectReportsEveryRow`, `TestRemoveRefusedRows` |
| `POST /api/rules/recommended` | `{action: install\|decline\|undecline, ids?}`; install with no ids is the offer (declined rules skipped), with ids installs only missing rules and takes them off the declined list; every install first runs `rulebook.Reconcile` and, if that fails, still installs and adds an optional `warning` string to the answer, which the Rules page shows as a toast since Plan 05 (`TestRulesShowsRecommendedWarning`) | `TestRecommendedInstallSkipsDeclined`, `TestRulesRecommendedInstallReconcilesFirst` |
| `GET /api/needs[?project=]` | open inbox items, each with `fixes[]` (`request`, `sentence`, `mechanism`, `file`, `index` into the item's raw `fix` list), `count`, `since`, `activity_enabled`, a `needs` nonce | `TestNeedsShapeFromDenied`, `TestNeedsWithBrokenSettings`, `TestNeedsActivityOff`, `TestNeedsFixIndex` |
| `POST /api/needs/{id}` | `{action: add_rule\|acknowledge\|dismiss\|undo, fix?}`; resolutions run under the settings lock after Apply; undo of nothing is a 404 (`rulebook.ErrNothingToUndo`) | `TestNeedsAddRuleClosesItem`, `TestNeedsAddRuleFixBounds`, `TestNeedsDismissSuppressesSignature`, `TestNeedsUndoReopens`, `TestNeedsUnknown404` |
| `GET /api/recent?limit=N[&project=]` | up to 50 recent decisions (asks, denies, rewrites, auto-mode denials, sandbox refusals) as `{ts, session_id, tool_use_id, agent_type, tool, what, outcome, authority, skill, project}`; never the command, the reason or the tool input | `TestRecentShapeAndOrder`, `TestRecentNeverCarriesCommand`, `TestRecentLimitBounds`, `TestRecentProjectFilter` |
| `GET /api/mcp` | the user MCP config path and its servers as `{name, stdio, wrapped, type, url}`; the URL loses userinfo, query and fragment; command, args and env never leave | `TestMCPListShape`, `TestMCPAbsentAndBroken` |
| `GET /api/sessions/{id}/map?authority=a,b` | see "The session map" | `TestMapAuthorityParam` |

`TestRulesWriteLockBusy409` holds `<stateDir>/claude-settings.lock` with `flock` on its own descriptor; the add answers 409 with a nonce and writes nothing, and a retry with that nonce succeeds after the release. `TestRecentNeverCarriesCommand` puts private text in a skill decision's command, a denied record's tool input and its reason, and checks none of it, nor the keys `command`, `reason` and `tool_input`, reaches the JSON.

### Home

Home opens with one sentence from `protectionWord` and two counts: "not installed in Claude Code yet" with Turn it on (offered only when no watchdeck registration is present and a sandbox backend exists), "watching but not enforcing: finish setup" with a link to Settings, or "watching N live sessions. M things need you." Turn it on uses the Settings install dialog through `write.js`. Then three sections:

- **Needs you** lists `/api/needs` items with who decided, the pack rules that fired, the reason and the first fix's sentence. Add rule (the first fix, or another from a menu) goes through `writeWithConfirm`; Acknowledge and Dismiss post directly (until 2026-10-28, when the row became one sentence and one button and the writes moved into a decision sheet, section 16). A resolved row fades, then an 8 s toast offers Undo; the toast takes focus and pauses while hovered or focused.
- **Live now** shows one card per live session with its last denial or last call.
- **Recent decisions** lists `/api/recent`, each row a link into the session's timeline at that call.

Tests: `TestHomePageIsHome` (the routes and helpers home.js uses, no import of the barrel) and `TestHomeFixRound1` (the fade waits for the row's own transition, the toast's focus and pause handlers, one install at a time, `canInstall`). Both read source text; the fade timing, focus, Undo and the install dialog were checked by hand. `scripts/fixture-home.sh` appends two records stamped at run time, an auto-mode denial and a pack-rule ask, so the fixture always has something in Needs you.

### Sessions and the live map

The Sessions list keeps its table, filters and search; every string moved into `COPY.sessions`. Above the cards, `mountLiveMap` draws one lane per live session from `GET /api/sessions?state=live&limit=12`: the last 40 calls as glyphs placed by the clock over ten minutes, filled by outcome and marked with who decided, a header with the project, agents and the catalog row's last call, and a link to the session's map. Each lane polls `?view=ticks` with `If-None-Match` through `api.fetch`, every 2 s for the first six lanes and 15 s beyond; a hidden page stops polling; a failure backs off 15 s; a 401 stops it. With nothing live the strip says when the last session ended. Tests: `TestLiveMapPollsThroughApi` (`api.fetch`, `If-None-Match`, no `EventSource`, no bare `fetch`) and `TestLiveMapTimeFromRowAndClock` (the header reads `last_ts`, glyphs use `Date.now()`, the 401 path exists); cadence, focus and back-off were checked by hand. `walk.mjs` ignores the `ERR_ABORTED` Chromium reports after a 304 on a `no-store` fetch.

### The session map

- **Server.** `mapmodel.Options.Authorities` keeps only the calls whose authority is listed; spawns always stay. `Node.Authority` is set on every event node built from a call and on a topology tool or MCP node whose drawn calls all agree. `Graph.Shown` counts the calls drawn after both filters, spawns included; ticks and totals stay the whole session. `HideNone` stays a policy filter beside it, so the old hide-no-opinion view is not an authority set (`TestNodeAuthorityOnEventNodes`, `TestAuthorityFilterDropsCalls`, `TestShownUnderHideNone`, `TestIsAuthority`, `TestAllAuthoritiesIsNoFilter`).
- **Route.** `?authority=` takes a comma list of the nine words; an unknown word, a wrong case or an empty piece is a 400 naming the nine; the set is sorted and deduplicated, so equal sets share an ETag, and the ETag differs from the unfiltered map and from `hide_none=1`. `buildVersion` is `mapapi-3` and the ticks body is `{session, ticks, totals, shown, updated}` (`TestMapAuthorityParam`, `TestTicksView`).
- **Page.** `map.js` exports `mapView`, mounted by `sessions.js` under `#/sessions/<id>`; gestures, zoom, the minimap and replay moved into `canvas.js` verbatim (`TestSessionsPageOwnsTheMap`). Eight "Decided by" chips, one per label, start on and live in the link as `a=`, written only when fewer than nine words are on; an unknown word in `a=` is dropped and an empty list means all, so an old link never gets a 400; the last chip on cannot be turned off. Totals add a count per label and, while filtered, how many calls are shown. Ticks, cards and timeline rows name who decided in words.

### Rules

- **Groups.** The five rulebook groups, strongest first: Sandbox (the OS enforces), Blocked before Claude decides, Auto check (Claude's own rules), watchdeck checks, Ask (you decide each time). Each row shows its sentence, scope, who added it, what enforces it, its state and its file with home as `~`.
- **Controls per kind.** A native or hook rule watchdeck added: Remove. A built-in pack rule: an on/off switch. A subject's first row: Remove, which takes every entry; its entry rows have no control. Rows the server would refuse (`subject:skill:*`, project-scope subject and hook rows, auto-mode, sandbox domain and exclusion rows, native strings someone else wrote) show "Not changed here: edit <file> by hand." The `$defaults` auto-mode row shows a fixed sentence instead of the list.
- **Recommended** rows have Add and Decline; Add all installs the offer; declined rows fold under a count with Add and Bring back.
- **Sandbox join.** The page adds a row for each skill with scripts and each wrapped MCP server that no rulebook row links to, from `/api/skills` and `/api/mcp`, on the client. A row id renders once.
- **Drill-ins.** `#/rules/skill/<name>` mounts the old skill detail (request, grant, runs-under, grant controls through `shared.js`), and `*` loads the defaults from `/api/overrides`. `#/rules/mcp/<name>` is read-only and prints the `watchdeck advanced mcp wrap|unwrap` line.
- **The sheet.** Add rule opens a modal dialog with six kinds (block or ask before a command, keep a path private, block a domain, block an MCP tool, raise a watchdeck rule to deny) and a scope choice. 250 ms after a change it posts the request to `/api/rules/propose` and shows the plan's sentences, file, key and what enforces each; a refusal shows under the field; the twin checkbox appears when the server suggests one. Confirm sends `{add: request}` through `writeWithConfirm`; the page never sends a plan back (`TestRuleSheetProposesServerSide`: the preview path is fixed in `shared.js`, one `writeWithConfirm`, no `steps:` key, no bare `fetch` or `api.post` in `rules.js`). Focus trap, focus return and the live regions were checked by hand. After a Remove (confirmed, whether it succeeded or failed) the list is rebuilt, so focus moves to the heading of the group the row lived in, or the page heading when that group is gone, never the body (final fix wave, 2026-10-19; `TestRulesRemoveMovesFocus` is a source assertion, since no committed test runs the page's JavaScript).
- `TestRulesPageWritesThroughWriteJS` pins the write path and the import rules; `TestSettingsPageWritesThroughWriteJS` pins the shared helpers.

### Settings

The gear page shows the Claude Code install for the user settings and, when present, the project settings in plain words, with Install and Uninstall through `write.js` (backup line in the dialog), then the dashboard: autostart (`/api/config`), Remember me and the theme. `write.js` retries once on a 403 after refetching the nonce and returns `cancelled()` on a declined dialog; it never throws (`TestSettingsPageWritesThroughWriteJS` reads the source; the retry and cancel paths were checked by hand).

### Carry-forward landed

The items Plan 03 left for this plan (section 9's spec "Carry-forward from Plan 03"):

- **Rule ids on inbox items.** `rule_ids` names each pack rule that fired; for an ask or a denial the first fix raises the first built-in pack rule to deny, then the usual suggestion follows (`TestItemCarriesRuleIDs`, `TestSuggestPrefersPackRuleDeny`, `TestInboxPackRuleFix`).
- **Pack-rule overrides.** A `pack-rule` request proposes `rule_overrides` in the user overrides file; `Apply` re-checks the value; `Remove` of a pack row clears the override; `SetEnabled` switches a pack rule and keeps its action (`TestProposePackRuleOverride`, `TestApplyPackRuleWritesOverride`, `TestRemovePackRuleClearsOverride`, `TestSetEnabledPackRule`, `TestSetEnabledRefusesOtherRows`, `TestRulesRemovePackTurnsOff`).
- **Declined list.** `recommended-declined.json` in the state dir, 0600, written under the settings lock; `List` marks declined rows; `RecommendedOffer` skips them; `Apply` takes what it writes off the list; an unreadable file never blocks an install or a listing (`TestDeclinedRoundTrip0600`, `TestListMarksDeclined`, `TestRecommendedOfferSkipsDeclined`, `TestDeclineTakesLock`, `TestLockedRunsUnderLock`, `TestApplyUndeclinesRecommended`, `TestLoadDeclinedCorruptNamesFile`, `TestListWarnsOnCorruptDeclined`, `TestApplyIgnoresCorruptDeclined`, `TestDeclineOverwritesCorruptFile`).
- **Reconcile.** `rulebook.Reconcile` drops ledger claims for strings removed by hand and declines a recommended one; `watchdeck rules reconcile` prints both, and `uninstall` reconciles first (`TestReconcileDropsAndDeclines`, `TestReconcileNonRecommendedNotDeclined`, `TestRulesReconcilePrints`, `TestReconcileDeclinesHandRemoved`, `TestUninstallReconcilesFirst`). `setup`, `rules recommended --install` and the dashboard's Add all reconcile before they compute the offer, so a rule removed by hand is not put back (`TestRecommendedInstallReconcilesFirst`, `TestRulesRecommendedInstallReconcilesFirst`; final fix wave, 2026-10-19). Setup's No declines what it offered, and `--yes` honours the list (`TestSetupNoDeclinesAll`, `TestSetupYesHonoursDeclined`, `TestRulesRecommendedDecline`, `TestRulesRecommendedUndecline`, `TestRecommendedInstallWarnsOnCorruptDeclined`).
- **Undo.** A resolution can be undone; undo of an add removes every row the add wrote, inside one transaction (`TestUndoReopensItem`, `TestUndoAddRuleRemovesRule`, `TestUndoRemovesEveryRow`, `TestUndoReadsOldRuleIDLine`, `TestUndoNothing`, `TestInboxResolveUndo`).
- **A view type for a file's error.** `/api/rules` sends `files[].error` as text (`TestRulesFilesErrorAsString`). `rules list --json` still prints `{}` (section 9, known limit 6).
- **POST a request, not a plan.** Both write paths take an `AddRequest` and propose server-side (`TestRuleSheetProposesServerSide`, `TestRulesAddWritesNativeWithBackup`).

### CLI

`watchdeck help` documents six commands: `watchdeck` (on a terminal, the same as `ui --ensure --open`; without one, the usage and exit 3), `setup`, `status`, `rules`, `doctor` and `uninstall`. `watchdeck help advanced` lists the rest, which run as `watchdeck advanced <command>` or bare with the same output and exit codes; `hook` and `run` are not listed but still dispatched, since the rewrite and the sandbox exclusion depend on their exact argv (`TestUsageListsSix`, `TestBareNoTTYUsageExit3`, `TestAdvancedDispatch`, `TestHelpAdvanced`, `TestHookAndRunStillAccepted`). `watchdeck status` prints protection, live and idle sessions, what needs you, whether the dashboard runs and the doctor's checks with one line per warning or failure; `--json` has exactly eight keys; it exits 1 on a failed check unless `--exit-zero`; with the plugin, the sandbox exclusion alone reads Protected (`TestStatusText`, `TestStatusOffSources`, `TestStatusJSON`, `TestStatusExitOnDoctorFail`, `TestStatusPluginProtected`, `TestStatusPluginNoExclusion`). `/watchdeck:status` runs `watchdeck status --plugin --exit-zero`. New forms: `rules set <id> on|off` (`TestRulesSetPackRule` in `cmd/watchdeck`), `rules recommended --decline|--undecline|--all`, `rules reconcile` and `inbox resolve <id> undo`. The bare `watchdeck` terminal branch has no Go test; it was run by hand on 2026-10-19.

### The hook and watchdeck's own commands

A watchdeck command the model types in a Bash call that may change watchdeck's or Claude Code's settings always asks, with the reason "`watchdeck <sub>` changes watchdeck or Claude Code settings; it always asks, so a change is never made without the person seeing it" and no rewrite. Before 2026-10-19 only `watchdeck allow` asked, so `watchdeck rules remove … --yes` or `watchdeck uninstall --yes` ran with no prompt. Read-only commands keep the no-opinion answer: `version`, `paths`, `status`, `doctor`, `audit`, `validate`, `sessions`, `activity`, `help`, `index`, `demo`, the flags `--help`, `-h`, `--version` and `-v`, the pairs `rules list`, `mcp status` and `policy check`, and `inbox` with no argument or a flag first and no `resolve` later in the command. Every other word after the name asks, including `advanced <sub>` (read as `<sub>`), `init` and `hook`. The spelling tolerance: single, double, `$'…'` and `$"…"` quotes and backslashes are stripped; ASCII case is folded, and U+212A KELVIN SIGN is read as `k`, since the default case-insensitive APFS volume runs `WATCHDECK` and `watchdecK` as the binary (the run guard folds both too); any word ending in the name counts, also inside `$(…)` or backticks; any spacing; inside a compound. Every mention is checked, so `watchdeck status && watchdeck rules reconcile` asks. Some commands ask with the reason "a watchdeck command …" because the subcommand cannot be read. That covers the name glued to a redirection, an expansion or a list (`watchdeck>/dev/null rules …`, `watchdeck&>log …`, `watchdeck${IFS}rules`, `{watchdeck,rules,reconcile}`, a script's `["watchdeck", "rules"]`), a non-name word where the subcommand goes (`watchdeck $(cat args)`, `watchdeck >x rules …`), and any command that also holds `xargs` or `parallel` anywhere (case folded, quotes stripped), whatever the word boundaries (`…|xargs watchdeck inbox`, `gxargs`, `parallel -N3 watchdeck inbox`); a word walk around them was evaded twice, so this is a substring rule (ruled 2026-10-19). Since the final fix wave of Plan 04 (2026-10-19), any command that names the binary and holds a `$` or a backtick anywhere also asks with that reason, once the word walk has found nothing to report: the name held in a variable (`a=watchdeck; $a rules reconcile`, `w=$(which watchdeck); $w uninstall --yes`, `read w <<< watchdeck; $w setup --yes`, `printf -v w watchdeck; …`, `export W=watchdeck; $W …`), the bash-only splits (`IFS=:; $a`, `${a//:/ }`) and `a=watchdeck; $a run …`, which the run guard does not see because no word ends in the name before `run`. The hook does not know the shell, so the bash-only forms are pinned as well. A backslash anywhere in the original text of a command that names the binary asks the same way (re-review of the fix wave, 2026-10-19): the folded text has every backslash stripped, so `printf 'watchdeck\tuninstall --yes' | sh`, `printf 'watchdeck\x20uninstall --yes' | sh`, `printf '%b' 'watchdeck\040uninstall --yes' | sh` and `echo -e 'watchdeck\tuninstall --yes' | sh` read as one word, and `printf` or `echo -e` turn the escape into a separator at run time; each of the four ran a fake binary in bash and zsh. The walk runs first, so `$(which watchdeck) rules …` still reports `rules`. This was the review's Major: every form above ran a fake binary on PATH in bash, and all but the four split forms in zsh. Tests: `TestModelTypedMutatingCommandsAsk`, `TestModelTypedReadOnlyCommandsPass` and `TestModelTypedRunAnyCaseAsks` (`internal/hook/mutatingguard_test.go`), and `TestModelTypedAllowAsks`. Known limits: what still passes is a `$'\xNN'` or other escape inside `$'…'` (`watch$'\x64'eck`; decoding through the shell parser is a follow-up), a glob that expands to the binary (`~/.local/bin/watchdec?`, `watch[d]eck`), a symlink or alias under another name, a name built at run time (`x=watch; ${x}deck rules remove`, or the name read from a file), and a command whose words are produced at run time by another program (`tr`, `sed`, `base64`, `rev`, a `printf` escape), which is not caught even when `watchdeck` appears in its text (`echo watchdeck_uninstall_--yes | tr _ ' ' | sh`, pinned as a documented miss in `TestModelTypedReadOnlyCommandsPass`): the guard reads the words as typed and asks only about the text Claude Code hands it. For these, the person's Claude Code permission prompt for the Bash call is the remaining check. The accepted false positives are any word ending in the name followed by an ordinary word (`echo watchdeck is great`), the name followed by `,` in prose, a read-only command next to `xargs` or `parallel` (`watchdeck status | xargs echo`), and any read-only command that names the binary and holds an expansion or a backslash (`watchdeck rules list > "$HOME/x"`, `grep 'watchdeck\b' notes`, `ls $HOME/.config/watchdeck`, and in a session that develops this repo `go build -o $BIN ./cmd/watchdeck`); each asks, none denies.

### Fast path

`scripts/measure-hook.sh` with BEFORE at the Plan 03 close and HEAD at the Task 14 merge, darwin arm64, 200 spawns per payload, three runs, 2026-10-19; the machine was not idle (run 2's BEFORE had an 18.83 ms maximum). No-opinion payload, in ms:

| Run | Before median | After median | Before p95 | After p95 | Median delta | p95 delta |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | 5.74 | 5.77 | 6.94 | 6.95 | +0.03 | +0.01 |
| 2 | 6.19 | 5.90 | 8.12 | 6.76 | -0.29 | -1.36 |
| 3 | 5.77 | 5.80 | 6.27 | 6.31 | +0.03 | +0.04 |

All three runs are inside the acceptance (median within +1 ms, p95 within +2 ms), and no package of this module has init work in either binary. The stripped binary grew from 11,184,498 to 11,451,634 bytes (+267,136). The measurement was taken at the Task 14 merge and not rerun. Three docs-only commits landed after it (the Home install-button refinement, the copy-lint closure with one line of `app.css`, and the GUIDE pass). The two static files are embedded in the binary, a few KB that do no init work and that the hook's no-opinion path never reads, so the table is expected to stand, but that is an argument, not a measurement. The final fix wave (2026-10-19) changed `internal/hook/runguard.go` too; its new check runs only when a command names the binary, which the no-opinion payload does not. The wave also changed dashboard and CLI code that is linked in but not on the hook path (`internal/ui/api_enforce.go`, `api_rules.go`, `static/rules.js`, `cmd/watchdeck/rulescmd.go`, `setup.go`); the init-free guards pass. None of it was measured.

The gates on the Task 15 branch, 2026-10-19: `go vet ./...` and `go test ./...` (38 packages ok), `scripts/test-install.sh` PASS, `scripts/walk.sh` PASS (17 rows: nine current routes, eight old hashes that redirect), `claude plugin validate plugin --strict` passed and `claude plugin test plugin` 50 pass, 0 fail.

### Known limits

1. **Recent is the activity log only.** A call a native deny rule refused shows in the session map, from the transcript, but not in Home's Recent decisions. A `denied` record for a Bash command that touches a skill keeps Claude Code's reason verbatim, so a Needs you item's reason can quote that skill command.
2. **The MCP drill-in is read-only.** Wrapping and unwrapping stay in the CLI; the page prints the line.
3. **`$defaults` is read-only.** The auto-mode defaults row shows a fixed sentence; it has no control.
4. **A row id renders once.** When two groups carry the same row id, only the first is drawn.
5. **The walkthrough is manual.** `scripts/walk.sh` needs Playwright and Chromium and never runs in CI; its failure path was not exercised by a real failing page. No committed test runs the dashboard's JavaScript.
6. **`set` exists only for pack rules.** Every other row is removed, not switched (`TestSetEnabledRefusesOtherRows`).
7. **Undo of an add removes the rule only while watchdeck owns it.** A string the person changed by hand stops the undo with `ErrNotOwned`. Undo is a stack: a second Undo cancels the resolution before it. Undo of a pack row clears the whole override, not only the action the add set. An ack or dismiss undo rewrites the ledger. A partial undo cannot be rerun. `Resolve` itself takes no lock, so a resolve between Undo's read and its append can be popped. Through the dashboard, add rule then resolve is two steps: if the resolve fails after the rule is written, the item closes on the next load but has nothing to undo.
8. **Rulebook edges.** A Book hides later denials of a pack rule raised to deny. A stale override of a pack rule a later build dropped cannot be cleared from the CLI. `Decline` overwrites an unreadable declined file while `Reconcile` refuses it.
9. **Write routes.** The recommended install takes the settings lock twice in a row (Reconcile, then Apply), so under contention it can wait up to twice the 2 s below. `overrides.yaml` edits (set, removing pack, hook or subject rows) take the server's mutex only, not the settings lock. The recommended install ignores a settings read error until Apply meets it. A partial Apply success is a bare 500. A lock wait holds other dashboard routes up to 2 s. `/api/needs` computes `since` with the server's clock, runs one Propose per fix with no item cap, and its `?project=` changes only the fixes.
10. **The map filter.** Runs no call claims ignore the authority filter. `shown` undercounts an all-none Bash node under the legacy `hide_none`. A repeated `authority=` uses the first value. No fixture has a call watchdeck's own check decided, so the `hook` filter is tested only on an empty result (`TestAuthorityFilterDropsCalls`).
11. **The live map.** Glyphs in a burst overlap, and only the top tooltip is reachable. The first six lanes keep 2 s while the list is expanded. The lane list refreshes every 15 s only while the event stream is down. The "nothing live" sentence can lag one refresh.
12. **The copy lint.** It misses a postfix `++` followed by `/` and a string, a regex after `if (…)`, plurals of banned words other than hooks, and escaped or split spellings. `groupTitle` echoes an unknown group id. Text the server sends is outside it: doctor messages shown by `status` and `/watchdeck:status` (for example "no hooks registered"), map labels, and a few English strings left in `canvas.js` and `topology.js`.
13. **CLI.** `--yes` with `--decline` is accepted silently. `rules set … on` on a rule already on prints "switched on". `status` runs the full doctor plus up to 2 s for the dashboard probe.
14. **Settings.** Saving autostart drops comments from `config.yaml` and writes no backup. A broken project `settings.json` makes `/api/skills` answer 500, so the skill drill-in shows that error.
15. **The command guard** has the limits in "The hook and watchdeck's own commands" above. Its fail-closed rule for expansions asks on read-only commands that name the binary and hold a `$` or a backtick (`watchdeck rules list > "$HOME/x"`, pinned in `TestModelTypedMutatingCommandsAsk`); in a session that develops this repo, `go build -o $BIN ./cmd/watchdeck` and the like ask once each.

### Not verified yet (2026-10-19)

- **Safari.** Every browser check ran in Chromium.
- **A keyboard pass and an axe run** over the whole dashboard: run since Plan 05, axe in both colour schemes (section 11, "The walkthrough").
- **The `hook` authority filter on a real capture** (known limit 10).
- **A person's "No" in the transcript**, and a hook ask in `dontAsk` and interactive `bypassPermissions`: unchanged from section 9.
- Nothing in this section was run on Linux or Windows.

## 11. Fixtures, the walkthrough and the screenshots (Plan 05, 2026-10-20)

Plan 05 made the dashboard demonstrable without a real `~/.claude`. It added a fixture home with hundreds of sessions, a walkthrough that also runs a keyboard pass and axe in both colour schemes, and five screenshots and a hero GIF taken from that fixture. It also landed the product fixes the screen review found. This section says what was verified and what was not. Each check names the test, the walk table or the provenance entry that shows it. Run on darwin arm64 only. The tests named below for shell scripts, Node scripts and the dashboard's JavaScript read source text; their behaviour rests on the walk.

### Fixture home

- **Flags.** `scripts/fixture-home.sh [OUTDIR] [--many N] [--live K] [--installed] [--seed S] [--now T]`. Without OUTDIR it builds under a fresh `/var/tmp/watchdeck-fixture-*` dir. With no flags it builds the earlier home plus session C. `--now` (the clock by default) is resolved once and passed to every block, so one home holds one instant. `--seed` defaults to 1.
- **The generator.** `scripts/fixture-sessions.py` is stdlib only and runs as `python3 -I`. It writes N synthetic ended sessions in the 30 days before `--now`, round-robin over nine `/Users/you/work/*` projects. Each session has 1 to 3 typed prompts and 3 to 12 tool calls (Read, Grep, Bash, Edit, WebFetch, Skill), and every fifth one spawns an Explore subagent with its own file and `.meta.json`. The results use the five shapes the transcript reader knows: an auto check accept, a person's yes, an allow rule, a deny-rule refusal with `toolDenialKind permission-rule`, and an auto-mode block with `automode-blocked`. One session in four gets activity records joined by `tool_use_id`, cycling ask, auto-mode denial, ask, WebFetch domain deny. One `random.Random(seed)` drives every choice. The activity and lifecycle logs are rewritten sorted by `ts`, mode 0600, under 0700 state dirs.
- **The seven pack rules.** Synthetic asks and denials draw from seven real built-in rules, each with its program and target: `pipe-to-shell/curl-pipe-shell`, `pipe-to-shell/shell-process-substitution`, `git-force-main/force-push-main`, `destructive/rm-root-or-home`, `destructive/dd-to-device`, `exfil/scp-secret` and `secrets-read/aws-credentials`. Each command was checked once against the Go matcher (`rules.Eval`, in a throwaway test since removed) to fire exactly its rule. The committed check is that a home holds at least three distinct rule ids and three distinct programs (`TestFixtureActivityVariety`). The scp and aws commands name `~/.ssh/id_ed25519` and `~/.aws/credentials` as text only; nothing reads those files.
- **Live copies.** `--live K` copies session A, the canary capture, K times under ids `a1000000-0000-4000-8000-0000000000NN` (NN in hex). Copy k moves to its own project, `/Users/you/work/api`, `web`, `infra` and on, with every `/Users/you/proj` in its rows rewritten. A's 25 seconds are stretched over 5, 4 or 3 minutes, and the last row lands 1, 3 and 6 minutes (then 9, 12 and on) before `--now`. Tool use ids are rewritten as `toolu_L<NN>…` at the same length, because the activity join assumes an id is unique across the log; with A's ids kept, A's records swallowed the copies' run records. The copies do not carry the two Needs records the script stamps for A. A live marker stays live for ten minutes after the build, so screenshots must follow it.
- **Session C.** The real deny capture, `internal/transcript/testdata/e2e-deny`, is placed into every home as an ended session in `/Users/you/proj`. Its start and end records in `sessions.jsonl` bracket the capture. It has no activity records (ruled 2026-10-20): its authority words come from the transcript only. Fetched straight from the API, its map needs `?project=/Users/you/proj`; the page sends it.
- **`--installed`.** The script copies `$WATCHDECK_BIN`, or builds the binary, into `OUTDIR/bin`, then runs `rules recommended --install --yes` and `enforce install` against the fixture home. Rules go first, so Read stays unhooked. `$WATCHDECK_BIN` is made absolute before any `cd` and refused with exit 3 unless it is an executable file. The fixture's settings name only paths under OUTDIR (`TestFixtureHomeInstalled`). Doctor in such a home reads "all 6 hooks" and "12 of 12 recommended rules installed", and `status` reads Protected (by hand, 2026-10-20).
- **Tests.** All five skip without `bash` or `python3` and build under `/var/tmp`.
  - `TestFixtureHomeMany` builds `--many 40 --live 3`. It finds 46 sessions, including A, B, C and the three copies, over nine project names. Four are live, with distinct projects and last-call times, and each copy shows 2 decisions, 1 denial and 1 ask. 42 are ended, and at least one synthetic session has a denial.
  - `TestFixtureHomeDeterministic` builds twice with the same seed, `--now` and OUTDIR. `diff -r` over `projects/`, the activity log and the lifecycle log is empty.
  - `TestFixtureSessionsNoRealData` walks every file of a `--many 60 --live 2` home. It fails on the login name, the real home, any `/Users/<x>` other than `/Users/you`, and the strings `sk-`, `AKIA`, `ghp_` and `-----BEGIN`.
  - `TestFixtureHomeInstalled` and `TestFixtureActivityVariety` are described above.
- **Timings.** Darwin arm64, binary prebuilt, machine not idle, 2026-10-20:

  | Measure | Result |
  |---|---|
  | `fixture-home.sh --many 400 --live 3` build | 0.92 s (projects tree 5.8 MB); 0.85 s in the review |
  | Cold session index, `advanced index sessions --rebuild` (406 sessions, 0 errors) | 0.11 s; 0.36 s in the review |
  | Dashboard launch to `/api/sessions` reporting 406 sessions, index removed first | 0.32 s |

### The walkthrough

- **Modes.** `scripts/walk.sh` passes the fixture flags to `fixture-home.sh` and takes `--keys`, `--axe` and `--screenshots DIR` with repeated `--shot NAME=ROUTE[@dark][#STATE]`. The states are `confirm-open`, `chips-filtered` and `fit`, and `<A>` and `<C>` stand for the two captured sessions; a misordered or unknown suffix exits 2. The route walk runs first, with axe on each route, then the screenshots, each in a fresh browser context, then the keyboard pass, last because it changes the fixture. Any failure in any mode exits 1; a setup problem exits 2. Console errors, page errors, API answers of 400 or more and failed requests are recorded in every mode, including the keyboard pass and each shot context. The cleanup trap is set before the build, so a failed build or fixture leaves no temp dir.
- **The keyboard pass.** `scripts/walk/keys.mjs` holds the script as data: 34 steps over Home, Rules, Sessions and Settings. The steps cover:
  - the skip link, the brand, the three tabs and the gear, in order;
  - Home's Add rule, which opens the confirm with focus on Cancel; Escape returns focus to the same button;
  - Dismiss, which gives the Undo toast focus; Escape then lands on the Needs heading;
  - Rules' Add rule sheet, which takes focus and gives it back;
  - a pack switch, where Space asks first (by design since Plan 04); Escape leaves the switch unchanged with focus on it, and a confirmed toggle and toggle back keep focus on the same switch;
  - a live lane that opens its session, a map node that Enter selects and Escape clears;
  - Settings' Install or Uninstall (whichever is enabled) and the autostart switch.

  Each step polls for up to 2 s, longer where a write or a route change sits in between. The first failing step ends its route and the rest print as not run. An empty script or a route with no steps fails (`TestWalkKeysNotVacuous`).
- **Axe.** `@axe-core/playwright` runs the `wcag2a` and `wcag2aa` tags on every route twice: light, then dark through `emulateMedia`. A body-background check proves dark rendered. Serious or critical findings fail the run, and so does a run that evaluated no rule (`TestWalkAxeAssertsRuleCount`). The first run on 2026-10-20, light only, failed 13 of 16 routes on three small-text contrast pairs. All three were fixed at the token, and `TestThemeContrastPairs` computes each ratio from `app.css`:

  | Pair | Old | New | Ratio now |
  |---|---|---|---|
  | Light link text (`--accent`) on the page | #0E7C86, 4.39:1 | #0D7882 | 4.64:1 |
  | Canvas axis and lane labels (`--cv-dim`) on the canvas | #6B7A90, 3.96:1 | #7D8CA2 | 5.06:1 |
  | Settings command hint on white | `--muted` at 85% opacity, 4.35:1 | `--muted` | 6.19:1 |

  The dark `--accent` stays #0E7C86 and is now set in both dark blocks. Since the fix, every route has been clean in both schemes on every run (32 route-scheme runs, 0 violations). Axe's "needs review" items, 2 on the map routes and 1 on Settings, are counted as incomplete and are not failures.
- **The token.** Playwright's navigation errors repeat the launch URL, `#t=<token>` included. `redact()` rewrites the token and any `t=…` in every stored or printed error and table cell, and a failed launch exits 2 with the redacted text (`TestWalkRedactsToken`). Every `.screenshot(` call has the guard that the hash holds no `t=` within the three lines above it (`TestWalkCaptureGuardsToken`).
- **The workflow.** `.github/workflows/walk.yml` is `workflow_dispatch` only, with inputs `many` "400" and `live` "3" passed through `env`. It runs with `permissions: contents: read` on ubuntu-24.04 with a 30-minute limit. It calls `scripts/walk.sh --many "$MANY" --live "$LIVE" --installed --keys --axe --screenshots out` and uploads `out/` with `if: always()`. It never appears in `ci.yml`. The actions are pinned by commit SHA, each checked against its tag with `gh api` on 2026-10-19; newer tags under two weeks old were passed over:

  | Action | Tag | Commit |
  |---|---|---|
  | actions/checkout | v7.0.1 | 3d3c42e5aac5ba805825da76410c181273ba90b1 |
  | actions/setup-go | v7.0.0 | b7ad1dad31e06c5925ef5d2fc7ad053ef454303e |
  | actions/setup-node | v7.0.0 | 820762786026740c76f36085b0efc47a31fe5020 |
  | actions/upload-artifact | v7.0.1 | 043fb46d1a93c77aae656e7c1c64a875d1fc6a0a |

  The npm dev dependencies are exact pins in `scripts/walk/package.json`: `playwright` 1.63.0, `@axe-core/playwright` 4.13.0 (published 2026-08-11) and `axe-core` 4.13.0.
- **The closing run.** `scripts/walk.sh --many 400 --live 3 --installed --keys --axe` on 2026-10-20 against this section's tree exited 0 in 105 s: `walk: PASS (17 rows)`, `axe: PASS (32 route-scheme runs …)` with 0 violations, and `keys: PASS (34 steps)`. Home's Needs heading read 33 after the Dismiss step, and the first live lane was infra's. No `/var/tmp/watchdeck-walk-*` dir was left, and the real `~/.claude` gained no backup file.

### Screenshots and the GIF

- **Five images.** `scripts/screenshots.sh` runs `scripts/walk.sh --many 400 --live 3 --installed --seed 7 --now <the run's UTC second> --screenshots`. It copies the PNGs into `docs/images` and exits 1 when one is missing or over 400 KB; nothing is resized or quantized. Each shot is 1280×800 CSS px at device scale 1 with reduced motion. The set in the tree was taken with `--now 2026-10-20T02:01:34Z`:

  | Image | Route and state |
  |---|---|
  | `sessions-live.png` | `#/sessions`: four live lanes (infra, web, api, proj) and their cards over the list of 406 |
  | `needs-resolve.png` | `#/` with `confirm-open`: Home's first Needs row with its Add rule dialog open |
  | `session-map.png` | `#/sessions/<C>/timeline` with `fit`: session C's four calls, two Deny rule and two Auto check, header "denied 3" |
  | `rules.png` | `#/rules`: the sandbox group, then the native rules watchdeck added |
  | `home-dark.png` | `#/` in the dark scheme |

  `docs/images/README.md` is the provenance: the flags, the dashboard state, the capture settings and sizes, and the eye review for login names, real paths and tokens. `needs-resolve.png` is Home's dialog, the inbox's resolve flow, not the Rules sheet (ruled 2026-10-20). `rules.png` shows no Recommended block, because `--installed` installs all twelve recommended rules (accepted).
- **The GIF.** `scripts/demo-gif.sh` records two halves with Playwright, 480×640 for the terminal and 800×640 for the dashboard (above the shell's 760 px breakpoint, so the sidebar shows; a 640 px half recorded the narrow bar layout, which read as the old header). The left half is a static HTML terminal in dark, typed with fixed delays. The right half is the dashboard on a `--many 400 --live 3 --installed --seed 7` fixture in light: Home, the first Auto check row's Add rule dialog, the confirm, the row fade, and the Needs count dropping. The dashboard script refuses to go on while the hash holds `t=`. ffmpeg cuts every frame recorded before that point (plus a 0.2 s margin) with `-ss`. It joins the halves with `hstack`, drops noise-only frames with `mpdecimate`, and samples at 12 fps. It passes through the 1280×640 scale step unchanged and builds a two-pass palette in one graph with bayer dither. The script removes the GIF and exits 1 unless it is exactly 1280×640, at most 4,194,304 bytes and 10 to 14 s long. `TestDemoGifScriptEnforcesCaps` checks that the script carries those checks, that the recordings are 480×640 and 800×640 and that the dashboard script has the `t=` guard. The settings and the ffmpeg line are in `docs/images/README.md`; `scripts/demo/README.md` says how to run it. The GIF in the tree was re-recorded on 2026-10-20 on the varied fixture. It is 1280×640, 12.0 s at 12 fps (144 frames) and 2,878,556 bytes, by ffprobe and `ls -l`.
- **No vhs.** The terminal half is an HTML rendering, labelled "demo" and headed "Claude Code · demo rendering", not a recorded terminal. `vhs` was not installed, and installing it is a system change the owner has not asked for (ruled 2026-10-20). `scripts/demo/README.md` names `brew install vhs` as the alternative.

### Carry-forward landed

From Plan 04 (section 10):

- **The Overview styles.** The unused `.page-overview` and `.ov-*` rules are gone from `app.css` (`TestNoOverviewCSS`).
- **The recommended install's `warning`.** The Rules page shows it as a toast through `COPY.rules.recommendedWarning` after Add and Add all (`TestRulesShowsRecommendedWarning`). Section 10's route table, which says the page does not show it yet, predates this.

From the screen review of 2026-10-20:

- **Refused calls in the map header.** `mapmodel.Totals` gained `blocked_calls`: calls refused before they ran, by a deny rule, by the auto-mode check, by watchdeck's own check with a deny, by a person's "No", or by a rejection the reader does not recognise (the last two since the Plan 05 fix wave, 2026-10-20). A sandbox refusal is not in it, and `denials` keeps its meaning (`TestTotalsBlockedCalls`, `TestBlockedCallsSources`). The header's denied number is `blocked_calls` plus the sandbox's refusals, each call once. The timeline hides its allow, ask and denied tags when all three are zero (`TestMapHeaderDenialsFromAuthority`). `buildVersion` is `mapapi-5` (it was `mapapi-4` before the fix wave). Session C now reads "denied 3", not "denied 0" beside "Deny rule 2".
- **The timeline fit.** The timeline fits to its drawn events, labels, lane headers and time labels plus 12 px, not to the whole canvas. Its zoom range is 0.5 to 1.6, and both views keep clear of the zoom buttons (`TestTimelineFitsItsContent`). In the hand check, session C's first-load scale went from 0.51 to 0.80.
- **The exclusion row's owner.** The `watchdeck run *` entry in `sandbox.excludedCommands`, in any scope but managed, reads "Added by watchdeck"; any other excluded command stays the person's (`TestExcludedRowOwnedByWatchdeck`).
- **Switch focus.** After a confirmed toggle the Rules page refocuses the same switch by row id (`TestRulesSwitchKeepsFocus`, and the keyboard pass's six switch steps).
- **The confirm race.** Escape followed by Space within about 2 ms reopened the confirm before the first one closed, so one click resolved both and sent two writes with one nonce. The confirm now settles on Escape and closes at once, ignores a late close, and settles any waiting confirm before a new one opens (`TestConfirmDialogSettlesEveryOpen`).
- **The file in Needs fixes.** `fixes[].file` from `/api/needs` shows home as `~` (`TestNeedsFixFileIsTildified`).
- **DESIGN lines.** The deleted image names, the stale "retaken" lines and the accent colour line in section 7 now match the tree.

### Known limits

1. **No JavaScript runtime tests.** The dashboard, walk and demo scripts are pinned by Go tests that read their source. Their behaviour rests on the walk and on hand checks.
2. **Axe covers the fixture states only.** It sees what the fixture home renders on each route in its first state. It does not see dialogs, sheets, empty states or error states.
3. **Safari and Firefox are untested.** Every run used Playwright's Chromium.
4. **The GIF's terminal half is a rendering**, not a real terminal, and the GIF is not byte-reproducible: the fixture uses the clock as `--now` and recording timing varies.
5. **The exclusion owner is a label.** The `watchdeck run *` entry reads "Added by watchdeck" whenever it is present, also when the person typed it or a partial uninstall left it. Excluded rows have no Remove, so nothing acts on the label.
6. **Two denial counts.** The Sessions table counts denials from the activity log. The map header also counts the refusals only the transcript recorded. A transcript-only session, session C among them, reads 0 in the table and 3 in its map. The header derives the sandbox's share on the client from `denials` minus `deny`.
