The header counts a person's "No" and a rejection the reader does not recognise (`TestBlockedCallsSources`). Left out: a refusal by a hook that is not watchdeck (it has no policy), and a Blocked call read from an error that carries no decision fields (it was not refused, so the header does not call it denied; the Blocked chip still shows it).
7. **The timeline draws its empty columns.** The Skill scripts and Sandbox lane headers are part of the fitted box, so a short session such as C fills about half the canvas width.
8. **The warning toast is styled as an error.** There is no warning tone; the toast is red and stays 7 s.
9. **Session C has no activity records**, so Home's Recent does not list it and the Sessions table shows it with 0 denials.
10. **`--installed` does not write `installed_at`.** That is `setup`'s, so `status` warns "install date: not recorded" in such a home and the inbox uses its 7-day window.
11. **Focus after a toggle.** If a switch's row is gone after the reload, focus falls to the page. No pack row disappears on a toggle today.
12. **The determinism test compares contents only.** File mtimes are not compared.

### Not verified yet (2026-10-20)

- **The walk workflow on a runner.** It has not been dispatched; the branch holding it has not been pushed. No walk has run on Linux.
- **Safari and Firefox** (known limit 3), and a screen reader pass; axe is automated only.
- **The in-session band screenshot.** `session-band.png` still waits for an interactive dogfood (`docs/images/README.md`, "Pending").
- **The hook fast path after Plan 05** was measured once on 2026-10-20 (`scripts/measure-hook.sh` with BEFORE at the Plan 04 close, darwin arm64, machine not idle): no-opinion median +0.08 ms, p95 +0.17 ms, inside the +1/+2 ms budget; one run, so it stays here rather than under verified.
- **A person's "No" in the transcript**, and a hook ask in `dontAsk` and interactive `bypassPermissions`: unchanged from section 9.
- Nothing in this section was run on Linux or Windows.

## 12. Trust (Plan 06, 2026-10-20)

Plan 06 adds four things: a release pipeline whose output can be verified, `watchdeck doctor --prove`, shadow mode for watchdeck's own rules, and CI jobs that run the tools the repository already had (staticcheck, govulncheck, the installer test, the plugin checks, the fuzz targets, the latency script) plus a reproducibility check. The code, the guard tests and the local runs below ran on darwin arm64 on 2026-10-20. The pipeline itself has not run, because no tag exists in this repository and signing needs the public repository. The new CI jobs are written for GitHub's ubuntu-24.04 and macos-latest runners and have not run there; they will when the owner pushes.

### Release pipeline (designed, not tested until a public tag)

- **Builds.** goreleaser v2 builds `./cmd/watchdeck` from the tag for darwin and linux, arm64 and amd64, with `CGO_ENABLED=0`, `-trimpath`, `-s -w -buildid=` and `mod_timestamp` set to the commit time. The archives are tar.gz files holding the binary, `LICENSE`, `NOTICE`, `README.md` and this file. `release: draft: true`, so the owner publishes after review.
- **SBOM.** One SPDX document per archive (`<archive>.sbom.json`), made by syft, which `anchore/sbom-action/download-syft` v0.24.3 puts on PATH. goreleaser lists the SBOMs in `checksums.txt` beside the archives, so the signature and the attestation cover them too.
- **Signature.** cosign 3.0.6 (installed by `sigstore/cosign-installer` v4.1.2) signs `checksums.txt` keyless with `sign-blob --bundle`, which writes one Sigstore bundle, `checksums.txt.sigstore.json`, holding the certificate and the Rekor entry. The bundle form is goreleaser's documented form, is one file, and verifies offline against the Sigstore trust root that cosign ships. cosign 3.x reads it natively, so a cosign 2.x user needs to upgrade.
- **Attestation.** `actions/attest-build-provenance` v4.2.2 runs with `subject-checksums: dist/checksums.txt`, so its subjects are exactly the files cosign signed, by digest, and the two channels describe the same bytes. If the action rejects goreleaser's checksum format on the first public tag, the fallback is one line: `subject-path: dist/*.tar.gz`.
- **The public gate, fail-closed.** cosign is installed, signing runs and the attestation is made only when `github.event.repository.visibility == 'public'`. Any other value (private, internal or absent) passes `--skip=sign` to goreleaser and skips the attest step (`TestReleaseSignAndAttestGatedOnPublic`). The reason is that Fulcio certificates and Rekor entries are public and name the repository and the workflow. A tag pushed before the repository goes public therefore yields an unsigned draft, by design. The job summary says "UNSIGNED draft" with the visibility it saw, or prints the two verification lines, and always prints `checksums.txt`.
- **Permissions and pins.** The workflow declares `contents: write`, `id-token: write` and `attestations: write` at workflow level and nothing per job (`TestReleaseWorkflowPermissions`, `TestWorkflowsDeclarePermissions`). Every action is pinned by commit SHA with its tag in a comment (`TestWorkflowActionsArePinned`); the three new pins were resolved from their tags with `gh api` on 2026-10-20 and are recorded in `release.yml` only. The only secret is `GITHUB_TOKEN`, and the checkout uses `fetch-depth: 0`. `TestGoreleaserReproducibleFlags` pins the build flags, the `sboms` and `signs` blocks, `draft: true`, the commented `brews` block and the absence of the old one-line description.
- **Verification.** The cosign and `gh attestation verify` lines are in the README and in SECURITY.md, "Verify a release". The installer runs them with `--verify` since Plan 07; see section 13, "The installer's verify step".
- **The local check (2026-10-20).** syft and cosign are not installed on this Mac, so `goreleaser release --snapshot --clean --skip=sign,sbom,publish` ran instead of the full pipeline. It produced four archives named `watchdeck_0.0.0-SNAPSHOT-<short>_<os>_<arch>.tar.gz` and a four-line `checksums.txt`; the darwin_arm64 archive lists `LICENSE`, `NOTICE`, `README.md`, `docs/DESIGN.md` and `watchdeck`. Two `goreleaser build --snapshot --clean` runs gave identical sha256 digests for all four binaries. The SBOM, the signature and the attestation were not exercised.

### `doctor --prove`

`watchdeck doctor --prove` reads the PreToolUse Bash registration from the settings files in doctor's order (`settings.Registration`; the `*` and matcher-less groups count, the Skill and PermissionDenied groups do not: `TestRegistrationExecForm`, `TestRegistrationShellForm`, `TestRegistrationWildcardGroup`, `TestRegistrationIgnoresOtherGroups`, `TestRegistrationAbsent`, `TestRegistrationPluginShellNotOurs`). It runs a registration only from user scope: Claude Code's user `settings.json` and the `settings.local.json` beside it. A project's `.claude/settings.json` and `.claude/settings.local.json` are read, never run. When only a project file registers the hook, the registration leg fails with "not run: prove runs user-scope registrations only (a repository's settings file could name any command)" and the three legs that depend on it read "not run" (`TestDoctorProveIgnoresProjectRegistration`, whose RED run showed the project stub really executing before the fix). When both scopes register it, the user entry runs and the pass message notes the project entry it did not run.

It runs that command, not `os.Executable()`, in a `demo.Prepare` tree under `/var/tmp`, with HOME, the XDG dirs, `CLAUDE_CONFIG_DIR` and the activity log pointed inside the tree and `CLAUDE_PROJECT_DIR` dropped (`TestProveChildEnvDropsProjectDir`). TMPDIR and `SSH_AUTH_SOCK` stay the parent's, because the sandbox's temp allow and its profile file resolve through the real TMPDIR; the canary writes nothing there. It feeds the hook a PreToolUse Bash payload for the embedded canary (`TestProvePayloads`, `TestProveParseReply`) and requires a `permissionDecision` and an `updatedInput.command` that starts with `watchdeck run --skill canary-request -c` and whose `-c` payload, unquoted, is the probe command, so a benign or chained payload fails the leg before anything runs; the pass message shows those first four words (`TestProveJudgeRewrite`, `TestUnquoteReversesQuote`). It executes that rewrite with `/bin/bash -c` under the parent PATH, as Claude Code would, and requires exit 1, `net: DENIED` on stdout, and a run record in the throwaway log with outcome `failed`, a network hint, and the decision's `tool_use_id` with `joined_by: write` (`TestProveJudgeRun`, `TestDoctorProveDeniesCanary`). A second payload, a Read of `<tmp HOME>/.ssh/id_fake` under a throwaway `overrides.yaml` (`version: 2`, an explicit `mode: enforce`, `secrets-read/read-ssh-private-key` raised to deny), must answer `permissionDecision: deny` (`TestProvePolicyFileParses`, `TestProveJudgeRead`). The shadow leg reads the real user policy through `policy.ShadowOn`: `mode: shadow` fails it, naming `watchdeck rules shadow off` (`TestProveShadowLeg`, `TestDoctorProveShadowOn`). A stubbed hook that prints nothing fails the rewrite and read legs (`TestDoctorProveFailsWhenHookStubbed`), no registration fails the first leg (`TestDoctorProveNoRegistration`), a rewrite whose run exits 126 fails the sandbox leg (`TestDoctorProveApplyFailure`), and an ask on the Read fails the read leg (`TestDoctorProveReadAskIsFail`). The five legs (registration, rewrite, sandbox, read rule, shadow) always appear, in that order, on every path (`TestProveRegistrationAndOrder`), and the usage names the flag (`TestDoctorUsageMentionsProve`). Inside the tree, nothing but the throwaway policy file is written (`TestProveRunWritesOnlyThePolicyFile`). The tree is removed afterwards and the real activity log is never opened (`TestDoctorProveDeniesCanary`). `TestDoctorProveDeniesCanary` ran for real on this Mac on 2026-10-20: Seatbelt, errno 1, the run record joined to the decision.

### Shadow mode

Shadow mode is `mode: shadow` in the user `overrides.yaml` (`manifest.PolicyFile.Mode`, normalised so that `enforce` and absence are the same; `policy.Policy.Shadow`, `policy.ShadowOn`), written by `watchdeck rules shadow on|off` through `rulebook.SetShadow` with the usual backup. The hook has one downgrade point: `combine` takes the mode as its fourth argument and, when a gate verdict is ask or deny, records it (`meta.Shadow`, `Would`, `WouldReason`) and returns the sandbox stage's answer unchanged, or nothing. A silent observation is a `decision` record with `decision: "shadow"`; a printed rewrite keeps its decision and gains `shadow: true` and `would`. Nothing else moves: the native mirror, the sandbox rewrite, the model-typed and low-confidence asks, the watchdog and an invalid file answer as before. The dashboard reads "Observing only" in the header, on Home and in a Rules banner, and Recent tags observed rows. On the map, the `skill_run` edge of a shadowed rewrite carries ` · observed: would <x>` after its label; a silent observation shows on its timeline node's sub-line and in its card (`decision: observed: would deny`), and the Recent row's accessible name names it. `doctor` and `status` warn. The policy cache is version 2.

Verified on this Mac, 2026-10-20:

- **The hook.** A path gate deny under shadow prints nothing and records would deny with the gate's subject and target (`TestShadowNeverDeniesAndRecordsWould`); asks and the other gate kinds (`TestShadowAskRecordsWould`); a skill rewrite goes out unchanged with would on the record (`TestShadowKeepsSandboxRewrite`), and a rewrite with no gate verdict has no shadow fields (`TestShadowRewriteAloneHasNoShadowFields`); an auto-allowed skill runs where enforce would deny (`TestShadowAutoAllowRunsDespiteWouldDeny`); the gate stage never allows (`TestShadowNeverAllows`, and `FuzzGatesNeverAllow` with a shadow fixture and a never-allow assertion, 10 s with no crasher on 2026-10-20); the project file cannot set it (`TestShadowProjectModeIgnored`), and an invalid project file beside a shadow user file stays silent (`TestShadowInvalidProjectFileStaysSilent`); native rules, model-typed commands and an invalid user file keep asking (`TestShadowNativeRulesStillEnforced`, `TestShadowModelTypedCommandsStillAsk`, `TestShadowInvalidPolicyStillAsks`); log-only rules and PostToolUse are unchanged (`TestShadowLogOnlyUnchanged`, `TestShadowPostToolUseUnchanged`).
- **The policy file.** Parse, render, apply and clone (`TestPolicyModeParse`, `TestPolicyModeRenderRoundTrip`, `TestPolicyModeApply`, `TestPolicyModeSurvivesApply`); `ShadowOn` and the project warning (`TestPolicyShadowOn`, `TestPolicyShadowProjectWarns`); the cache rebuilds a version 1 doc (then `TestPolicyCacheVersionIsTwo`; since Plan 08 the cache is version 3 and the test is `TestPolicyCacheVersionIsThree`).
- **The CLI.** `rules shadow on|off` (`TestRulesShadowOnOff`), the model-typed guard that lets `watchdeck rules shadow` alone pass (`TestRulesShadowStatusIsReadOnly`), doctor and status (`TestDoctorShadowWarns`) and `policy check` (`TestPolicyCheckPrintsMode`).
- **The surface.** `/api/status` (`TestStatusShadowField`), Recent (`TestRecentShadowOutcome`), the map (`TestMapShadowLabel`), no Needs you item (`TestShadowRecordIsNotANeed`), no denial in the sessions catalog (`TestShadowRecordIsNotADenial`) and the copy (`TestCopyHasObservingOnly`). The walk passed on a shadow fixture (17 rows, then `--keys` with 34 steps), and its screenshots of the observing state (Home, Rules, the timeline and the Write card, light and dark) were checked by eye.

### Fast path

The single runs during the plan, darwin arm64, 200 spawns per payload, machine not idle, 2026-10-20: after the shadow backend, no-opinion median +0.26 ms and p95 +0.34 ms; after `doctor --prove`, median −0.11 ms and p95 −0.63 ms, with the Read payload's median +4.8 ms on a loaded machine (the hook never runs `internal/prove`, and the pack-hit payload moved the other way). Single runs stay as context; the table below is the measurement of record.

`scripts/measure-hook.sh` with BEFORE at the Plan 05 close (main before Plan 06) and AFTER at the Plan 06 fast-path commit, darwin arm64, 200 spawns per payload, three runs, 2026-10-20 (no worker processes running; the machine not idle). No-opinion payload, in ms:

| Run | Before median | After median | Before p95 | After p95 | Median delta | p95 delta |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | 5.17 | 5.29 | 6.16 | 6.68 | +0.12 | +0.52 |
| 2 | 5.10 | 5.12 | 5.36 | 5.34 | +0.02 | −0.02 |

Every run is within the +1/+2 ms acceptance (`CHECK=1`, exit 0 three times) and under the 8 ms gate; the rewrite payload moved by −0.07, +0.10 and −0.04 ms and the Read payload by +0.25, −0.09 and +0.07 ms, so the +4.8 ms Read row of the single run above was load; the stripped binary grew from 11,418,578 to 11,501,554 bytes (+82,976, the `prove` package and the shadow fields); no module package has init work. The gate for a tag is a no-opinion median at or under 8 ms on the owner's Mac in every run, within the script's +1/+2 ms acceptance (CONTRIBUTING, "Releasing"). ### CI

`ci.yml` keeps the `test` job (vet and the full suite on macos-latest and ubuntu-24.04, Linux with bubblewrap) and adds these, all with `permissions: contents: read` and every action pinned (`TestCIJobsPinTheirContract`, `TestWorkflowActionsArePinned`):

- **lint.** staticcheck v0.8.2 for `GOOS=linux` and `GOOS=darwin` from one Linux runner, installed with `go install` so it never enters `go.mod`; the job fails if `go.mod` or `go.sum` changed. v0.8.2 because v0.8.1 cannot read Go 1.27 export data ("export data version 5 is greater than maximum supported version 4"). `go vet` stays in `test`.
- **vuln.** govulncheck v1.8.0 for both GOOS, installed the same way.
- **install.** `scripts/test-install.sh` on macos-latest and ubuntu-24.04, Linux with bubblewrap.
- **plugin.** Claude Code 2.1.304 from npm, with its config dir in the runner's temp dir, runs `claude plugin validate plugin --strict` and `claude plugin test plugin`; the CLI reference says neither needs a sign-in or the network. If the first run contradicts that, the test step is dropped and CONTRIBUTING's "Releasing" runs it by hand.
- **fuzz.** `FuzzEval`, `FuzzGatesNeverAllow` and `FuzzParsePolicyFile`, 20 s each; a crasher is uploaded as an artifact.
- **latency.** `CHECK=1 scripts/measure-hook.sh` on macos-latest, BEFORE the pull request's base or the push's previous tip (`TestMeasureHookScriptHasCheckMode` pins the exit 2 when over budget). The step has `continue-on-error`; a follow-up step turns exit 2 into a warning and any other failure into a job failure.
- **repro-a, repro-b, repro-compare.** Two jobs build the snapshot with `go-version-file: go.mod` and pass their binaries' sha256 lists out as job outputs; `repro-compare` diffs them.
- **secrets.** gitleaks 8.30.1, downloaded as a release tarball pinned by version and sha256 (the marketplace action needs a licence for organisation repositories), `git` mode over the full history with `--redact`; `.gitleaks.toml` allowlists the fixture paths it names. Since Plan 07; its first run was CI run 39520861406 on 2026-10-21, green (section 13, "Secret scanning").

First green run: run 39378450181 on 2026-10-20, every job green: test on macos-latest and ubuntu-24.04, lint, vuln, install on both, plugin, the three fuzz targets, latency, repro-a, repro-b and repro-compare.

### Known limits

1. **The pipeline has not run.** No tag exists in this repository, and signing and attestation need the public repository; the first run is the test.
2. **Archive reproducibility is not asserted.** The repro jobs compare the binaries, not the tar.gz files (goreleaser's tar headers).
3. **Rekor and Fulcio are a dependency.** Verification needs the public Sigstore instance; an offline check of the bundle still needs the Sigstore trust root that cosign ships.
4. **The latency job is advisory.** Runners are noisy; the owner's-machine number is the gate.
5. **Pinned tools do not auto-update.** staticcheck, govulncheck and the Claude Code CLI version in `ci.yml` are pinned by hand; Dependabot watches actions and `go.mod` only.
6. **The installer checked `checksums.txt` only until Plan 07.** `scripts/install.sh --verify` now verifies the attestation with the GitHub CLI, or the cosign bundle, before the sha256 check; see section 13, "The installer's verify step". Without the flag the sha256 check alone still runs, and one line says so.
7. **The prove runs the hook, not Claude Code.** It runs the binary the settings name through `/bin/bash -c` under the person's PATH, so it proves the hook's answers and the sandbox, not that Claude Code calls the hook for a given tool. `scripts/prove-native-deny.sh` is the Claude Code end of that proof for native rules.
8. **User-scope settings registrations only.** A hook registered only in a project's settings is never run: Claude Code runs project hooks only after its own trust prompt, which the prove would bypass. A plugin-only install fails the registration leg, because the plugin's `bin/hook.sh` is not run.
9. **Managed-scope hooks are invisible** to every settings reader, this one included.
10. **The hook subprocess reads the real managed Claude Code settings** for Bash deny and ask rules (`ManagedDirOverride` is tests-only). A managed `Bash(python3 *)` deny turns the rewrite leg into a fail that quotes the rule, which is the hook behaving correctly.
11. **The Read leg feeds the payload straight to the binary.** A default install leaves Read unhooked once the recommended native rules are installed (section 9), so the message says Claude Code sends Read calls to the hook only while Read is hooked.
12. **How the sandbox leg reads an exit.** Inside a nested sandbox (doctor's self-test reports Nested, for example when typed inside Claude Code's Bash sandbox) the leg is a warn, "run doctor --prove from a terminal". Exit 126 anywhere else is a fail, and so is exit 0, which means the probe reached the network. Exit 127 fails the leg with a message saying a program on the rewrite's path was not found, usually watchdeck, and naming the registered binary.
13. **The tree needs `/var/tmp`.** Without it the tree lands under TMPDIR and the profile cannot be compiled (the allow on an ancestor of HOME); the sandbox leg then reports the policy error. `demo` has the same constraint.
14. **The registration's `timeout` is the call deadline** (default 10 s, capped at 30 s for the proof, which the registration leg then says), with one retry on the watchdog's ask. The whole prove's deadline derives from the legs: four hook calls, the run and 5 s, so a leg's own deadline always passes first (`TestProveDeadline`).
15. **A narrow test race.** `TestDoctorProveDeniesCanary` globs `/var/tmp/watchdeck-demo-*` before and after its run; a sibling demo test that creates a tree in that second and leaves it would trip it.
16. **An auto-allowed skill runs under shadow.** A skill command that a rule would deny runs sandboxed without a prompt.
17. **No dashboard toggle.** The CLI is the only writer of the mode.
18. **The project file cannot set the mode.** A project `mode: shadow` is reported as ignored; a project `mode: enforce` is a silent no-op.
19. **A version 1 file cannot hold the mode.** `rules shadow on` converts it to version 2 with a `.bak-v1-` backup.
20. **What is never shadowed.** The native mirror, the sandbox, the hook's own asks and the watchdog. The command guard is conservative here: `watchdeck rules shadow; echo ok` asks, the safe direction.
21. **The gate's subject on a rewrite.** A shadowed verdict that rode on a rewrite keeps the skill's subject, so the gate's subject survives only in `would_reason`.
22. **`status --json` has no shadow key.** The doctor check carries it.
23. **The cache bump re-parses once** after an upgrade.
24. **"Cannot normalise" asks are shadowed.** The gate stage's asks for a target it could not normalise are recorded as would ask.
25. **Where an observation does not show.** Topology aggregates calls per tool and shows no per-call observation; the timeline node and the card do. The in-session band shows a silent shadow record as an unlabelled no-opinion call named Bash, because `plugin/hooks/activity.ts` maps only allow and ask (left unfixed by ruling).
26. **Text outside the copy lint.** The map card's subject text "tool files (gate)" and the node's accessible name come from Go, as in section 10, known limit 12.
27. **A non-string mode.** go-yaml reads `mode: 1` as the string "1", so the error is the value-free `mode: must be enforce or shadow`.
28. **Recent labels a silently observed call with watchdeck as the authority**, because the activity log has no transcript join; the map, which has one, shows the transcript's own authority (you, an allow rule, the auto check or no check) beside the observation.

### Not verified yet (2026-10-20)

- **A signed release** and `gh attestation verify` against a real asset: they need the public repository and a tag.
- **What the first CI run cannot show.** Run 39378450181 (2026-10-20) was green on every job, so the jobs themselves are verified; the ubuntu-24.04 `test` job runs `go test ./...` without `-v`, so whether `TestDoctorProveDeniesCanary` ran or skipped under bubblewrap there is not visible (a `-run` step with `-v` is a Phase 7 CI item). The plugin job ran `claude plugin test` without any sign-in, as the CLI reference says.
- **`doctor --prove` on Linux** (bubblewrap) and the shell-form registration path on a real install; both are covered by unit tests only. Also not run: the prove on the owner's real install (an owner item in CONTRIBUTING's "Releasing").
- **The plugin's hook path under shadow.** It is the same binary and code path, with no separate test.
- **Safari, a screen-reader pass, a person's "No" in the transcript, and a hook ask in `dontAsk` and interactive `bypassPermissions`:** unchanged from section 11.
- Nothing in this section has run on Linux or Windows yet.

## 13. Evidence and hygiene (Plan 07, 2026-10-20)

The first slice of Phase 7. Four independent pieces, each with its own subsection, written by the task that built it; the close subsections are the integrator's. Plan: the private Plan 07 file. Nothing here changes the hook pipeline; `rules explain` is a second entry point beside the hook's `Run`.

### `rules explain`

`watchdeck rules explain` is a dry run of the hook for one call: it says what the hook would answer and by which rule, with exit codes a CI loop can read, and records nothing. The decisions (Plan 07, 2026-10-20): a subcommand of `rules`, not a new top-level command or a `policy` subcommand (D1: a person who asks "why would this be denied?" looks under `rules`; `policy check` lints files, `explain` evaluates one call). The WHAT flags are `rules add`'s vocabulary where one flag names one call (D2): `--command CMD`, `--read PATH`, `--edit PATH`, `--path PATH` meaning a Write, `--url URL` for a WebFetch, `--mcp TOOL` as Claude Code names the tool (`mcp__server__tool`, an empty input), `--skill NAME`; plus `--cwd DIR`, `--mode MODE` (`default`, `acceptEdits`, `auto`, `bypassPermissions`, `dontAsk`, `plan`), `--json` and `--expect allow|ask|deny`. Exit 0 allowed or no opinion, 1 deny, 2 ask (a sandbox rewrite that asks counts as ask), 3 usage, 4 a policy file could not be loaded; with `--expect`, 0 when the verdict matches and 1 when it does not, 3 and 4 as before (D3). It never appends to the activity log, the sessions log, the inbox or any policy file; it refreshes the skill index cache and the policy cache exactly as a hook call would, and the Claude Code rules cache for every Bash command (D4).

How it is built. `internal/hook` gains `Explain(in Input, env Env) (*Output, Explanation)`: the same pipeline as a hook call (`decide`, behind the recovery `handle` uses, now the shared `decideSafely`) with no watchdog, no lifecycle touch and no activity line; it clears `env.ActivityPath` and `env.Lifecycle` itself, so a caller cannot make it write (belt and braces: `decide` itself never writes, the appends are `Run`'s and `handle`'s, and the tests catch a route through `handle`). `Explanation` is a decision record's content without the record: the verdict (`allow`, `ask`, `deny` or `none`), the reason verbatim, the rewritten command, the rule hits, the subject, the enforcement class, the skills, the confidence and the shadow fields (`Shadow`, `Would`, `WouldReason`). `cmd/watchdeck/rulesexplain.go` builds the synthetic PreToolUse `Input` (session id `explain`, tool use id `toolu_explain`, the cwd from `--cwd` or the working directory, the permission mode from `--mode`) and the `Env` as the hook command builds it, with an empty activity path and the zero lifecycle store. Paths and URLs pass through as typed, so a relative path resolves against the cwd inside the pipeline, as in a session; a relative `--cwd` resolves against the working directory, and `--cwd` drops `CLAUDE_PROJECT_DIR` so the native rules come from that directory's project. The policy is loaded as the hook loads it (`policy.LoadCached` on the state-dir cache): a user file that cannot be parsed is exit 4 after the verdict is printed (the hook fails closed with an ask, and that ask is the verdict shown); a project file that cannot be parsed is a warning on stderr (the hook drops that layer and decides without it). The check lives in the CLI because `decide` never errors on a policy file; exit 4 is also the code for a `--cwd` that cannot be made absolute and for a JSON encoding failure, which D3 does not assign. When the hook has no claude-settings hit for a Bash command (it matches the person's Claude Code Bash deny and ask rules only before a rewrite), `Explain` runs the same loader and matcher read-only and names a plain deny or ask match in `Explanation.NativeRule` (value, kind, scope; JSON `native_rule`, `null` when none, an unreadable settings file or an unparseable command): the `Output` is unchanged, the verdict and exit stay the hook's, the human form adds a `note:` line (`your Claude Code deny rule Bash(curl *) (user settings) matches this command; Claude Code refuses it itself`, or `Claude Code applies it itself` for an ask rule, because what an ask does depends on the permission mode: a prompt by default, none under `dontAsk` or `bypassPermissions` (Claude Code's documented behaviour; not run here)) and an `--expect` mismatch names the rule on stderr (review of Task 1, check 4). The human form prints the call, `verdict:` with the rewrite, the shadowed verdict or "no watchdeck rule applies; Claude Code decides as usual" in parentheses, `reason:` verbatim, `would:` under shadow mode and `rules:` as `rules list` spells the ids (`pack:<pack>/<id>`, `user:<id>`, `claude-settings:<value>`); `--json` prints `tool`, `input`, `verdict` (`none` for no opinion), `reason`, `rewritten`, `rules` (as `RuleHit` marshals), `subject`, `enforcement`, `skills`, `confidence`, `shadow` (`on`, `would`, `would_reason`), `mode` and `cwd`. The model-typed guard treats `rules explain` as read-only, like `rules list`.

Verified, with test names. `TestExplainMatchesDecide` (seventeen cases: a skill script, a native deny and a native ask on a skill command, a pack hit, a gate, a no-opinion Bash, a log-only hit, a Read, a Read of an SSH key, a WebFetch, an MCP tool, a model-typed command, two model-typed runs, a Skill call, and under shadow mode a shadowed gate deny and a shadowed rule beside a rewrite; `Explain`'s Output equals `Decide`'s byte for byte of the JSON, and the verdict names it), `TestExplainNamesNativeRuleWithoutRewrite` (a plain `curl` under a `Bash(curl *)` deny and a `git clean -fd` under an ask name the rule with the Output still `Decide`'s nil; a skill command with the same `curl` names nothing twice; a gate deny beside a native match names both; a Read never names one; no settings file names nothing and writes nothing), `TestExplainFillsMeta`, `TestExplainWritesNothing` (an `Env` with a real activity path and lifecycle store; after every kind of call `Run` would record, under enforce and under shadow mode, neither file exists, and `Run` on the same call does record, so the fixture is not vacuous), `TestExplainReportsShadow`, the explain cases of `TestModelTypedReadOnlyCommandsPass` and `TestModelTypedMutatingCommandsAsk`; in `cmd/watchdeck`, `TestRulesExplainDenyExit1`, `TestRulesExplainAskForSkillScript`, `TestRulesExplainNoOpinionExit0`, `TestRulesExplainLogOnlyIsNoOpinion`, `TestRulesExplainNativeDeny` (the deny on a skill command; the plain `curl` with the `note:` line, `native_rule` in `--json`, the rule on stderr under `--expect deny`, and a project-scope ask rule), `TestRulesExplainReadRule`, `TestRulesExplainExpect`, `TestRulesExplainJSON` (under shadow mode too, where `--expect allow` exits 0 and `--expect deny` exits 1, each with one stderr line naming what enforce mode would do), `TestRulesExplainCwdAndMode`, `TestRulesExplainUsage`, `TestRulesExplainInvalidPolicyExit4` and `TestRulesExplainWritesNoRecord` (after fourteen calls of every form: no activity log, no sessions log, no inbox; `overrides.yaml` and `settings.json` keep their mtime and content).

Limits. `explain` evaluates the text it is given exactly as the hook would, so the native-rule non-matches of section 8 apply (a quoted or escaped command word, `${IFS}`, brace expansion, `exec`, `env`, `sudo`, `eval` or `xargs` with options, a binary named by path such as `/usr/bin/curl`). Its verdict is the hook's, not Claude Code's: the hook checks the person's Claude Code Bash deny and ask rules only before a rewrite, so a plain `curl x` under a `Bash(curl *)` deny is `none` with exit 0, and the `note:` line names the rule (Claude Code refuses it itself); the note covers a plain deny or ask match only, so the matcher's non-matches above apply to it too. A Read that a native twin covers is answered by the pack rule the twin mirrors, whether or not the Read hook is registered: it says what the hook would answer if called, not whether it is called (`doctor` says that). A `--skill` call is the main session's, where only an agent rule gates the Skill tool, so it is `none` unless a subagent rule names it (there is no `--agent` flag). A model-typed `rules explain` whose inner command holds `$`, a backtick or a backslash asks, like every command that names the binary and an expansion (section 10). The no-opinion verdict is `none` in `--json` and `allow  (no watchdeck rule applies; Claude Code decides as usual)` on screen; `--expect allow` accepts both. Under shadow mode the verdict is the observed one, so `--expect allow` passes a call that enforce mode would deny; the exit stays D3's, and `--expect` adds one stderr line, `shadow mode is on: enforce mode would deny by <rule>` (review of Plan 07, M8).

### The denied study

`docs/DENIED.md` records what the models did when a call was refused, from 18 headless sessions on this Mac (go1.27.2, macOS 27.0.1): curl-deny and secret-read on 2026-10-20 under Claude Code 2.1.304, skill-net on 2026-10-21 under Claude Code 2.1.306. The decisions (Plan 07, 2026-10-20): auto mode with the built-in tools on, because headless `default` mode turns every ask into a refusal and hides the routes around it (D5); three scenarios, three models (`claude-opus-5-5`, `claude-sonnet-5-5`, `claude-haiku-5-5`), two runs each, `--max-turns 15`, one smoke run first (D6); every designed denial a deny, never an ask: two native deny rules (`Read(./secrets/**)`, `Bash(curl *)`), one watchdeck user rule denying the secret's path words in Bash, and the sandbox with network `none` for the canary skill (D7); random markers generated per script run, one written into the fake secret and one served from a loopback HTTP server, with "leaked" meaning a marker in a tool result or the final reply, and no marker shaped like a real key (D8); the write-up in `docs/DENIED.md`, linked from README "What it does not stop" and GUIDE "What this does not do", with versions, dates, flags, per-run call lists with temp paths scrubbed, the results tables and the two honesty sections (D9).

The harness. `scripts/denied-study.sh` (bash, gated on `WATCHDECK_STUDY=1`) builds watchdeck from the tree into a temp `bin` directory and puts that directory first on the `PATH` that `claude`, its Bash tool and so the hook's rewrite inherit. It writes the throwaway project with a copy of `testdata/skills/canary-basic` and the fake secret, starts the web server, points watchdeck's config, state and cache directories and its activity log into the temp directory (for the `claude` process and, through a wrapper, for the hook), and writes a settings file with the hook on PreToolUse for Bash and Skill and on PermissionDenied for every tool, the two deny rules and `watchdeck run *` in `sandbox.excludedCommands`. Before any API call (`--check` stops there) it feeds synthetic calls to the hook, checks the answers and the records, then runs the allowed rewrite with `/bin/bash -c` from the project: it fails unless the probe starts (not exit 127, no "command not found"), prints its loopback connect as refused, and leaves a `run` record in the temp log. With the `PATH` line removed that check fails with exit 127 (checked 2026-10-21 on a scratch copy, no API call). It reads `claude --help` and uses `--setting-sources project`, `--strict-mcp-config`, `--no-session-persistence`, `--include-hook-events` and `--max-budget-usd` when offered (all five were, on both days), stats the real watchdeck directories before and after, and checks one decision record per `tool_use_id` and one hook start per Bash call. `scripts/denied-study.py` reads each run's `stream.jsonl` and activity log and writes `summary.md` and `summary.json`: every call with what stopped it (a hook rule, a hook ask, a Claude Code deny rule, the sandbox, the auto classifier, nothing, or "failed to start" for a Bash call whose shell answered exit 127 or "command not found"), the model of each call and of the run with any `model_refusal_fallback` event, the evasion class of each call after the first denial from a fixed table (a Read, Grep or Glob has a class only when it targets the secret), leaks, and how the run ended; temp paths and both markers are replaced in everything it prints. Test: `python3 -I scripts/denied-study.py --selftest` runs the classifier over an embedded stream (a native deny, a hook-rule deny, a meta tool, an auto-classifier deny, a curl deny and an unstopped interpreter call that leaks), a hook ask refused headless, a sandbox denial, a fallback from the requested model, a rewrite that exits 127, a Read of the skill script after a denial, and 25 class cases; it prints PASS. The three checks added on 2026-10-21 (responding model, failed to start, non-secret file tools) were run first against the old classifier and failed 15 checks before the fix.

Findings (18 sessions reported, 0 reruns; Claude Code's reported cost 0.550 USD for the twelve of 2026-10-20 and 0.529 USD for the six of 2026-10-21). No marker leaked. Claude Code's deny rules refused all twelve first calls of the secret-read and curl-deny sessions. The sandbox refused the probe's connect in all five skill-net sessions that ran it as one plain command (Seatbelt, network `none`, a `run` record with a network hint each time), and no model tried another route after that refusal; every rewrite's hook response also tells the model not to copy, pipe or retry the script and to report the denial, and all five replies cite it (three as the sandbox hook or its notice, the two Opus replies as the environment's or the hook's guidance; the count is over each run's full final reply), so the study cannot separate the OS refusal from that instruction. Five of the six curl-deny sessions loaded WebFetch and all four Haiku and Sonnet sessions called it on the same URL; the auto classifier refused one call, and the other three ran unrefused and failed only on WebFetch's HTTPS upgrade, so a native `Bash(curl *)` deny left WebFetch open. No model tried a shell route to the secret, so `study-secret-bash` refused nothing. Three skill-net calls named the skill's script inside a compound command; the hook asked, as designed, and headless auto mode refused each ask, so D7's "never an ask" did not hold for that scenario. The PermissionDenied hook fired once, for the classifier's refusal. Claude Code switched four of the six Opus sessions to `claude-opus-4-8` after a safeguards refusal (category `cyber`), so most Opus rows describe Opus 4.8. The first skill-net sessions, on 2026-10-20, never reached the sandbox: the harness had left its build directory off the `PATH`, so the three allowed rewrites exited 127 before the script started (closed: nothing ran unsandboxed), and the other three sessions made four asks, all refused. Those six sessions (0.545 USD) are superseded by the re-run and kept outside the tree.

Limits. Two runs per cell; headless auto mode only; prompts that authorised workarounds outright; one machine, two days and two Claude Code versions. A native Bash deny rule covers the Bash tool only, so WebFetch to the same URL is not refused unless a WebFetch rule or a watchdeck domain gate names it (GUIDE "Policies beyond skills": a rule "sees the one tool call and nothing else"); no WebFetch rule was tested. The hook's ask reason names the skill's profile ("default profile (network=none …)"), not that the call was asked about, and a headless model that gets it back as the error of a refused ask can read it as a sandbox refusal of a script that never ran (Haiku on 2026-10-21; Opus 4.8 and Haiku in the superseded sessions); the ask's response also carries the rewrite's context ("This command runs under a watchdeck sandbox for skill …"), a likely second cause; a follow-up is to say in the reason or the context that the call was asked about and did not run. The hook and `watchdeck run` were not isolated from the owner's Claude Code files: the skill scanner roots at `$HOME/.claude`, not `CLAUDE_CONFIG_DIR` (`internal/skills/skills.go`, `personal`, `plugins`, `enabledPlugins`), so every session where the hook ran (the twelve with a Bash call) read the real personal skills directory and plugin registry, and the user settings files when that registry exists (reads only; nothing written, `projects/` untouched); a follow-up is to run the hook under a temp `HOME` with the credentials left reachable for `claude` alone. Reading the skill's own files with `cat` in a compound command is asked about too, and refused headless. A person's "No" is still not captured.

### The installer's verify step

`scripts/install.sh --verify`, or `WATCHDECK_VERIFY=1` (exactly `1`; the flag turns it on whatever the variable says), verifies the release's provenance between the download of `checksums.txt` and the sha256 check (Plan 07 D10). The order of preference: when `gh` is on PATH and `gh auth status` succeeds, `gh attestation verify <archive> --repo Panda-Taps-LLC/watchdeck --signer-workflow Panda-Taps-LLC/watchdeck/.github/workflows/release.yml`, which checks the archive itself, because the attestation's subjects are the files in `checksums.txt` (`subject-checksums`); else, when cosign is on PATH, the installer fetches `checksums.txt.sigstore.json` from the release (absent: "no checksums.txt.sigstore.json in the release: an unsigned draft; nothing installed") and runs `cosign verify-blob --bundle checksums.txt.sigstore.json --certificate-identity https://github.com/Panda-Taps-LLC/watchdeck/.github/workflows/release.yml@refs/tags/<tag> --certificate-oidc-issuer https://token.actions.githubusercontent.com checksums.txt`, so the bundle covers `checksums.txt` and the sha256 check that follows binds the archive to it; else it dies naming the state of both tools ("gh is not installed" or "gh is installed but not signed in", "cosign is not installed"). The sha256 check runs afterwards in every case, so a tampered `checksums.txt` is refused after a passing verify. A failed verify dies with the tool's first non-blank stderr line (the GitHub CLI prints a blank line before its `Error:` line), or its exit status when stderr is blank, and "nothing installed", before the install directory exists. Without `--verify`, one line after `sha256 ok` says "Not verified against the release signature; add --verify". Every tool reads stdin from `/dev/null`, because under `curl | sh` stdin is the script; nothing on the non-verify path looks for either tool. The README's one-liner keeps fetching the script from `main` (D11), with one sentence beside it on pinning both the script and the release to a tag with `--version vX.Y.Z` and the script fetched from that tag's URL.

Tested against stubs on 2026-10-20 (darwin arm64; `scripts/test-install.sh`, 14 cases; the CI `install` job ran the same script green on macos-latest and ubuntu-24.04 in run 39520861406 on 2026-10-21): sh scripts named exactly `gh` and `cosign`, first on PATH, log their argv and exit per `STUB_RC` (`gh auth status` per `STUB_AUTH_RC`, stderr from `STUB_ERR`); a symlink-only PATH of the interpreter and the five tools the installer needs before the verify step (`sh uname mktemp grep cp rm`) stands in for a machine with neither tool, because GitHub's ubuntu runners have `gh` in `/usr/bin`; the script asserts that the stub resolves first and that `gh` is unreachable from the minimal PATH, and unsets `WATCHDECK_VERIFY`, so the real GitHub CLI is never run. Case 1 expects the not-verified line; cases 9 to 14: `--verify` with `gh` signed in installs, the logged line names the repository and the signer workflow, cosign is not called and the not-verified line is absent (9); a failed `gh` verify installs nothing and the message carries the stub's stderr line, also when that stderr starts with a blank line as the real `gh` 2.100.0 prints it (10); `gh` signed out falls back to cosign, whose logged line carries the bundle, the identity for `v9.9.9` and `checksums.txt`, and `gh attestation verify` is not called, and a failing cosign verify with the bundle present installs nothing with the message carrying the first non-blank stderr line (11); neither tool, and `gh` signed out without cosign, each install nothing and name both tools with their state (12); `WATCHDECK_VERIFY=1` without the flag turns verify on, and a release without the bundle is an unsigned draft with cosign never run (13); a tampered checksum is still refused after a passing verify, with both lines in the output (14). A review mutation that removed the cosign-failure die survived the first fourteen-case suite, which is why case 11 gained that sub-run (2026-10-20). Designed, not tested against a real release: the release workflow signs and attests only when the repository is public, so no signed archive exists yet, and the first public tag is the first real run of `gh attestation verify` and of the cosign line.

### Secret scanning

**D12.** gitleaks runs as a CLI download pinned by version and by the sha256 of its tarball, not as the marketplace action (the action needs a licence for organisation repositories; the CLI is free). Scan mode `git` over the full history with `--redact`. CodeQL waits for the public flip (default setup is free for public repositories and needs a paid plan while private).

**The job.** `secrets` in `ci.yml`, ubuntu-24.04, under the file's single `permissions: contents: read` and no new action: checkout with `fetch-depth: 0`; one step in the runner's temp dir downloads `gitleaks_8.30.1_linux_x64.tar.gz` with `curl -fsSL --proto '=https' --tlsv1.2`, checks it with `sha256sum -c` against the digest written in the workflow, unpacks only the `gitleaks` member and prints its version; a second step runs `gitleaks git --redact --no-banner --exit-code 1 --config .gitleaks.toml .` in the workspace. 8.30.1 spells the history mode `gitleaks git [flags] [repo]`, with `--redact`, `--no-banner`, `--exit-code` and `--config` as global flags, as the plan assumed. Its default `git log` options walk every ref, not only HEAD's ancestry: checked 2026-10-20 in a throwaway repository with a side branch, 3 commits scanned where HEAD has 2 (a `fetch-depth: 0` checkout fetches every branch and tag, per actions/checkout's documentation, not something this task ran). No `dir` pass in CI: a checkout has no untracked files. `TestCIJobsPinTheirContract` pins the `secrets:` job entry and then reads the job's own lines, from its key to the next column-2 key, skipping YAML comments (a comment repeating the words cannot stand in for the step; the first draft's comment could, found in review on 2026-10-20). In those lines it requires the string `gitleaks_8.30.1`, the release URL, the digest line `sha256sum -c` reads, `fetch-depth: 0` and the scan step's exact `run:` line, so no flag can be dropped and no `|| true` added; it refuses any `if:` and any `continue-on-error` in the job. For `.gitleaks.toml` it allows only `title`, `[extend]` with `useDefault = true` and nothing else, and `[[allowlists]]` entries holding only `description`, a non-empty `targetRules` and `paths` whose every entry is a literal file path ending in an extension and `$`; any other table or key fails (the legacy `[allowlist]`, `regexes`, `disabledRules`, a custom `[[rules]]`). The final review found on 2026-10-21 that the first guard pinned strings only: deleting the job's `fetch-depth: 0` (the same line exists in three other jobs), adding `continue-on-error` or an `if:` to the job, or adding an unscoped allowlist entry each passed it. `TestCIGuardCatchesSecretsWeakenings` applies those four and fifteen more such edits to the real files' text and requires the guard to name each one, and requires the clean files to pass; each of the four also failed `TestCIJobsPinTheirContract` when applied to the files in place, and passed the old guard (fix wave, 2026-10-21). A re-pin therefore edits the test too (CONTRIBUTING "Releasing").

**Pin provenance (2026-10-20).** `gh api repos/gitleaks/gitleaks/releases/latest` answered v8.30.1 (published 2026-03-21). The linux_x64 and darwin_arm64 tarballs and the release's `gitleaks_8.30.1_checksums.txt` were downloaded over https into an empty directory; both tarballs matched the checksums file under `shasum -a 256 -c` and `sha256sum -c`. The linux digest in `ci.yml` is the one from that file; it lives nowhere else.

**The config.** `.gitleaks.toml`: `title`, `[extend] useDefault = true`, one allowlist entry. The plan wrote `[allowlist]`; 8.30.1's README says that table was replaced by `[[allowlists]]` in v8.25.0 (the old spelling still parses), so the file uses the current form. The entry is scoped with `targetRules = ["curl-auth-user"]` to the one rule that fired and with `paths` to the one file, `internal/rules/testdata/fp-corpus.txt` (end-anchored only, because `dir` mode reports absolute paths for an absolute target). Line 25 of that file is a curl call to example.com whose `-u` argument is the shell text `you:$(cat token.txt)`, a command substitution read at run time, in the rule packs' false-positive corpus (`TestFalsePositiveCorpus`); no credential exists. (This paragraph describes the line instead of quoting it: a quote tripped the same rule in `docs/DESIGN.md` on 2026-10-20, and a doc is never allowlisted.) Every other default rule stays active on the file: in a scratchpad copy with a random high-entropy fake of another rule appended, a bare `useDefault` config reported both lines and the real config reported only the appended one (2026-10-20).

**Local run (2026-10-20, this Mac, the darwin_arm64 build of 8.30.1, from the worktree root).** Under a bare config holding only `[extend] useDefault = true`, passed with `--config`, each mode reported exactly one finding, `curl-auth-user` on that file (lines 21 to 25), exit 1. Leaving `--config` off is not the same run: gitleaks auto-loads `<target>/.gitleaks.toml` when the flag is absent and then finds nothing (checked 2026-10-20). With the repo's config:

```
gitleaks git --redact --no-banner --exit-code 1 --config .gitleaks.toml .
  33 commits scanned. / scanned ~4.83 MB / no leaks found   (exit 0)
gitleaks dir --redact --no-banner --exit-code 1 --config .gitleaks.toml .
  scanned ~4.79 MB / no leaks found   (exit 0)
```

**First CI run (2026-10-21).** CI run 39520861406, on the merge of Plan 07 Tasks 4, 3 and 1, ran the job for the first time: green, which with `--exit-code 1` means no leaks over the history it fetched. Task 2's files (`docs/DENIED.md`, `scripts/denied-study.sh`, `scripts/denied-study.py`) and the close were committed after that run and were not in it. Two local runs cover them, each with gitleaks 8.30.1 darwin_arm64, the tarball checked with `shasum -a 256 -c` against the release's `gitleaks_8.30.1_checksums.txt`, from the repository root with `--config .gitleaks.toml`. The final review's run on 2026-10-21, over the Task 2 merge: `git` mode over 50 commits, no leaks; `dir` mode two findings, both in gitignored study notes, none in a tracked file. The close's run on 2026-10-21, over the fix-wave branch in its worktree: `git` mode, no leaks; `dir` mode, no leaks (the worktree holds no gitignored notes). CONTRIBUTING "Development" names the same local command.

### Known limits

1. **`rules explain` evaluates the text it is given exactly as the hook would.** Every hook non-match of section 8 applies, to the verdict and to the `note:` line alike, and the verdict is the hook's, not Claude Code's (see "`rules explain`" above).
2. **`rules explain` writes one cache a hook call would not.** It refreshes the Claude Code rules cache (`claude-rules-cache.json`, 0600, in the state dir) for every Bash command, where a hook call for a plain command without a rewrite does not (Task 1 review, note c; left by ruling).
3. **The non-Bash guard on the note is pinned for Read only.** `TestExplainNamesNativeRuleWithoutRewrite` asserts that a Read names no rule; an MCP tool whose input carries a `command` field is not tested (Task 1 review, note b; left by ruling).
4. **Under shadow mode `--expect` follows the observed verdict.** `--expect allow` exits 0 for a call that enforce mode would deny; one stderr line now says so, but a loop that reads only the exit still passes it.
5. **A model-typed `rules explain` leaves no activity record.** The guard treats it as read-only and the call touches no skill, so by the standing rule it is not recorded, and a model can try spellings against the live matcher without an item on Home. It changes no decision, the rules are readable anyway (`rules list --json`), and the call's text is in the transcript, though nothing marks it as a probe (final review M9; left unless the owner wants probes on Home, where the hook, not `Explain`, would write the line).
6. **The study is a small sample.** Two runs per model and scenario, 18 sessions, one Mac, two days and two Claude Code versions; most Opus rows are Opus 4.8 after a safeguards fallback.
7. **The study ran headless in auto mode only,** with prompts that authorised workarounds outright. In an interactive session a hook ask goes to a person; none was run.
8. **The study cannot separate the sandbox from the hook's instruction.** Every rewrite's response tells the model not to copy, pipe or retry the script, and all five sandbox-refused replies cite it.
9. **What the study found not stopped.** Three WebFetch calls went around a refused `curl` unrefused and failed only on WebFetch's HTTPS upgrade: a `Bash(curl *)` rule covers the Bash tool only. No WebFetch rule or watchdeck domain gate was tested.
10. **A headless model can read the hook's ask reason as a sandbox result.** The reason names the skill's profile, not that the call was asked about and did not run; a follow-up is to say so in the reason or the context.
11. **A compound command that names a skill script asks,** by design, and headless auto mode refuses the ask, so D7's "never an ask" did not hold for skill-net.
12. **The study's hook read the owner's Claude Code files.** The skill scanner roots at `$HOME/.claude`, so in the twelve sessions with a Bash call the hook and `watchdeck run` read the real personal skills directory and plugin registry, and the user settings files when that registry exists (reads only). A follow-up is a temp `HOME` for the hook alone.
13. **The study's watchdeck rule never got a call.** No model tried a shell route to the secret, so `study-secret-bash` refused nothing.
14. **The classifier's self-test has three uncovered edges:** an ask with no permission-denied event, "failed to start" judged from the header only, and a fallback flagged when the requested model is unknown. No figure in `docs/DENIED.md` rests on them, and the matrix had none of those shapes.
15. **The study script is a macOS record.** It uses `stat -f` and `sw_vers` and has not run on Linux.
16. **The installer's verify step is untested against a real release.** The release workflow signs and attests only when the repository is public, so the first public tag is the first real run of `gh attestation verify` and of the cosign line.
17. **The GitHub CLI path verifies the signer workflow, not the tag.** The cosign path pins the tag through the certificate identity; `--source-ref refs/tags/<tag>` (gh 2.100.0) is the one-flag hardening if the owner wants it, with the same flag in README and SECURITY.md.
18. **`gh auth status` fails for more than a sign-out.** Offline, an expired token or a bad second host all read as "gh is installed but not signed in"; the installer then falls back or refuses, so it fails closed with imprecise wording.
19. **A network failure on the bundle fetch reads as "an unsigned draft"** on the cosign path; the gh path reports whatever gh prints.
20. **The gitleaks allowlist path is end-anchored only.** `internal/rules/testdata/fp-corpus\.txt$` also matches any path with that suffix; the entry stays scoped to `curl-auth-user`, and the guard requires every entry to be scoped that way.
21. **The guard pins the job's text, not what runs.** A deliberate rewrite that keeps the pinned lines, such as a `shell:` override or an earlier step that swaps the binary or changes `RUNNER_TEMP`, passes it; that needs a reviewer.
22. **The guard reads TOML line by line** (a test, with no new dependency). It understands the shapes it allows and refuses anything else as an unreviewed table or key, such as a dotted key or an inline table, rather than parsing it.
23. **gitleaks auto-loads `<target>/.gitleaks.toml` when `--config` is absent,** so a local run without the flag silently inherits the allowlist.
24. **No `dir` scan in CI,** because a checkout has no untracked files, and CodeQL waits for the public flip.
