## 14. The control plane grows (Plan 08, 2026-10-24)

Four features the owner approved on 2026-10-24: leak watch, intent rules, temporary allows, and rule stats with a try box. Each has its own subsection, written by the task that built it; the close subsections are the integrator's. Plan: the private Plan 08 file. Nothing here lets a gate return allow; the hook's no-opinion fast path gains no file stat.

### Leak watch

Leak watch finds secret-shaped strings in session transcripts and lists each one in Needs you (built 2026-10-24). It is read-only: it scans what Claude Code already wrote, it never blocks a call, and the hook does not run it.

**Where it runs (D2).** The scan sits in the session index's parse pass (`internal/sessionindex`), which already reads every text of every session. The findings live on the index entry (`Entry.Findings`, `Entry.FindingsCapped`), in `meta.json` (0600, in the state dir that sandboxed scripts cannot read). The index format went from version 3 to 4, so the first run after the upgrade rebuilds every entry once (`TestMetaVersionFourRebuilds`). Each finding is `{pattern, label, where, tool, target, ref, agent, ts}`. `where` is `result` (a tool result), `reply` (the text of a model reply) or `input` (a tool call's input). `ref` is the tool use id for a result or an input and the assistant row's `uuid` for a reply, so two replies give two findings (`TestReplyFindingsHaveDistinctRefs`). `target` is at most 120 bytes: the file path for the file tools, the `path` of Grep and Glob, the program name for Bash, the host for WebFetch, else empty. A longer target is cut and the finding carries `target_cut: true`, which `Catalog.Leaks` passes on as `rulebook.Leak.TargetCut` (`TestFindingTargetCutFlag`, `TestCatalogLeaksCarryTargetCut`; Task 5 fix round 1). `agent` is the subagent id for a finding in a subagent file (`TestFindingsAgentFromSubagentFile`). One finding is kept per pattern, ref and place, and at most 50 per session; a 51st sets `findings_capped` (`TestFindingsDedupeAndCap`). Findings survive an incremental append (`TestFindingsSurviveIncrementalAppend`). The three places and their fields are checked by `TestFindingsFromResultReplyAndInput`.

**Patterns (D3).** `internal/leakscan` holds eight lexical patterns, in this order: `aws-access-key-id`, `private-key-pem`, `github-token`, `slack-token`, `anthropic-key`, `openai-key`, `stripe-live` and `aws-secret-key`. The first four are the cred-in-write pack's regexes and literals, and `TestPatternsMatchCredInWritePack` pins them equal. The Anthropic pattern is the `sk` `-ant-` prefix and 32 or more key characters. The OpenAI pattern is the project, service-account and admin prefixes and 32 or more. The Stripe pattern is a live secret or restricted key prefix and 16 or more. The AWS secret pattern is 40 key characters only after an `aws_secret_access_key` label, in any case. Every pattern has literal prefilters, and a regex runs only after a prefilter `strings.Contains` hits (`TestScanNoRegexWithoutPrefilter`: no regex runs over a text with no prefilter substring; the test builds 1 MiB, of which `Scan` reads the first 64 KiB). Placeholders, prose and an old bare `sk-` key do not match (`TestScanTable`). A finding never holds the matched text, and a target that is secret-shaped before or after its 120-byte cut is dropped (`TestLeakFindingsCarryNoSecret`: neither `meta.json` nor any field holds a key-shaped value). `TestLeakSurfacesCarryNoSecret` plants all eight kinds in one session, one as the Bash program and one inside a file path long enough that the cut lands inside the key. It then checks that no key and no key's first 9 bytes appear in `/api/sessions`, the session detail, `Catalog.Leaks`, the inbox items built from it, or `/api/needs`. The search text in `text/<sha256(id)>.txt` holds the same bytes as before; leak watch adds no copy.

**What is scanned (D5).** Tool results, model replies and tool inputs. A prompt the person typed is not scanned: a person pasting their own key is not the agent leaking it. Thinking and hook output are not scanned either (`TestFindingsFromResultReplyAndInput` puts a key in each of the three and finds none there). Summaries and system rows are not scanned, by the same code path; no test covers them.

**To the inbox (D4).** `sessionsapi` adds `leaks` (a count) to every session row and the findings to the session detail's `risk.leaks`, with targets in `~` form (`TestRowAndDetailLeaks`). `Catalog.Leaks(since)` turns them into `rulebook.Leak` values (`TestCatalogLeaksSince`). `rulebook.Open` makes one item of kind `leak` per leak, with the id `sha256(session NUL ref NUL "leak")[:16]`. Its What is the tool and target ("Reply" for a model reply), its Why "<label> appeared in the <where>", its authority `leak`, and it has no rule ids (`TestInboxLeakItems`); its fixes are under "On Home" below. Unlike every other item, a leak item stays listed when its fix is already a rule in force, because the leak has already happened and a rule does not undo it (`TestInboxLeakNotHiddenByExistingRule`; Plan 08 final review). A dismiss suppresses by signature, so a later leak of the same pattern from the same tool, target and place stays out; another pattern does not. The dashboard gets the leaks through `ui.Options.Leaks`, so `/api/needs` lists leak items even with the activity log off (`TestNeedsListsLeakItemsWithoutActivity`), and a dismiss hides them (`TestNeedsLeakDismiss`). Under `--no-transcripts` the option is nil and the catalog has no index, so there is no leak watch (`TestCatalogLeaksOffWithoutTranscripts`).

**On Home (Task 5, 2026-10-24).** A leak row shows the authority chip "Secret seen", the item's Why, and an "Open in timeline" link to its call (the session's timeline with the call's node selected; a reply leak has no tool use id, so the link opens the timeline with nothing selected). A leak from one of the file tools (Read, Write, Edit, MultiEdit, NotebookEdit) gets a deny on reading that file as its one-click fix, whichever tool it came from, because the secret now sits in that file and reading it again is what would show it. The path's every-route form is its second fix (`TestSuggestLeakPathFix`; the every-route fix is described under "Intent rules"). A Grep or Glob leak gets no fix, because its target is the search root, not the file that held the secret. A Bash, WebFetch or reply leak gets none either. A leak whose target the index cut shows the target ending in "…" and gets no fix, since a rule on the cut path would match nothing. Each of these is acknowledged or dismissed. `rulebook.Open` drops a leak target that is itself secret-shaped, so a `Leak` a caller hands it without going through the index cannot put a key into What or a fix. `TestNeedsLeakItemCarriesNoSecret` feeds `/api/needs` such a leak through `ui.Options.Leaks` and finds neither the value nor its first 9 bytes in the answer; with the drop removed it fails. Leak items show with the activity log off, under a line that says only secrets seen in transcripts can show. `/api/needs` carries `leak_watch`, false under `--no-transcripts`, and the empty state then says "Leak watch is off because transcripts are off." Home reloads Needs you on the catalog's `index` event, so a new finding shows within the catalog's refresh. The `leak` authority word stays out of `AUTHORITY_ALL`: the map never draws it and its `?authority=` filter refuses it. `TestHomeWiresLeaksAndAllow` pins the page wiring. When Task 5 ended the walk's fixture held no leak; Task 7 added one, and the walk now checks the row and its timeline link ("Fixtures and walk" below). The row was checked by hand on 2026-10-24 against a temp fixture home with a fake Slack-shaped token, built by concatenation, appended to one session: the row, its fixes and its timeline link rendered, axe found nothing in either colour scheme, and the token was nowhere on the page.

**Limits.** These are designed, not tested, unless a test is named above.

- The scan is lexical. A key with an unknown prefix, a JWT, an old bare `sk-` key or a high-entropy string is not found, and a documented example key in a doc or test is found and has to be dismissed.
- Each text is scanned up to its first 64 KiB (`TestScanCapsAtMaxBlock`). A tool input is scanned string by string, so a key past the first 64 KiB of one large Write content is missed.
- Lines over 2 MiB are skipped by the transcript reader, and the head of a file read from its tail (over 32 MiB) is read for metadata only, so neither is scanned.
- A result whose tool use was read in an earlier pass shows the tool "unknown" and no target until the next rebuild.
- A result and an input finding on one tool use share an item id, so Needs you shows one item for that call, the newer finding's. Resolving that item hides later findings with other patterns on the same call too.
- A reply's ref depends on the row's `uuid`; a row without one falls back to its timestamp.
- When one tool input holds two different patterns in two different fields, the order of those two findings can change between rebuilds (the input's fields are walked in map order). The set of findings and the item ids do not change.
- The first run after the upgrade rebuilds the whole index once.
- A new finding reaches Needs you on the catalog's next index refresh, up to 15 s.
- `watchdeck inbox` and `watchdeck status` read the activity log only, so they do not list leak items yet.
- A leak from a path over 120 bytes, and a Grep or Glob leak, offer no rule to add; the person adds one on the Rules page. An index built by a version 4 binary before the `target_cut` field existed has no flag on its cut targets until the entry is rebuilt; no release ever wrote such an index.

### Intent rules

An intent is one thing a person means, such as "never read this file", "no network to this host" or "never run this command". It is written as every concrete rule that covers a route to it, under one intent id, and removed as one. A request asks for it with `AddRequest.Everywhere` (`everywhere` in JSON, `rules add --everywhere` on the command line). The intent id is `intent:<what>:<hex8>`, where hex8 is fnv32a of the value as typed (`rulebook.IntentFor`). Every step of the plan carries that id. The native strings are claimed under it in the ownership ledger. The policy file has no ledger, so the hook rule is named `intent-<what>-<hex8>-bash` and the tool subject `intent-<what>-<hex8>`, and `List` reads membership back from those names. `Apply` keeps a hook rule id only when it is exactly the id of its step's own intent; any other id is recomputed as `user-<hex8>` as before (`TestApplyKeepsIntentRuleID`, `TestApplyRecomputesHookRuleID`).

| What | Native settings | Tool subject | Bash hook rule |
|---|---|---|---|
| path | `Read(<a>)` and `Edit(<a>)`, anchored as a plain path rule is | Grep and Glob, `paths` on the gate spelling | literals: the value as typed, with the home directory as `~`, and with `~` expanded |
| host | `WebFetch(domain:<h>)`; for deny also `<h>` in `sandbox.network.deniedDomains` | WebFetch, `domains` on `<h>` | `argv0` of 22 network-capable programs (curl, wget, nc, ssh, git, python and others), literal `<h>`, regex `(?i)` and the quoted host |
| command | `Bash(<prefix> *)` | none | the existing twin (literal prefix) |

An ask intent writes ask lists in place of deny lists. The sandbox's domain list has no ask, so an ask host intent has no `deniedDomains` step. The gate spelling of a path is `~/<rel>` under the home directory, else the absolute path; a project-scope relative path is joined to the project root first. A relative path at user scope is refused with `--everywhere`, because the native rule names a different file in every working directory and the only gate form for that, `**/<rel>`, would make every Grep and Glob in every project ask. A project-scope intent writes its native strings to the project's `.claude/settings.json` and its subject and hook rule to the project's policy file; a deny or ask only narrows, which the project file may do. Only intent steps may go to the project policy file; pack rule actions and MCP launches still go to the user file only (`TestProjectScopeIntentWritesProjectFiles`). Regex, text, MCP tool, MCP launch, pack rule and skill requests are refused with `--everywhere`, with a reason that holds no value (`TestProposeEverywhereRefusesOtherWhat`).

`Apply` tries every policy file edit on the loaded file before any write, so an edit the policy file's own check refuses writes nothing (`TestApplyRefusedPolicyEditWritesNothing`). The new tool subject step has its own branch; before it, such a step would have been written as an MCP launch subject. `sandbox.network.deniedDomains` is written only for a plain host and only under a host intent (`TestApplyRefusesDeniedDomainsWithoutHostIntent`). Settings writes keep one `ManageKeys` call per file, so one backup per file, and the policy file is saved once (`TestApplyEverywherePathWritesThreeMechanisms`, `TestApplyHostWritesDeniedDomains`).

`Remove(row)` still removes one row, even a row of an intent (`TestRemoveRowKeepsRestOfIntent`, `TestRulesRemoveRowStaysOneRow`). A `domain:` row is now removable when the ledger shows an everywhere intent claims it; a domain the person wrote is still `ErrNotOwned`. `RemoveIntent(in, id)` drops the intent's claim on every ledger string, one `ManageKeys` per settings file, so a string another rule also claims stays. It deletes the intent's subject and hook rule from the policy file of the intent's scope, all under the settings lock. The policy files are loaded and edited in memory before any settings write, so one that cannot be read stops the removal with nothing written. A project's policy file is never read for a user-scope intent, so a malformed `.watchdeck.yaml` in a cloned repository cannot block the removal of the person's own intent (`TestRemoveIntentMalformedProjectFileWritesNothing`). `Removed.RowIDs` lists the rows that went (`TestRemoveIntentRemovesEveryRow`, `TestRemoveIntentKeepsSharedClaim`). The routes are `POST /api/rules {nonce, remove_intent}` (`TestRulesRemoveIntent`) and `rules remove --intent <id>`, which lists every row and asks first (`TestRulesRemoveIntentAsksAndRemovesAll`). An inbox Undo of an everywhere add removes every row it wrote: the intent's rows leave `List`, the ledger holds no claim for it, the policy file holds no `intent-` rule or subject, and the settings object equals the one before the add once empty arrays, and the objects they leave empty, are dropped (`TestUndoEverywhereFixRestoresFiles`). Byte equality cannot hold, because `Manage` leaves an emptied array in place and `policy.Save` re-renders the policy file.

On Home (Task 5, 2026-10-24), `rulebook.Suggest` follows a path or host fix with its every-route form: the same value with `everywhere` set, and for a path the tool left to its default, so reading and changing are both covered. That form is never the first fix, so the one-click fix stays one rule (D8). An item with a pack rule fix first lists the pack rule, the plain rule and then the every-route rule. A Bash item keeps its one ask, because its every-route form is the twin Propose suggests, and an MCP item gets none (`TestSuggestEverywhereSecondForWebFetch`, `TestSuggestTable`). Needs you shows the every-route fix as its own button, "…and every other route". Its confirm lists every step's sentence and every file it writes, from `fixes[].steps` and `fixes[].files` in `/api/needs`. `rulebook.StepSentence` spells home as `~` in a hook rule's literals and drops repeats, so the answer holds no home directory. The add_rule resolution of an every-route fix records `intent_id`. An Undo of it removes the intent the way `RemoveIntent` does, through the same code under Undo's lock, rather than row by row. Only the intent's own claims are released, so a rule the person had added by itself on the same string stays, and an intent the person already removed is no error (`TestNeedsEverywhereFixAndUndo`). A path every-route add answers with the same Grep and Glob `warning` as `POST /api/rules`, and the page shows it.

`List` sets `Rule.Intent` (`id`, `sentence`, `rows`) on every row of an intent: native rows by the ledger claim, policy rows by their names (`TestListShowsIntentOnEveryRow`). The sentence names the value from the best row left: the hook rule's first literal, which is the value as typed, then a tool gate entry, then the native string's inner value. For example: "Block reading or changing ~/.env, by every route". With none of those left it reads "Rules added together to cover every route".

Limits:

- **Skill scripts are not covered** (D6). A skill's grant only widens, there is no sandbox deny path a person can add, and the sandbox's network setting is `none`, `localhost` or `all`. A skill script that reads the path or reaches the host is held only by its own grant.
- **`sandbox.network.deniedDomains` is designed, not tested against Claude Code** (D7). Nothing has shown that Claude Code's sandbox honours a value watchdeck wrote, and the intent's own hook rule refuses a `curl` naming the host before the sandbox could.
- **The hook rule's literals are text, not paths.** A bare `.env` literal also matches `.envrc`. A command that spells the home directory as `$HOME` or `${HOME}` is not matched, because the shell expands it after the hook has looked. A glob in the value (`~/.ssh/*.pem`) is matched as written and matches no real command. The expanded literal writes the absolute home directory into the policy file. A literal is found anywhere in the command, so `bash -c 'cat ~/.env'` is matched (`TestIntentHookRuleMatches`).
- **The tool subject holds only once the hook is registered for its tools.** A settings install registers Grep, Glob and WebFetch only when the policy has rules for them. A path intent added after the install therefore leaves Grep and Glob unwatched until `watchdeck enforce install` runs again. The add says so: the dashboard's answer carries a `warning` and the command line prints a note, unless a watchdeck group in a settings file Claude Code reads here already names both tools, or the plugin is active (`rulebook.SearchWatchWarning`, `settings.UnhookedTools`; `TestRulesAddPathIntentWarnsAboutSearchHook` for the route and the command, `TestUnhookedTools`). The plugin case has no test of its own. `watchdeck doctor` names the missing registration too. The plugin registers Grep and Glob already, and Claude Code's own WebFetch rule covers a host intent's WebFetch route either way: with the hook off WebFetch, `WebFetch(domain:127.0.0.1)` refused a model's fetch in a headless session on 2026-10-25 UTC (DENIED.md, "The WebFetch route").
- **The host rule needs a known program in command position.** A program not in the list, a host built at run time, or a program inside `bash -c '…'` or `sh -c "…"` is not matched. The host regex has no name boundary, so it also refuses `notfake-example.com` and `fake-example.com.other.test` for a `fake-example.com` intent: it refuses more than the host, never less (`TestIntentHookRuleMatches`).
- **An intent id carries no scope.** The same value typed at user and at project scope gives one id; `RemoveIntent` then removes both. A project intent's rules in another project's files are named in the error, not removed.
- **A project policy file write leaves `.watchdeck.yaml.bak-<ts>` beside it** in the repository root, as a project settings write leaves its own backup.
- **A plain `remove` of an unowned `domain:` row now answers 400, not 409,** on the dashboard, because the page still classes domain rows as the person's to edit.

### Temporary allows

**What it is (D9, D10).** A person can pause one of watchdeck's own rules (a built-in pack rule or a rule of their policy file) for an hour, for eight hours or for one session, and can widen one skill's sandbox grant for up to eight hours. Both cover watchdeck's own enforcement only. Claude Code's native rules and auto mode's rules cannot be paused: `rulebook.Pause` refuses any row other than `pack:…` and `user:…` and writes nothing: a native or auto mode row with "Claude Code's own rule; change it in Claude Code", a skill's grant row with "a skill's sandbox grant; use watchdeck allow <skill> --for 1h", any other policy-file subject with "remove it instead" (all three match `ErrNotPausable`). Nothing here emits allow. A paused rule has no opinion, exactly like a rule switched off, for a while; a timed grant may never carry `auto_allow` (R8), so the rewrite of a skill under a timed grant still asks. `TestGatesNeverAllow`, `FuzzGatesNeverAllow` and `TestShadowNeverAllows` are unchanged and green.

**Schema.** The user policy file gains a top-level `temporary:` list, rendered after `subjects:` in file order:

```yaml
temporary:
  - id: "t-0a1b2c3d"
    kind: rule
    rule: "user/no-prod-db"        # "<pack>/<id>"; pack "user" for a policy-file rule (R9)
    session: "s1"                  # optional; "" pauses every session
    until: "2026-10-24T18:00:00Z"  # exclusive
    added: "2026-10-24T10:00:00Z"
  - id: "t-4e5f6071"
    kind: grant
    skill: "web"
    grant:
      network: all                 # network, write, read, env, caches; never auto_allow
    until: "2026-10-24T11:00:00Z"
    added: "2026-10-24T10:00:00Z"
```

`id` is `t-` and the fnv32a hex of kind, rule, skill, session and added, NUL-separated (`manifest.TemporaryID`; until is not part of it). A second timed grant for the same skill written within the same second would share that id, so `GrantFor` then takes `manifest.TemporaryIDN(t, n)`, which folds in the first n that no other entry uses (`TestGrantForTwiceInOneSecond`; Plan 08 final review). Times are RFC 3339; the parser keeps them in UTC to the second and Render writes them that way, so a file round-trips byte for byte. The strict parser refuses, with value-free errors: a missing or malformed id, a duplicate id, a kind other than rule or grant, a rule entry without a rule key or with a skill or grant, a grant entry without a skill (or with `*`), without a non-empty grant, with a session, or with `auto_allow` (true or false), a grant path that names a built-in deny, a bad session id, an until or added that is not RFC 3339, an until not after added, and an until more than 8 hours after added on a session-bound pause or a grant. A project `.watchdeck.yaml` may not hold the key at all: `manifest.LoadOverrides` and `policy.LoadProjectFile` refuse such a file, and `policy.NewAt` drops a project layer's entries with a warning. `policy.CacheVersion` is 3, so a version 2 cache holding "unknown field" for a file with temporaries is discarded.

**Semantics.** `policy.NewAt(now, user, project)` keeps the user file's entries in force at now (now before until); `New` is `NewAt(time.Now(), …)`. `LoadCached` calls it on every load, a cache hit included, so a temporary ends on the first hook call after its until: nothing reaps, and the no-opinion path gains no file stat and no init work (the cache holds the parsed file, expired entries included). `CacheMatchesFresh` (doctor) uses one clock for both loads, so an entry expiring between them cannot make it flap. An expired entry stays in the file until the next `policy.Save`, which drops it from what it writes; every watchdeck write goes through Save.

- **Paused rules.** In the gate stage, a pack or policy-file rule hit for which `(*Policy).Paused(pack, id, session)` finds an entry (every session, or `in.SessionID`'s) is kept in the record's rules with action `log` and raises nothing. A call whose only hits are paused is a `rule_log` record with `temporary` set; beside a hit that is not paused, the decision (or, under shadow mode, the shadow record) carries `temporary` too. `temporary` is the ids, sorted and comma-joined, because one call can hit several paused rules (R10). A paused prompt-injection rule adds no context after WebFetch or WebSearch ran; its hit is a `rule_log` with `temporary`. Subagents share their session's id, so a session pause covers them.
- **Resumed sessions.** A session-bound pause has until = added + 8 h (the backstop): Claude Code keeps a resumed session's id, so the pause would otherwise last as long as the id. Within those eight hours a resumed session is still paused. Pausing the rule again for the session restarts the backstop: the new entry's added is now.
- **Timed grants (R7).** `manifest.SkillOverridesAt(now, f)` folds the grant entries in force into `Skills[name]` by widening (network the larger, write, read and env appended, caches or'ed) and sets that block's `auto_allow` to false while any grant temporary for the skill is in force; the skill's own user block is the last step of the grant chain, so the skill asks even when its permanent grant, the defaults or `*` auto-allow it (fix round 1, 2026-10-24; `TestTimedGrantNeverAutoAllows`); `SkillOverrides()` is `SkillOverridesAt(time.Now(), f)`, so every consumer that reads a grant through `manifest.LoadOverrides` or `policy.SkillGrant` sees it: the hook's rewrite, `runner.Resolve` (the OS sandbox profile), audit, the map. Writers read `BaseSkillOverrides()` instead, so `watchdeck allow` and the dashboard's grant edit never save a timed grant into the permanent one (`TestApplySkillKeepsTemporaryOutOfGrant`). A grant is timed only: `watchdeck run` carries no session id.

**Writes.** `policy.Edit{AddTemporary, EndTemporary}` add an entry (id filled when empty; the same entry again is no change, another entry with its id an error) or remove one; both go through `policy.Apply` then `policy.Save` with a `.bak-<ts>` first. `rulebook.Pause(in, rowIDs, forSpec)` takes `1h`, `8h` or `session=<id>`, writes one temporary per distinct rule with the same until in one write (replacing an earlier pause of the same rule for the same sessions), and refuses an unknown pack or user rule (the project file is read with the untrusted loader). `rulebook.GrantFor(in, skill, edits, d)` takes `UngrantedEdit{Skill, Field, Value}` edits for one skill, at most 8 h, checks paths the way `watchdeck allow` does (through `manifest.Apply` with the account home) and refuses `auto_allow`. `rulebook.End(in, id)` removes one entry or, with `all`, every one; an id not in the file is `ErrNoTemporary`. `rulebook.ListTemporaries(in)` lists the entries in force with a sentence. `rulebook.PauseRowID` turns an inbox rule id (`<pack>/<id>`, `user/<id>`) into the row id Pause takes; a `claude-settings/…` id has none.

**Inbox (R11).** `skill_request` and `sandbox_refusal` items carry `ungranted`: one edit per item of a decision record's ungranted list whose field is network, write, read, env or caches, and `network all` for each skill of a run whose hints include a network refusal. A write or read hint gives nothing: its line is the script's own output and is never read as a policy value.

**Surfaces.** `GET /api/rules` adds `temporaries` (never null); `POST /api/rules {nonce, end_temporary: <id|all>}` ends one under the rules nonce (400 without a nonce, 403 for a stale one, 400 for an id not in the file). CLI: `watchdeck rules pause <row-id>... --for 1h|8h|session=<id> [--yes]` and `rules resume <id|all> [--yes]` confirm before writing; `rules pause --list` only reads. `watchdeck allow <skill> --network …|--write …|--caches --for <duration up to 8h>` writes a timed grant and refuses `--auto`, `--no-auto` and every narrowing flag. The model-typed guard treats `rules pause --list` (alone, or followed only by a new command) as read-only; `rules pause … --for`, `rules resume` and `allow … --for` ask (`TestModelTypedMutatingCommandsAsk`, `TestModelTypedReadOnlyCommandsPass`).

**Verified (2026-10-24, this Mac).** `TestParseTemporaryRoundTrip`, `TestTemporaryStrictErrors`, `TestSkillOverridesAtFoldsTemporaries`, `TestTemporaryIDStable`, `TestNewAtDropsExpired`, `TestPausedMatchesSessionOrAny`, `TestPolicyCacheVersionIsThree`, `TestSavePrunesExpiredTemporaries`, `TestApplyAddAndEndTemporary`, `TestApplySkillKeepsTemporaryOutOfGrant`, temporary rows in `TestErrorsCarryNoValues` and a `FuzzParsePolicyFile` seed; in the hook `TestPausedRuleHasNoOpinion`, `TestPauseExpiryAndSession`, `TestPausedUnderShadowStillLogsTemporary`, `TestPausedPostToolUseWarnsNothing`, `TestTemporaryGrantWidensRewriteAndRunner`, `TestTemporaryGrantExpiredNarrows`, `TestTimedGrantNeverAutoAllows`, and a paused and an expired row in `TestExplainMatchesDecide`; in rulebook `TestPauseWritesOneTemporaryPerRow`, `TestPauseRefusesNativeRowWritesNothing`, `TestInboxItemsCarryUngranted`, `TestGrantForWritesTimedGrant`, `TestGrantForRefusesAutoAllow`, `TestEndRemovesEntry`; `TestRulesListsTemporaries`, `TestRulesEndTemporaryNeedsNonce`; `TestRulesPauseAndResume`, `TestRulesPauseListReadOnly`, `TestAllowForWritesTemporaryGrant`. `TestTemporaryGrantWidensRewriteAndRunner` checks that `runner.Resolve` builds a different profile with network all; no script ran under that profile in a test (designed, not tested end to end).

**Known limits.** During a timed grant the skill always asks, even if it has `auto_allow`; the permanent `auto_allow` returns when the grant ends. A paused rule still counts as watching its tool when a file tool's path cannot be normalised, so such a call still asks (fail closed). `TestTemporaryGrantWidensRewriteAndRunner` and `TestTemporaryGrantExpiredNarrows` have run on macOS only so far. A pause on `user/<id>` also pauses a project-file rule with the same id, since both report under pack `user`. The skill drill-in and `GET /api/overrides` show a timed grant folded into the grant without its until (R7), and `watchdeck allow` prints the folded grant. A paused hit is recorded with action `log`, so rule stats count it as a log hit unless the close decides otherwise. Expiry follows the wall clock: a clock set back lengthens a pause. `allow --for` widens network, write and caches; read and env are reachable only through `GrantFor`. `rules explain` runs with its own session id, so it shows a timed pause but never a session-bound one. Removing a rule (`rules remove user:<id>`) leaves its pause in the file until it expires; it then pauses nothing. Pausing a rule that is already paused for the same sessions replaces the earlier entry from now (`TestPauseWritesOneTemporaryPerRow`), so an Undo of the second pause does not bring the first back.

**On Home (Task 5, 2026-10-24).** `GET /api/needs` gives each item `pausable` and `allow_for`, which is never null, from `rulebook.AllowFor`. An ask qualifies when every rule id on it maps through `PauseRowID` (a pack or policy-file rule) and its record names no path, domain, MCP or agent rule. It gets `session` when it names a session, and `1h`. A skill request or sandbox refusal with `ungranted` gets `1h` only, and nothing else gets either (`TestNeedsPausableFlag`). The page shows "Allow for this session" and "Allow for an hour" only from these fields. Each goes through `writeWithConfirm`, whose confirm names the rules to be paused or what the skill gets, the duration, and the policy file (`allow_file`). `POST /api/needs/{id} {nonce, action: "allow", for: "session"|"1h"}` spends the needs nonce, as add_rule does, and `for` is checked before the spend. An ask has every rule on it paused, one temporary per rule with the same until, and `session` means `session=<the item's session>` (R10; `TestNeedsAllowSessionPausesEveryRule`). A skill item has its edits granted for an hour, one `GrantFor` per skill it names. When one fails, those already written are ended again (`TestNeedsAllowHourGrantsSkill`). An item that `AllowFor` does not offer that while for gets a 409 whose error says why, with a fresh nonce, and nothing is written. Auto mode's denial, a person's no, and an ask carrying a Claude Code settings rule get "Claude Code's own rule; change it in Claude Code" (D9; `TestNeedsAllowRefusedForNativeDenial`). A skill asked for one session, an ask decided by a path, domain, MCP or agent rule, an ask with no rule, and a leak each get their own sentence (`TestAllowForAndRefusals`). So does an ask naming a rule that is no longer there: a policy-file rule removed since the call, or one in a project's `.watchdeck.yaml`, which Home does not read because it names no project. That answer names the rule and is a 409, not a 500 (`TestAllowRefusesGoneRule`, `TestNeedsAllowGoneRuleIs409`). The resolution is `{action: "allow", temporary_ids}`, and it closes the item. Undo ends all of those temporaries in one write of the policy file, so one backup (`TestUndoAllowIsOneWrite`), and reopens the item. A temporary that is already gone, because it was ended on the Rules page, pruned or replaced by a later pause, counts as ended (`TestNeedsAllowUndoEndsTemporaries`). The page's 8 s Undo toast posts that undo.

Home limits (designed, not tested, unless a test is named). An ask item's rule ids hold every hit, a `log` hit too, so an allow also pauses a log-only rule beside the one that asked; a paused log rule changes nothing. A rule already paused at least as long as the allow asks is left alone (Task 5 fix round 1): for this session, a pause bound to that session or one for every session lasting the eight-hour backstop; for an hour, a pause for every session lasting the hour. So a longer pause the person set on the Rules page is neither shortened nor ended by the allow's Undo, and an item whose every rule is already paused that long is a 409 that says so (`TestNeedsAllowSkipsLongerPause`). A shorter pause for every session is still replaced by the hour's, and an Undo then ends it. An ask decided by a path, domain, MCP or agent rule is told apart only by the record's `subject`, a non-skill subject; an ask raised with no rule hit (an invalid policy file) has no rule ids and no allow buttons. An ask raised because a file tool's path could not be normalised, beside a pack rule hit, still offers the allow, and the paused call then still asks (the fail-closed limit above). The walk's keyboard pass opens an allow confirm and cancels it (Task 7, "Fixtures and walk" below); no walk step accepts one. The allow on a pausable ask, its confirm, the Undo and the every-route confirm were checked by hand on 2026-10-24 against a temp fixture home, with axe clean in both colour schemes.

### Rule stats and the try box

Two read-only dashboard routes for the Rules page (Plan 08 Task 4, 2026-10-24): how often each rule fired, and what the hook would answer for one call the person types.

Rule stats (D11). `GET /api/rules/stats[?project=]` answers `{activity_enabled, window: {since, until, records, rotated}, pack: {"<pack>/<id>": Stat}, user: {"<id>": Stat}, native: {"<value>": Stat}}`, each `Stat` being `{fired, last_ts, last_action, shadow}`. The counts come from the rule hits on activity records: a pack rule by `<pack>/<id>`, the person's own rule by its id, and a Claude Code Bash deny or ask rule by the rule as written (`Bash(curl *)`). Those Claude Code hits exist only when the hook checked the rule before a sandbox rewrite (section 8), so a plain `curl x` that Claude Code refused by itself is not counted. Every other Claude Code rule, the auto-mode rows and the sandbox rows get no count. A record counts once per rule id, however often the id appears in it. A `rule_log` record counts with `last_action` `log`, and so does a paused rule's hit, which the hook records as a `log` hit: the rule matched, though it changed nothing. A shadow-mode record adds to `shadow` as well as `fired`, and its `last_action` is the action that was observed. `last_ts` and `last_action` come from the newest record by its timestamp, not the last line in the file. The window says what the counts cover: the oldest and newest record time (`""` when there is none), the number of records read, and `rotated`, true when `<log>.1` exists, which means anything older than `since` is gone. The log holds about 8 to 16 MiB of history in its two files, with no date bound. The log is global, so `?project=` is only checked the way every project-scoped route checks it (an unknown project is a 400); counts are never filtered by directory. With the log off (`WATCHDECK_ACTIVITY=off`) the answer is 200, `activity_enabled: false`, an empty window and empty maps.

How the fold works. The fold lives on the `Server` under a mutex and reads by offset with `activity.ReadFrom`, the way the session catalog does. It never keys a cache on the log's size or mtime, because the mtime moves on every hook call and such a cache would miss on every page load during a live session. Each request folds only what was appended since the last one. It starts over from offset 0, which reads `<log>.1` and then the current file, in two cases. The first is `ReadFrom` reporting `Reset`, meaning the file shrank in place. The second is the file at the log's path no longer being the one the fold read: the hook renamed it to `<log>.1` and began another. That second check is by file identity (`os.SameFile`), so a new file that has already grown past the old offset is not mistaken for the old one. Either way each record in the two files counts once. Tests: `TestStatsFoldsPackUserAndNative`, `TestStatsAcrossRotation` (a new file longer than the old offset, a poll while only `<log>.1` exists, a second rotation that drops the oldest file; it fails with the identity check removed), `TestStatsResetRefolds` and `TestStatsActivityOff`.

The try box (D12). `POST /api/rules/try[?project=]` with `{what, value, mode?}` runs `hook.Explain` for one synthetic PreToolUse call. `what` is one of `command`, `read`, `edit`, `write` (a Write; the CLI's `--path`), `url` (a WebFetch), `mcp` (the tool name as Claude Code spells it, `mcp__<server>__<tool>`, with an empty input; any other spelling is a 400) or `skill`. `mode` is Claude Code's permission mode and defaults to `default`. The answer is the document `watchdeck rules explain --json` prints plus `project_scope`, as one flat object. The builder moved from the CLI into `internal/hook`, as `hook.Doc`, `hook.BuildDoc(in, out, e)` and `hook.RuleID(h)`, so the CLI and the route print the same document. `TestExplainJSONUnchangedByMove` pins the CLI's bytes for a deny, an ask and a no-opinion call; it passed before and after the move. The call carries session id `try` and tool use id `toolu_try`. Its cwd is the project root when the request has `?project=`, and `""` otherwise; `project_scope` is true only in the first case. The route drops `CLAUDE_PROJECT_DIR` from the environment it hands the hook, so the Claude Code project rules are the named project's, not those of whatever session started the dashboard. A policy file that cannot be loaded gives the hook's own answer: a 200 with verdict `ask` and the policy reason, never a 500. A body that is not one `{what, value, mode?}`, an unknown `what` or `mode`, an empty value or an unknown project is a 400. The route takes no nonce because it writes nothing of its own; the guard's Origin, Sec-Fetch-Site, JSON, body-cap and bearer checks still apply. One mutex serialises `Explain`, because its cold skill-index build is synchronous and two runs at once in one process are untested. One 3 s deadline covers waiting for that mutex and the run (R15). A request not answered in time gets a 503 with `{error: hook.SlowReason}`, the hook's own words; a run that outlives its request keeps the mutex until it returns, and a request that cannot get the mutex within its own deadline gets the same 503. The handler calls an unexported `explain` field on the `Server`, which defaults to `hook.Explain`, so a test can make it slow. Like `rules explain`, a try appends nothing to the activity log, the sessions log, the inbox or any policy or settings file. It does refresh the policy cache, the skill index cache and the Claude Code rules cache, as a hook call would. Tests: `TestTryVerdicts` (a pack deny, a no-opinion read, a skill script's rewrite that asks, a `?project=` call that picks up the project's Claude Code ask rule, and each `what`, each response equal to `hook.BuildDoc` of a direct `Explain`; an invalid policy file is a 200 ask), `TestTryWritesNothing`, `TestTryDeadline` (a stub that never returns: 503 within 3.5 s for the first request and for a second one sent 200 ms later, and the stub ran once), `TestTryRejectsUnknownWhat` and `TestTryNoNonceButSameOrigin`.

On the Rules page (Plan 08 Task 6, 2026-10-24). A caption under the page head says what the counts cover: "Counts since <ago>, <N> records", with a line when the log has rotated and, when the page lists a Claude Code Bash block or ask rule, the note that such a rule counts only the calls watchdeck checked before sandboxing a skill script; with the log off it says "Activity log is off" and no row gets a count. A chip ("fired N · last <ago>"; "matched N · last <ago>, noted only" when the newest hit only noted the call, as a paused or log rule does; or "never fired") sits on every built-in rule row and every row of your own rules, joined by `<pack>/<id>` and by id, and on each Claude Code Bash block or ask row, joined by the rule as written, with the same note in its hover text. Rows that are off, auto-mode rows, sandbox rows and every other Claude Code row get no chip (D11). The page head holds the "Try a call" box before Add rule: a choice of the seven kinds of call, a field, a Try button and a result card. The card shows the verdict as a tag (allowed, asks first, blocked) or the line "no watchdeck rule applies; Claude Code decides as usual", the reason, each matched rule as a link that scrolls to its row and outlines it for three seconds, the matching Claude Code Bash rule watchdeck did not check, the sandbox command in monospace, what shadow mode would have done, and "Session-bound pauses are not applied here." while any pause is tied to a session (a try runs as session `try`). The box sends `?project=` with the page's project root whenever it has one, so a project's rules count; its lead says it tries the saved rules, not one still drafted in Add rule (D12). Enter tries the call, clears any rule outline the last try left, and moves focus to the result card, which is also a polite live region; Escape anywhere in the box puts focus back in the field. The head is built once per mount and later renders only refill the body under it, so a render never takes focus from the field. A row added together with others to cover every route carries "part of: <sentence> · N rules", and its Remove opens one confirm that lists every row of the intent: "Remove all N" (`remove_intent`) is the main, red button and "Only this rule" (`remove`, one row as for any row) the lesser one (R5). That second action needed a third, hidden-by-default button in the shared confirm dialog (`altLabel` in `confirmDialog`, `alt` in `writeWithConfirm`); every other confirm is unchanged. A Temporary notice above the groups lists the paused rules and timed grants in force, each with when it ends (the day in front when that is not today) and "End now" (`end_temporary`, confirmed, naming the overrides file). In the Add sheet, "Cover every route" is a checkbox for a path or a domain, on by default and again whenever the kind changes (D8). Its notes say what else it stops and that skill scripts are not covered (D6). The path hint asks for `~/…` or a full path, since a relative path at user scope is refused with every route covered. The preview lists every rule the plan adds, the confirm names every file it writes, and the `warning` a write returns (Grep and Glob not watched yet) shows as a toast. Tests: `TestRulesWiresStatsTryIntents` (static pins of the wiring and the fixed strings), `TestCopyLint`, and the walk's Rules key steps (Tab to the field, type `curl https://example.com`, Enter, the answer holds focus, Escape back to the field, then the existing Add rule and switch steps) with `--axe` on both schemes; the screenshot state `try-open` and the `rules-try` shot exist; Task 7 took the image. When this task ended the fixture had no intent, temporary, user rule or Claude Code Bash rule, so those parts were checked by hand against a scratch fixture home. Task 7 added all four to the fixture, and the walk now checks the intent chip, the Temporary notice and both counts and opens an intent's Remove confirm ("Fixtures and walk" below); it accepts neither that confirm nor End now.

Limits. The try box evaluates the saved rules only: a rule being drafted in the Add sheet is not evaluated until it is written (D16 keeps draft evaluation out of scope). The verdict is the hook's, with every limit `rules explain` lists in section 13: Claude Code's own answer can differ, and the document's `native_rule` names a matching Claude Code Bash rule the hook did not check. Stats count only what the activity log recorded. A call the hook allowed, a hit on a Claude Code rule without a rewrite, and anything older than the window are not counted. A rule absent from a map has not fired in the window. The counts do not say whether a rule is on now: a rule turned off keeps the hits it had.

**The evidence runs (D14, R13).** `scripts/denied-study.sh` gained `--intent-host` and `--leak`; DENIED.md has both addenda, "With the intent rule" and "Leak watch". Four sessions ran on 2026-10-25 UTC under Claude Code 2.1.306, with no reruns and no smoke session. Their raw output was not kept, so the final fix wave ran the same four sessions again later that day (`--keep`, output kept in the private notes); DENIED.md's addenda are written from the re-runs, which reported 0.102 USD together (0.1001 for the three intent sessions and 0.0018 for the leak session).

- `--intent-host` replaces the bare `Bash(curl *)` rule with the host intent for `127.0.0.1`, written by `watchdeck rules add --everywhere` into the study's temp settings and policy file. The pre-run check requires the hook to deny a curl to `127.0.0.1` by the intent's shell rule. In the three re-run sessions, that rule refused every model's curl, and every model then stopped: Opus and Haiku cited the hook's instruction not to retry another way, and Sonnet called the refusal a policy gate it would not route around. No model called WebFetch, so the run does not show Claude Code's `WebFetch(domain:…)` rule refusing a fetch; the follow-up's `webfetch-deny` sessions below do. `sandbox.network.deniedDomains` stays designed, not tested against Claude Code (D7), because Claude Code's own sandbox was off.
- `--leak` runs the scenario `secret-read-allowed` and then reads `/api/needs` from a dashboard started in a temp `HOME`, over the run's stream written out as a transcript, with the activity log off. In the one Haiku session the model read and quoted the token; Needs you listed a leak item for the result and one for the reply, and no part of the token was in the answer.
- The follow-up of 2026-10-25 UTC added the scenario `webfetch-deny`, whose prompt asks for WebFetch by name. Under `--intent-host` the study now registers the hook on WebFetch and WebSearch, as `enforce install` does for a WebFetch rule, and the pre-run check requires the hook to deny a WebFetch of `127.0.0.1` by the intent's WebFetch rule. `--no-web-hook` leaves that registration out, so a fetch meets Claude Code's own rule alone. `--real-transcript`, with `--leak`, keeps session persistence on and copies Claude Code's own transcript of the session by its exact path, never listing a directory. What the five sessions showed is under "Plan 08 verified".
- `--check --intent-host --leak` runs both pre-run checks with no API call; the `--leak` one lists a leak item for a synthetic stream. The classifier's self-test covers the Slack-shaped scrub and the Leak watch line (`python3 -I scripts/denied-study.py --selftest`).

**Limits.** Designed, not tested, unless a test or run is named above.

- The fixture's pause is in force for one hour after `--now`. A home built with an older `--now`, or walked more than 50 minutes after it was built, has no Temporary notice, and the walk's check fails.
- The intent needs a binary. `fixture-home.sh` run by hand without `$WATCHDECK_BIN` or `--installed` builds a home whose Rules page fails the walk's intent check, by design.
- The intent's shell rule holds the fixture home's absolute path as one of its literals (the expanded form, section 14 "Intent rules"), so `rules list` prints a `/private/var/tmp/…` path in that row. It is a temp path with no login name.
- The walk opens the allow and intent-removal confirms but never accepts them. The writes behind them rest on the Go tests and the hand checks in "On Home" and "Rule stats and the try box" above.
- Without `--real-transcript`, the leak check reads the run's stream rewritten as a transcript by the script. With it, it reads Claude Code's own file of one headless session, which leaves that throwaway session in the person's session list.
- The intent study's sample is three curl sessions and four WebFetch sessions, and the leak study's is two sessions, one per transcript source.

### Known limits

Each feature's own limits are listed in its subsection above: "Leak watch", "Intent rules", "Temporary allows", "Rule stats and the try box" and "Fixtures and walk". In short, and with what the close added:

1. **Leak watch is lexical and late** (D3). It finds only the eight patterns, scans each text up to 64 KiB, keeps 50 findings per session, and reaches Needs you within the index's 15 s refresh. `watchdeck inbox` and `watchdeck status` list no leaks. It never blocks anything.
2. **A leak dismiss is as wide as its signature.** A reply has no target, so dismissing a reply leak hides that kind of secret in every later reply; a Bash leak hides it for that program and a WebFetch leak for that host. The Home button's hover text and toast say so, and so does the GUIDE. Resolving one finding also hides later findings with other patterns on the same call.
3. **A leak item stays when its fix already exists.** This is by design since the fix wave (`TestInboxLeakNotHiddenByExistingRule`): the leak already happened. Its Add rule then offers a rule that is already in force.
4. **Skill scripts are not covered by an intent** (D6). There is no sandbox deny path a person can add, and the sandbox's network setting is `none`, `localhost` or `all`.
5. **`sandbox.network.deniedDomains` is designed, not tested against Claude Code** (D7). The study's sandbox was off, and the intent's own Bash rule refuses a curl first.
6. **An intent id carries no scope.** The same value at user and at project scope shares one id, so Remove all takes both. The confirm lists every row. Scope in the id would change the format, so it is left for a later plan.
7. **Claude Code's own rules and auto mode cannot be paused** (D9). Only watchdeck's pack and policy-file rules can, and a skill's grant can be widened for a while.
8. **A session pause ends after 8 h at the latest.** Pausing the same rule again restarts that backstop from the new write. A shorter pause for every session is replaced by "Allow for an hour", and that allow's Undo ends it.
9. **A timed grant always asks,** even for a skill with `auto_allow`. A `GrantFor` refusal other than the fixed id collision still answers 500, not 409.
10. **Rule counts cover the activity log's window only.** They count Claude Code Bash rules only on calls the hook checked before a sandbox rewrite. A paused or log-only hit is counted, and the chip then reads "matched N …, noted only". Counts are fetched once per Rules page view, not after a write.
11. **The try box tries the saved rules only.** It never tries a draft rule, and it ignores session-bound pauses, because a try runs as session `try`.
12. **The fixture's pause lasts one hour after `--now`,** and the fixture's intent needs a binary. The walk opens the allow, intent-removal and End now confirms but never accepts them.
13. **The study is small and headless.** It has three curl and four WebFetch intent sessions and two leak sessions, one of them read from Claude Code's own transcript file. When the hook is registered for WebFetch it answers before Claude Code's rules, so no session shows watchdeck's WebFetch rule and Claude Code's `WebFetch(domain:…)` refusing the same call. The study hook still reads the owner's Claude Code skills directory, as in section 13, limit 12.
14. **Left by the final review's triage:** `TestTryVerdicts`' project case asserts equality with `Explain` only; `tryMode` repeats the CLI's mode list; the first stats load reads up to 16 MiB under its mutex; a Stat/read race can undercount one response; an every-route Undo releases a co-owner's claim (the string stays while another rule claims it); the library accepts an `intent:` id without `everywhere` that no surface passes; a hand-written rule named `intent-…` reads as part of that intent; a temporary written by Allow stays if `Resolve` then fails; and `claude -p` updates `~/.claude.json` during a study run.
15. **`home-leak.png` was retaken alone** with a later `--now` than the other six images (`docs/images/README.md`).

### Not verified yet

Carried from section 13:

- **A signed release** and `gh attestation verify` against a real asset, and the installer's `--verify` on both its paths. All of these need the public repository and a tag.
- **Whether `TestDoctorProveDeniesCanary` ran under bubblewrap**, `doctor --prove` on Linux, and the prove on the owner's real install.
- **The plugin's hook path under shadow.**
- **What Claude Code does with an ask rule in each permission mode.**
- **`--no-session-persistence` writing no transcript.**
- **The study beyond its matrix:** interactive sessions, a run without the hook's instruction, and Linux.
- **Safari, a screen-reader pass, a person's "No" in the transcript, and a hook ask in `dontAsk` and interactive `bypassPermissions`** (unchanged).
- **Windows.** On Linux only the CI jobs have run anything.

The Plan 07 fix wave's own fast-path item is closed: this plan's baseline, the Plan 07 close, includes that fix wave, and its numbers are above.

New with Plan 08:

- **CI on any of Plan 08,** the `secrets` job included. The push has to carry the history with the Task 4 commit rewritten (above), or that job's whole-history scan fails.
- **`sandbox.network.deniedDomains` biting** in Claude Code's own sandbox (D7).
- **Both WebFetch refusals on one call.** Each was seen refusing a fetch on its own on 2026-10-25 UTC ("Plan 08 verified"). With the hook registered for WebFetch it answers first, so Claude Code's rule behind it is not seen.
- **Leak watch on a long, interactive or subagent session's transcript file.** One headless session's file was scanned on 2026-10-25 UTC ("Plan 08 verified"). Summaries and system rows are left unscanned (no test).
- **Linux** for `TestTemporaryGrantWidensRewriteAndRunner` and `TestTemporaryGrantExpiredNarrows`. The CI `test` job will run them on ubuntu-24.04.
