## 15. The Mac-native dashboard (2026-10-26)

On 2026-10-26 the owner picked the "A3" board, the Mac-native family, from the private design canvas after rejecting three rounds that were either card-heavy, dense like a log viewer, or sparse plain text. The pick was applied as styles and markup only: `app.css`, `index.html`, `rules.css`, `home.css`, `sessions.css`, `pages.css`, two label lines in `map.css`, and the switch control's ARIA in `shared.js`, `rules.js` and `settings.js`. No Go, no route, no copy string and no id or class that a script or test names changed.

**Shell.** The `header.top` is a 212 px sidebar in the first column of a two-column body grid, sticky and full height: the brand mark (a teal-to-ink gradient square), then Home, Sessions, Rules and Settings as rows with a tinted rounded-square icon each (`.tab-ico`, colour keyed on `data-page`, blue, green, teal and grey), the page's search slot under them at full width, and at the foot the protection line and the theme toggle. The Settings gear gained its visible word from the existing `nav.settings` copy. Below 760 px the same header wraps back into a bar above the page. The DOM order is unchanged (skip link, brand, the three tabs, the gear, slot, status, toggle), so `scripts/walk/keys.mjs`'s Tab sequence and `route()`'s `.top [data-page]` query hold.

**Tokens.** The system font first (San Francisco on a Mac) at 13 px body. The embedded faces stay in the stacks but behind the system entries: JetBrains Mono is reached only where `ui-monospace`, SF Mono and Menlo are all absent (Linux and Windows Chromium), and Space Grotesk is not reachable at all, since `system-ui` resolves in every browser; both files were kept because `TestScriptsAndStylesStayLocal` and `TestFontsAreWOFF2AndSmall` pinned them, and dropping them was an owner decision, taken on 2026-10-28 (section 16). Light: `--bg` #F5F5F7, `--surface` #FFFFFF, `--side-bg` #ECEBEF, `--ink` #1D1D1F, `--muted` #6E6E73 (5.07:1 on the surface, 4.66:1 on the page), `--line` #E2E2E6; the accent stays #0D7882. Dark: `--bg` #1A1A1D, `--surface` #232326, `--side-bg` #202023, `--muted` #A1A1A6, `--link` #5FD1D8, the accent #0E7C86 at 3.15:1 on the surface with white on it at 4.5:1; both dark blocks say the same (`TestThemeContrastPairs`, `TestThemeTokens`). Cells have a 10 px radius, a 1 px `--line` border and no raise (`--raise: none`); popovers (the Home "Other rule" menu, the Sessions date filter) keep a shadow through `--pop-shadow`; controls are 30 px, small ones 26 px; the switch is 30×18; `.lbl` is a 13 px sentence-case header rather than small caps; table headers are sentence case. The confirm dialog and the Add rule sheet share `.dialog`, now anchored to the top edge with a bottom-only radius and a drop-in animation, which `prefers-reduced-motion` turns off as before. The Flow canvas (`--cv-*`) is untouched; in `map.css` only the decision drawer's two light-surface labels (`.map-card .box .box-title`, `.dvd-kv dt`) changed from `--fs-lbl` small caps to 12 px sentence case, since `--fs-lbl` is now 13 px. The switch is `role="switch"` with `aria-checked` (it was a toggle button with `aria-pressed`); `.sw` styles key on `aria-checked`.

**Rules.** Each row keeps its sentence and its one control (a switch on pack rows, Remove where the backend can remove, nothing on a join row); the facts under the sentence (scope, owner, enforcement, state, counts, file) are one muted 12 px line separated by middots, the state word keeping its colour. The recommended and temporary blocks are ordinary cells (their heading still sits inside the cell, a `rules.js` layout the fix wave left alone). The title line carries Add rule as a plain button and Try a call sits under it at full width (`.rules-tools` is `display: contents`). The middots between the facts carry empty alt text (`content: "·" / ""`) so screen readers skip them. **Home.** The sentence sits in a status cell; in each Needs you item only the first action is a filled button and the rest are text, with the small buttons' hit boxes shrunk so neighbours do not overlap; the rule chip and the count pill read as plain text; Live now is one cell of rows. Recent decisions and the leak row are unchanged, which `scripts/walk/walk.mjs` and `scripts/screenshots.sh` rely on. **Sessions and Settings.** Live now is the same cell of rows as on Home, Open map is a plain button, the filter controls lose their pill radius, and the Settings file cards stack as cells with their state words as text.

**Verified (2026-10-26, darwin arm64, Playwright 1.63.0 Chromium, fixture `--many 60 --live 3 --installed --seed 7`):** `go test ./internal/ui/` green (contrast pairs, theme tokens, the six-stylesheet index, copy lint, the static guards); the 17-row route walk; `scripts/walk.sh --keys` PASS (44 steps; `keys.mjs` now reads the switch's `aria-checked`, falling back to `aria-pressed` for the map nodes) and `--axe` PASS (32 route-scheme runs, both schemes), both rerun after the review fix wave, after two findings from the first axe run were fixed (the 12 px sidebar status line read `--muted` on `--side-bg` at 4.27:1, now `--side-muted` #5C5C61 at 5.4:1; the dark deny tag #F26B6B over its .12 fill on the Mac-grey surface gave 4.45:1, now #F47C7C at 4.9:1); screenshots of Home, the confirm sheet, Rules in light and dark, the leak row, the try box and Settings read by the integrator. `docs/images` retaken with `scripts/screenshots.sh` after the fix wave (2026-10-27 UTC).

**Review (2026-10-26).** A separate Fable reviewer with the diff, the boards and the screenshots returned PASS WITH FIXES and fifteen findings; the fix wave above took the ones that are styles, markup or ARIA. Its plain reading, kept here: the shell and the Rules groups are the board; Home is the board's shell around the old row model (a bold mono tool name, an authority glyph, a Fix line and up to six actions per item), so a page of many items still reads as a column of toolbars. Changing that needs new copy and markup (one sentence per item, the secondary actions folded away) and is the next round, not this one.

**Not verified:** Safari and a screen reader (unchanged from §11); the hook fast path was not re-measured (the change is to embedded static bytes only, no Go); the decision sheet with lifetime and scope popups from the canvas's A2 board, which needs new copy and an inbox form the backend does not expose, so Home keeps its inline actions.

## 16. Decisions on Home (2026-10-28)

On 2026-10-28 the owner asked for the three follow-ups section 15 held over: Home's rows as one sentence per item with the secondary actions folded away, the canvas's A2 decision sheet with its lifetime and scope popups, and the embedded fonts gone. The work was planned in `~/.claude/plans/watchdeck-ui-home-decisions.md` and done on the branch `ui-home-decisions` by an integrator and two workers in worktrees (the inbox form; the fonts), each merged after its own suite.

**Rows.** A Needs you item is `li.home-need` with `data-kind`: one sentence in ink with the item's `what` in mono, one muted 12 px line (who decided and when, the board's "Stopped by watchdeck, 2 minutes ago"; the reason waits in the sheet, since a row that carried it read as dense as the old one), and one filled 26 px button at the right. The sentence comes from `COPY.home.need.sentence(it, proj)` as parts (strings and the `WHAT`, `RULE` and `SKILL` tokens, which `home.js` renders in mono), by kind: "Claude Code's auto check stopped Bash · curl in api.", "Your Claude Code rule Bash(npm publish *) stopped Bash · python3 in proj." (an ask naming a `claude-settings/` rule), "A watchdeck rule caught Bash · curl in proj.", "The sandbox refused Bash · grep in api.", "The skill canary-request asked for more than it has, in api.", "You said no to Bash · curl in proj.", and for a leak the server's own sentence followed by the call ("Slack token appeared in the result of Read · /Users/you/proj/config/notify.env, in proj."; a reply: "Slack token appeared in the reply, in proj."). `proj` is the last part of the item's project path. The button carries the sheet's default choice with an ellipsis, so the row reads like the board's row and the ellipsis says a sheet follows: "Keep stopping it…" (a deny fix), "Block reading it…" (a leak's deny fix), "Ask me next time…" (an ask fix), "Let it through…" (no fix, allowable for a while), "Acknowledge…" (nothing to add or allow); its `aria-label` is "Decide on <what>: <verb>". Nothing else is on the row.

**The sheet.** The button opens `dialog.dialog.need-sheet` inside the page root (one at a time; `data-kind` on the dialog), a modal from the top edge at 560 px: the shield in a 44 px teal square at the left (the board's app icon), the sentence as the title without the project, a muted lead "In <proj>, <ago>. <question>" (the question follows what the item offers: "Keep stopping it, or let it through?", "Ask next time?", "Let it through for a while, or leave it?", "Stop Claude reading that file, or leave it?", or "Nothing to add as a rule: acknowledge it, or hide it for good."), the links "Open the skill to grant what it asked for" (a skill request, to its Rules drill-in) and "Open it in the timeline" (a leak), the reason in a quiet mono box (the item's `why`; a skill request lists what it asked for; a leak has none), then the popups in a label-and-popup grid (`select.pop` in a `.pop-wrap` that draws the chevron, so both colour schemes share it): **For** (Always when the item has a fix; This session and An hour from `allow_for`; shown only with a choice), **Rule** (one option per fix, its sentence, the every-route form as "<sentence>, by every route"; static text for one fix), **Where** (Everywhere, or Only in <proj> when the chosen fix has `project_files`), then the file line (the fix's files, or the project files, or the allow file) and the backup sentence; with For set to a while, the Rule and Where rows give way to the allow lines of Plan 08 (the rules paused or the grant widened, the duration, "Claude Code's own rules still apply"). The actions row has the lesser text buttons "Just acknowledge" and "Don't show this again" (a leak's hover text says how wide it hides) at the left and Cancel beside the one filled button at the right, whose label follows the choice and which carries `data-choice` (`rule`, `allow`, `ack`): "Keep stopping it", "Block reading it", "Ask me next time", "Let it through", or "Acknowledge" (then "Just acknowledge" is hidden). The sheet is the confirmation: it names the file, and its filled button posts `POST /api/needs/<id>` directly (`add_rule` with `fix` and `scope`, a project scope adding `?project=<the item's project>`; `allow` with `for`; `acknowledge`; `dismiss`), a 403 fetching a fresh token and retrying once (`postNeed`). On success the sheet closes first (a modal keeps focus inside it), then the row fades and the Undo toast takes focus as before; an undo sends no `?project=`, since the server reads the project from the resolution it cancels. On failure the server's sentence is a toast and the sheet stays with its buttons back. Focus opens on the first popup, else the filled button; Escape and Cancel close it and focus returns to the row's button (the Needs heading when the row is gone). `confirmDialog` and `writeWithConfirm` are no longer used for Needs you (Turn it on still uses them).

**The inbox form (the backend).** `POST /api/needs/{id}` `add_rule` takes `scope` (`user` or `project`); with `project` the fix is written at project scope into the project `?project=` resolved to, which must be the item's own (the cleaned expansion of its `project`; a known root that is another project is a 400 that names no path, and a bad scope word, a scope on another action or a missing root are refused before the nonce is spent). `GET /api/needs` serves `fixes[].project_files`, the files the fix would write at project scope (empty when Propose refuses that scope, when the item has no project, or when `projectScopeOK` refuses the root: not a git root on this machine, since the write resolves `?project=` to the git root and would refuse a plain directory; the home directory, whose project settings file is the user file, which the write refuses too, before the nonce is spent; or a root `?project=` would not accept, the known roots), so the sheet never offers "Only in this project" where the write would refuse it. An intent id carries no scope (section 14) and neither does a policy row id, so a project-scope add of an everywhere fix records its rows, not the intent, and `Undo` marks its Input so an intent's policy rows are removed from the project policy file only (`removeIntentPolicyRow` otherwise tries the user file first): the undo removes those rows alone, and a user-scope twin of the same value stays in both user files. The add_rule resolution carries `project`, and `rulebook.Undo` removes those rows with that root as the project, so the dashboard without `?project=` and `inbox resolve <id> undo` from any directory find the project settings file. Tests: `TestNeedsAddRuleProjectScope`, `TestNeedsAddRuleProjectScopeOtherProjectRefused`, `TestNeedsFixesCarryProjectFiles` (a command fix in a git root, a pack-rule fix, an item without a project, an item from a plain directory, an item from a git root `?project=` would not accept), `TestNeedsProjectScopeNotForHome`, `TestNeedsEverywhereFixProjectScopeKeepsUserTwin`, `TestUndoUsesResolutionProject`.

**Fonts.** Since 2026-10-28 the dashboard ships no font file: the stacks are `-apple-system, BlinkMacSystemFont, system-ui, 'Segoe UI', sans-serif` and `ui-monospace, SFMono-Regular, Menlo, Consolas, monospace`, `internal/ui/static/fonts/` and its routes are gone (`/fonts/*` is 404), and the CSP has no `font-src` directive. `TestNoFontFilesEmbedded` refuses any font file or fonts directory under `static/`, and `TestScriptsAndStylesStayLocal` refuses any `@font-face` in a shipped stylesheet; the stripped binary lost 99,136 bytes (11,852,786 to 11,753,650 on darwin arm64). The demo terminal (`scripts/demo/terminal.html`) renders in the system monospace too.

**Walk, screenshots and the GIF.** `keys.mjs` gained a `select` step (Playwright's `selectOption`, so the change event fires as from the keyboard). The Home route: Tab to the first row's button, Enter opens the sheet with focus on the For popup, Escape returns focus to the button; Enter again, For set to This session turns the filled button into "Let it through" (`data-choice="allow"`), For set to Always brings the Rule popup back; Tab to "Don't show this again", Enter writes it and the Undo toast holds focus, Escape closes it with focus on the Needs heading; then a leak row's button opens a sheet with its timeline link and Escape returns. The content checks on `#/` look for a leak row (`data-kind="leak"`) and a row each whose button reads "Keep stopping it", "Let it through" and "Ask me next time". The screenshot state `confirm-open` keeps its name and opens the first row's sheet; `scripts/demo/dashboard.mjs` presses the row's button and then the sheet's filled button. The sheet's Where popup cannot be shown on the fixture, whose project paths are not directories on this machine; it was checked by hand on 2026-10-28 against a scratch git root appended to a fixture's activity log: the popup offered "Only in gitproj", the file line switched to `<root>/.claude/settings.json`, the filled button wrote `Bash(wget *)` into that file's `permissions.ask`, and Undo emptied it, with no page error.

**Verified (2026-10-28, darwin arm64, Playwright 1.63.0 Chromium, fixture `--many 60 --live 3 --installed --seed 7`):** `go vet ./... && go test ./...` green (40 packages, with the copy lint, the contrast pairs, the font guards and the four inbox tests); the 17-row route walk; `scripts/walk.sh --keys` PASS (47 steps); `--axe` PASS (32 route-scheme runs); the four Home shots and the Where check read by the integrator; `docs/images` retaken by `scripts/screenshots.sh` (the final set at `--now 2026-10-29T00:25:08Z`, after the fix wave) and the hero GIF by `scripts/demo-gif.sh` (2,616,670 bytes), every image read by eye after the fix wave.

**Deviations from the boards.** These were the integrator's calls, made here for the owner to accept or send back; the owner saw the boards, not these, and accepted all five on 2026-11-04. The A3 Home board's row has two buttons ("Keep stopping" filled, "Allow once" text); the row here has one, carrying the sheet's default with an ellipsis, since the reviewer's point was that six actions per row read as a toolbar and the sheet holds the rest, so "Allow once" has no counterpart on the row (it is the For popup's This session and An hour). The board's sentence is plain prose; the row's carries the call in mono ("Bash · curl"), because the item names a tool and a target, not a story. The A2 sheet's mono box holds the command; the sheet here holds the reason, because an inbox item carries no command (`rulebook.Item` has `what`, `why` and the authority, never the call's text). The A2 sheet's "Applies to" popup mixed the rule and the project in one list; here they are two popups, Rule and Where, because the backend writes them as two choices. The A2 sheet's two buttons ("Allow Once", "Keep Stopping") are one filled button whose label follows the For popup, so the sheet has one default.

**Review (2026-10-28).** A separate Fable reviewer with the diff, the boards and the screenshots returned PASS WITH FIXES and eight findings, all taken: Escape during an in-flight write could drop a successful write (the sheet now ignores Escape while posting and a write that went through always fades the row and offers Undo); a home directory that is itself a git root was offered as a project (refused on both routes); the undo of a project-scope everywhere add removed a user-scope twin (rows, not the intent, are recorded); the muted line carried the reason and read dense (who decided and when only); the deviations list was short three items (above); a root `?project=` would not accept could be offered (the known-roots gate); stale sentences in the image provenance, the walk README, a `walk.mjs` comment and two CHANGELOG lines; and a whitespace-brittle source pin (now three plain pins).

**Not verified:** Safari and a screen reader (unchanged); the hook fast path (static bytes and one inbox route changed; no hook code); the Where popup on the fixture (hand check above); the sheet's state machine has no unit test beyond the walk's steps and the hand check.

## 17. Distribution, part one: the release checklist, its scripts and the install routes (2026-11-04)

Plan 09 is the first public release. This section covers the part that landed before the flip to public; the flip, the tag and the published release are recorded here when they happen. Nothing in the product changed: the plan touched scripts, docs, workflows and one guard test, and no package linked into the hook.

**The repository's rules.** Changes reach `main` by pull request with one approving review from a code owner (`.github/CODEOWNERS`) and every `ci.yml` job green; rebase or squash merge, linear history, no force pushes, no deletions; repository admins may bypass and every bypass is logged (CONTRIBUTING "Development"). `ci.yml` runs one job set per branch (`concurrency`, cancel in progress). The `latency` job skips with a notice, not an error, when the push has no base commit to measure against (a first push, or a single-commit history has no previous commit); a real failure of the measurement still fails the job (`skipped` is a separate step output from the script's exit code).

**The release checklist** (CONTRIBUTING "Releasing") gained the one-time flip step, with the `gh api` call and a read-back for each setting (visibility, fork pull-request approval for every outside collaborator, private vulnerability reporting, code scanning default setup, secret scanning with push protection, the ruleset still active), and three scripted steps after the tag:

- `scripts/verify-release.sh <tag> [--draft] [--from DIR] [--keep DIR]`: downloads the four archives, the four SBOMs, `checksums.txt` and the bundle (a draft only with `--draft`), runs the cosign block from SECURITY.md on `checksums.txt` and the `gh attestation verify` block on every archive, checks every sha256 line by line (a nameless line, an unlisted file, a listed file that is missing, a malformed or duplicate digest each refuse), unpacks every archive and runs `watchdeck version` from the host's own; one line per check, exit 1 on the first failure, everything in one temp dir removed on exit. `--keep DIR` copies the verified `checksums.txt` and bundle into a new or empty directory only after every check passed, so the formula is filled from a file the same run verified.
- `scripts/tap-formula.sh <tag> <checksums.txt> [--out FILE]`: fills `packaging/homebrew/watchdeck.rb.tmpl` with the version and the four digests by archive name; refuses a missing or malformed digest; never downloads; writes `--out` only after every check.
- `scripts/test-release-scripts.sh`: thirteen cases in the style of `scripts/test-install.sh` (a fake release of four archives holding a shell `watchdeck`, stub `gh` and `cosign` first on PATH, a minimal PATH for the no-cosign case so no real tool is reachable, a private TMPDIR asserted empty after every run); the CI `install` job runs it on both OS.

**Install routes.** README and GUIDE name three: the installer script, `brew install Panda-Taps-LLC/tap/watchdeck` (a hand-committed formula in the tap repository, updated per release) and `/plugin install watchdeck --marketplace Panda-Taps-LLC/watchdeck` typed inside Claude Code; `claude plugin validate . --strict` passes with no advice.

**The guard on public text.** `TestNoOutsideNamesInTree` (`cmd/watchdeck`) walks every tracked text file and fails on a short list of names that no public file of this repository may contain; the list is stored hex-encoded in the test so the test does not spell it either, and a planted subtest proves the scanner on a temporary tree.

### Plan 09 verified (2026-11-04, this Mac, darwin arm64, go1.27.2; the ubuntu runner only through CI once pushed)

- The release scripts: `scripts/test-release-scripts.sh` PASS under `sh` and `dash`, 13 cases, about 5 s, with a canary `gh` and `cosign` first on PATH reached 0 times; ten mutations across the two reviews (the sha256 compare, an attestation skipped, the issuer flag dropped, digest validation, an unexpected listed file, the draft check, the check order, the line-loop digest compare, uppercase digests, the EXIT trap) each caught by the suite. `TestCIJobsPinTheirContract` RED without the CI step. Identity strings byte-equal across SECURITY.md, `scripts/install.sh`, `scripts/verify-release.sh` and the release workflow's summary.
- The flip calls: every endpoint, method, field and value checked against the GitHub REST reference on 2026-11-04 by the worker and again by two reviewers; none executed yet (the fork approval endpoint answered 422 on the private repository, which is how its existence is known).
- `TestNoOutsideNamesInTree`: RED on a temporary copy of the tree with one planted line, green on the tree; the planted subtest; uppercase caught; untracked files and symlinks not scanned.
- Gates on the merged tree: `go vet`, the whole suite, `scripts/test-install.sh`, `claude plugin validate plugin --strict`, `claude plugin validate . --strict` (no advice), `claude plugin test plugin` (50 tests).
- Fast path, three runs of `scripts/measure-hook.sh` (200 spawns, BEFORE at the initial commit, machine not idle): no-opinion median 6.23/6.22/6.20 ms after (deltas −0.05/−0.30/−0.19 ms), p95 6.84/7.02/6.93 ms (deltas −0.12/−0.34/−0.64 ms), every run under the 8 ms gate; binary 11,737,170 bytes before and after.

### Not verified yet (2026-11-04)

- `scripts/verify-release.sh` against a real release: the `gh release view` and `gh release download` path (including a draft), real `cosign verify-blob` and `gh attestation verify` outcomes and their stderr wording, and a real `watchdeck version` from a downloaded archive. The first signed draft is the rehearsal (`--draft`).
- `brew install Panda-Taps-LLC/tap/watchdeck` (the tap repository does not exist yet) and the marketplace install line (the repository is private).
- The six flip calls on the public repository.
- `watchdeck doctor --prove` on the owner's real install (the owner's own gate).
- `shellcheck` was not available, so the scripts were syntax-checked with `dash -n` and `bash --posix -n` only. (The release-scripts suite did run green on both CI runners once pushed: run 41958693203, 2026-11-04.)
- Everything section 16 lists as not verified (Safari, a screen reader, a person's "No", dontAsk and interactive bypassPermissions, Windows).

### The flip (2026-11-04)

The repository went public on 2026-11-04 with CONTRIBUTING's step 6 run in order and read back: visibility `public`; fork pull-request approval `all_external_contributors` (the first call right after the visibility change read back the old value; the retry answered 204 and reads back correctly, so the read-back in the checklist earns its place); private vulnerability reporting `true`; code scanning default setup `configured` (its first run, "CodeQL Setup", succeeded); secret scanning and push protection `enabled`; the ruleset on `main` `active`. The tag `v0.1.0` and the release record follow below when they exist.
